### Joint choice of language teaching and timing

Results for the November 24 preregistration. Source 6144 randomized Karen installations, 4922 purchases, 6406.5 units in simulated fees. Training net 11619.5 vs 16000 for skipping (4380.5 source deficit). Across all eight held-out deployment conditions together the fitted policy recovered 4174 units vs skipping, leaving a cumulative deficit of 206.5. No empirical whole-study break-even. Each condition contains 64 paired simulated streams of 32 installations using recorded acoustic/meaning forks; the streams do not represent independent human speakers.

Mean net per installation in persistent/alternating regimes: Karen learner 2.778/2.704 vs skip 2.635/2.585, tuned partition-and-price rule 2.753/2.687, transition forecaster 2.748/2.713. Moira learner 3.257/3.214 vs skip 2.893/2.843, partition rule 3.187/3.121 and forecaster 3.288/3.252. Paired learner-minus-partition means and 95% intervals: Karen .024 [approximately 0,.049] / .017 [-.005,.039]; Moira .070 [.046,.095] / .094 [.071,.116]. None of the four point estimates met the preregistered +.1 margin vs partition rule. On unordered Moira persistent menus the learner selected 232 endpoints, 969 full paths and 847 skips out of 2048. Reversing menu presentation with the same public tie priorities preserved every selection. The learned weights were reconstructed independently by a dense fit on the chosen-action log. Hidden reversal: net learner/forecaster 2.708/2.647 Karen and 3.191/3.154 Moira. Under independent demand the partition heuristic slightly exceeded the learner on both voices.

One held-out candidate phrase also occurred in the rare future workload. Excluding this offer entirely, keeping all exogenous draws and fees for the remaining options, reduced learner-minus-skip to .060/.027 Karen and .259/.264 Moira in persistent/alternating streams. This changes the size and usefulness of the menu as well as removing direct phrase overlap. Research script and archive in the November 24 external dual-role-word directory: joint_ask.py, check_joint_ask.py, offer_hearing.py, joint_no_overlap.py, mac.sh and chosen-action/stream JSON. Role partitions, prior over meanings and a generic linear value-model structure supplied; choice weights learned from actual chosen net outcome plus the known price. No live interaction or measured monetary cost claimed.

### Equally informative questions about different future mistakes

Supply a four-lexicon prior crossing two possible referents of one name (0 or 2) and two reset relations (4 or 6). This balanced prior is a fixture, not learned from the test. Offer two .5-cost full-path lessons, each generating an equally sized 2+2 partition, plus skip. The chooser gets one heard recent practice instruction, no action labels and no preview of future audio. Forecast features compare pairs of hypotheses separated by a possible receipt to pairs predicting different full paths on the practice instruction. Fit action returns from 512 uniformly exploratory source installations on Karen; no access to outcomes of unchosen arms. Compare partition-only fit, skip, equally priced uniform inquiry, and exact public-information planner assuming the recent task recurs. Test on both voices after permuting sound handles and changing all question, practice and job recordings (including separating lesson audio from future audio). Fee .5 and 4 future outputs. Cross name-dependent and cue-dependent practice with matched, reversed or irrelevant demand; same fee, scene and offers across contrasting practice forks. Require >=75% joint appropriate choices and >=.5 net over random inquiry on each matched-demand target voice and report no-payback and reversal separately. Freeze waveform rates 179 source/217 target, seeds 250100–250611 source, 250900–251027 target. Equal partition and relevance are subject to public-hearing and evaluator audits. Scene semantics, prior, features and practice-demand link supplied; no broad natural-language generality claim.

Both offers split the four possible lexicons 2+2 in the public acoustic replay for both voices. Across 128 paired placements per voice the fitted chooser selected the name lesson in the name-dependent context and the reset lesson in the cue-dependent context in every pair, and skipped in all 128 neutral contexts. On matched demand, mean net per four outputs was 2.000 Karen and 3.500 Moira, versus 1.434 and 2.602 for equally priced random inquiry, 1.418 and 2.609 for the partition-only fit, and 1.367 and 2.203 for skipping. The exact recurrence planner matched the learner. Reversing demand without warning reduced the learner to 0.867 and 1.703, exactly the fee below skipping; surprise neutral demand earned 3.5 versus 4 for skipping. Randomized source exploration finished 51 units ahead of skipping on its 512 sampled placements. This is a reward-fitted choice over supplied hypotheses and an explicit alignment feature. The 128 seeds resample four possible worlds and menu tie priorities; they do not represent 128 speakers or distinct vocabularies. Raw audio reads, chosen-action training log and held-out returns in dual-role-word/mistake-audio-*.json and mistake-choice.json, generated by mistake_audio.py and mistake_choice.py.

### Combining corrections to a spoken name and a discourse cue

Freeze the previous target sound bank, four public lexicons and two teaching recordings. Cross the true vs other valid pointer for each purchased lesson, and withhold either or both receipts without refunding the fee. Use six new mixed instructions, canonical (7,0,1), (7,0,2,1), (1,7,0,2), (2,7,0,1), (0,7,1,2), (0,7,2,1), at 201 wpm. Every exact-word test must distinguish all four worlds; audit before interpreting the recordings. Predict both true lessons improve complete-trajectory accuracy by at least 20 points over the better single lesson per voice and recover ≥75% absolute accuracy. Each changed receipt should change the disputed interpretation and leave trajectories on unrelated archived neutral commands intact. Report exact-hearing ceiling and degree of sensitivity to the other valid receipt. Both lessons experimentally fixed; no claim of autonomous second purchase.

The prespecified geometry check failed for (1,7,0,2): when the disputed name denotes object 2, interpolating between two copies of that object erases the distinction between the two relations. Retained all six recordings and separately scored the five that distinguish all four lexicons. With two true receipts / name only / cue only / both withheld, complete-path scores were 16/24, 9/24, 8/24, 5/24 Karen and 24/24, 13/24, 12/24, 7/24 Moira. On the five fully distinguishing recordings the corresponding totals were 12/20, 6/20, 6/20, 3/20 and 20/20, 10/20, 10/20, 5/20. Both voices passed the 20-point incremental criterion; only Moira passed the absolute criterion. Substituting either alternative valid pointer while holding the other true produced zero correct on all 20 fully distinguishing cases per voice. On four unrelated neutral commands under four worlds, all nine receipt interventions preserved 16/16 correct per voice. The exact-word diagnostic reached 24/24 with both receipts in both voices. Acoustic transcripts matched 4/6 Karen and 6/6 Moira. Each waveform is reused in four semantic worlds; six synthetic recordings per voice, experimentally purchased lessons, supplied lexicon family and exact receipt filtering. External combined_audio.py, combined_check.py, combined-reads-*.json and combined-receipts.json reproduce the run.

### Receipt-dependent second language lesson

Fix the first name lesson at .5, and offer the previous cue lesson at .5 after its receipt. Reuse the frozen four-feature chooser and its coefficients. Normalize pair features over the remaining compatible lexicons, predict on one public practice recording, and charge both purchases from the first sound. Use canonical practice (7,0,k) and four distinct future strings formed by prefixing (1), (2), (1,2), (2,1), with k=2,1,0 at 189 wpm on both existing voices. For k=2, predict ask after the name denotes object 0 and stop after it denotes object 2; for k=1 always ask and for repeated k=0 always stop. Cross all four worlds and include skip-second, always-second, public exact planner, exact hearing, and changing only the first paid pointer. Score four complete trajectories per installation. Primary selective arm must switch correctly in both directions and exceed the better fixed continuation by at least .20 net per installation per voice; report both controls and any acoustic failure. This tests conditional continuation with experimentally fixed first purchase.

All four selective worlds on each voice chose the predicted continuation: buy in both unresolved-relation worlds where the anchors differ, skip in both where the anchors coincide. Replacing only the first paid pointer with the other valid pointer reversed all four decisions and dropped net to −.75 for both voices. Always-useful control bought 4/4, redundant control skipped 4/4 on each voice. Selective net over four outputs, including the first fee: fitted / never-second / always-second / public planner = 2.25/1.75/2.00/2.25 Karen and 3.25/2.50/3.00/3.25 Moira. Both cleared the .20 margin against fixed continuations by earning .25 more than always buying. Exact hearing reached 3.25 for each. Karen misheard the first word on two of 15 recordings; one was in the primary selective workload. Moira transcribed all 15 correctly. Small exhaustive four-world acoustic replay, prior and operation-effect features supplied; weights unchanged from single-question source training. No statistical population or autonomous first-purchase claim. Reproduction: second_audio.py karen/moira through mac.sh, second_choice.py then verify_second.py in the external dual-role-word directory.

### Both language lessons optional from the first sound

Replay the opened 189-wpm panel with the previously source-fitted weights frozen and both questions initially optional. Include the existing neutral practice and four archived neutral outputs as a fourth context (217 wpm); report separately. Give no future command or label to any policy. Stop or request a remaining lesson, receive its observed path, filter the public lexicons and score again. Compare the one-step fitted chooser, a public exhaustive two-request planner assuming four recurrences of practice, five fixed strategies (neither, either singleton, both orders unconditionally), and both conditional fixed-first policies with learned continuation. Cross equal fees .5/.5 and .5/.8 in both orders; reverse menu order, using stop first on ties. Primary equal-price selective panel: predict first chooses the name and then asks only where needed regardless of menu order, and gains ≥.20 net over best unconditional routine per voice. Other conditions are diagnostics on already opened audio; no fresh voice claim.

On the primary selective panel the fitted chooser requested the name first, then the cue in two worlds and stopped in two, for both voices, both menu orders and all three price pairs. At equal fees, learned net / best unconditional net = 2.25/2.00 Karen and 3.25/3.00 Moira, meeting the .20 margin. The fixed-name-first conditional policy and exhaustive planner matched this. Selective Moira net with name/cue fees .5/.8 and .8/.5 was 3.10 and 2.95. In controls the chooser bought both when both were necessary, only the name for repeated-anchor commands, and neither for familiar neutral commands. Equal-weighted across four contexts at equal fees: learner 2.9375 Karen / 3.4375 Moira, same as planner; fixed-name-first conditional 2.8125/3.3125. The whole .125 advantage over that control comes from avoiding .5 on the one neutral context. The study establishes variable-depth inquiry under this replay, with neutral audio coming from a different rate; it does not establish conditional inquiry superiority to an adaptive planner or economic superiority on a new distribution. Independently checked every row's charges, all 48 forced-name-first equal-price nonneutral decisions against the earlier fork, and all 48 learned selective traces. Reproduce with optional_lessons.py, outputs optional-lessons.json in the external dual-role-word directory.

### A convention that makes the cue worth asking about first

Freeze the acquisition weights, search, two enrolled voices and public four-lexicon prior. Supply a new candidate cue effect: maintain the prior two anchors, emitting their current interpolation even when a following name is heard. Its alternative is the familiar shift. Bare names shift; the disputed name may mean object 0 or 2. Surface permutation for canonical 0,1,2,3,7 is 1,2,7,0,3. At 197 wpm use first practice (7,0) and jobs extending that practice with (1), (2), (1,2), (2,1). Name offer (0) and cue offer (7,2) cost .5 each. Predict cue first under both presentation orders, followed by a name question only for shift. Require .20 net over the best unconditional routine and fixed-name-first with learned continuation per voice. Include exact hearing, actual heard receipts, public exhaustive planner, and same-rate practice controls (0,1) name only and (1,2) neither. The prospective task is transfer of the ordering policy; hold semantics and the four hypotheses are supplied. This is one remapped miniature vocabulary, not a new acoustic inventory.

The frozen chooser selected cue first in every primary fork for both voices and both menu orders. In the two shift worlds it then asked the name; in the two hold worlds it stopped. Correct complete trajectories 16/16 per voice (four recordings reused across four worlds). Learned net 3.25 versus 3.00 for both the best unconditional routine and the conditional name-first control, 2.50 for cue alone, and 2.00 for no lesson. The public planner and conditional cue-first control tied 3.25. Name-only practice triggered just the name question and gave 3.50 for both voices. Neutral practice triggered no purchase, but Karen misrecognized the first word in that practice and in all four subsequent commands, earning 0/4 per world; Moira and exact hearing earned 4/4. Even buying both semantic lessons yielded zero correct on Karen's neutral workload. Karen transcribed 12/17 recordings and Moira 17/17. This isolates confident acoustic error as a remaining failure distinct from resolving semantic uncertainty. External hold_audio.py, hold_order.py, hold-reads-*.json and hold-order-{0,1}.json; charges and primary traces independently checked against registered choices and all four worlds. Semantic interpretation/chooser evaluated by external replay; the installed acoustic service decoded the locally synthesized audio.

### Grounding a pronunciation correction from one demonstrated movement

On the opened hold-position benchmark, show the two-point trajectory of the neutral practice command once, charging .5 units in a forced-demonstration condition. The listener sees only movement, audio, and the four supplied semantic hypotheses. If its own best hearing predicts the demonstration under any hypothesis, keep the bank. Otherwise choose the best acoustic interpretation among all paths compatible under any hypothesis, with one through four words (two observed moves, at most one cue per move); no word count or boundaries. Add that hypothesized connected recording once to the frozen bank. Compare the original bank and a different valid demonstration for reversed named movements. Primary recovery on the four previously failed longer commands ≥3/4 Karen, with ≤1 lost exact transcript on the other twelve recordings and zero losses for Moira after the true receipt. Score frozen test decisions, transcript changes and repeat at 211 wpm on the four commands; distinguish cost of compulsory feedback from correcting hearing. No autonomous detection prior to feedback claimed.

Karen's initial hearing (7,7) could not produce the observed practice movement. The receipt admitted 2 two-word, 8 three-word and 8 four-word readings. Acoustic ranking selected (2,7), the actual physical forms, without receiving a transcript. Adding that single aligned recording recovered exact hearing and all four subsequent trajectories from 0/4 to 4/4 at both 197 and 211 wpm, with 12/12 other original-rate recordings still exact. Moira already predicted the observed movements and kept the original bank, retaining 4/4 at both rates and 12/12 elsewhere. Each rate repeats the same four longer strings with the corrected prefix, so transfer to a different phonetic context is open. The compulsory .5 receipt cost gave net 3.5 vs 0 for Karen and 3.5 vs 4 for Moira on each neutral deployment. Replacing the true path with the other valid path produced the reversed two-word label on both voices, 0/4 target strings at both rates and 9/12 other recordings exact. The four lexicons and repair rule were supplied; the acoustic alignment and chosen reading were inferred. Confirmed uncued original-rate replay exactly matches the previous acoustic log; unchanged chooser and complete-trajectory evaluator were rerun under each bank. Local registered acoustic service, external ground_hold.py, check_ground_hold.py, ground-hold-{karen,moira}.json.

### Medial transfer of a grounded pronunciation example

Freeze the 197-wpm correction from the previous study. At 207 wpm test eight new minimal pairs per voice: canonical prefix (0), (2), (7,0), or (3,2), followed by disputed word 1 or confusable 2, then suffix (0) or (7,2). The target word is always internal, its right neighbor differs from the practice example, and each pair shares both neighbors. Compare the unchanged recognizer, true positional update, and the diagnostic of sharing only the newly aligned first-word exemplar across positions (store its position as unspecified). Run all arms on identical recordings and the opened 197-wpm panel. Predict useful transfer only if ≥2 additional complete pairs are transcribed correctly on Karen with at most one foil lost and ≤1 earlier unrelated transcript lost; report lack of headroom explicitly. Report full paths using a common known semantic world separately from recognition. No new labels or fitting on the test panel; Moira's compatible receipt should cause no update.

All three banks on both voices transcribed 8/8 complete minimal pairs and executed 16/16 full paths in the known test world. All retained 12/12 unrelated original-rate recordings; original and true arms exactly reproduced every archived original-rate transcript. Zero attainable improvement. The changed-neighbor test therefore does not identify a repair effect in medial position.

Separate localization at 203 wpm: cross initial vs internal disputed word (prepend canonical 2 for internal), following name 2 (original troublesome neighbor) vs 0, target 1 vs foil 2, two common suffixes (1) or (7,2). Hold prefix, following sound and suffix fixed within each pair, report all four cells for baseline, ordinary and shared banks. No adaptation or selection of new lessons. Predict baseline errors concentrated with the original neighbor in initial position; report if false. These 16 recordings share strings with previous rates in some cells and are a diagnostic, not a new vocabulary.

Karen made three first-word substitutions at 203 wpm, one before the original neighbor and two before the changed neighbor. Initial minimal-pair scores rose from 1/4 to 4/4 after the ordinary grounded update; all four foils stayed correct. Both errors before the changed neighbor disappeared, demonstrating first-position transfer beyond the taught two-word prefix. Medial scores were already 4/4 pairs. Thus position matched the predicted concentration, the original following neighbor did not. Sharing the first exemplar across positions produced exactly the same transcripts. Moira reached 8/8 pairs before and after. This panel is small (two paired suffixes per position/neighbor); synthesized speech and earlier learned word inventory. External medial_hold.py, check_medial.py, neighbor_hold.py and check_neighbor.py, with per-arm JSON logs, reproduce both experiments.

### Buying grounded pronunciation help from acoustic disagreement

Freeze an unfitted gate: decode the same practice sound with the full enrolled bank and with its existing isolated examples alone. Buy the .5 movement demonstration only when the two interpretations imply different trajectories for at least one of the four supplied lexicons. No access to actual movements before purchase. Run both synthetic voices, paired practice (1,2) vs foil (2,2), at 181 and 223 wpm, with four subsequent commands formed by appending (1),(2),(1,2),(2,1) at the same rate. Compare full and isolated free readers, unconditional purchase, gate and a hindsight buy/skip reference. Charge the fee even if no change to the bank. Require a profitable buy and a correct skip within each voice with economic headroom; compare per-voice net against best fixed buy/skip by .20 per installation. Report zero headroom, harmful requests and missed profitable requests separately. The gate and four-job workload are supplied and earlier evidence identifies this vocabulary and contrast as relevant.

Fresh-rate results before the historical check: full bank earned 4/4 on all eight deployments, so zero had positive purchase headroom. Karen 223 target was the sole disagreement: the full bank misheard the practice as (7,7), the isolated bank read (2,7), and purchase inferred the true (2,7); yet subsequent full-bank actions were already all correct. Gate mean 3.875 Karen and 4 Moira, full free 4/4 both, forced buy 3.5 each, isolated free 2 and 4. Thus 1 harmful buy, 7 correct skips, 0 missed profitable buys across the fresh panel. Check the locked rule separately on the historical 197-wpm target, where a profitable correction is already known, plus the same-rate foil. Do not pool it with the fresh confirmation.

Historical 197-wpm matched pair: Karen full bank gave the same incorrect/correct (7,7) on target/foil. Isolated bank gave (2,7)/(7,7); the gate bought only for the target and earned 3.75 mean net versus 2 for either fixed free reader and 3.5 for buying always. Moira skipped both and earned 4. The decision rule is supplied, not learned. A stronger post hoc fee-free control selects the isolated bank for the subsequent jobs after practice disagreement, else uses the full bank. It achieved 4 on every historical and fresh deployment, showing the paid receipt was unnecessary across this particular panel if switching readers was allowed. Free here means no modeled teaching fee, not zero acoustic compute. The same pair of decision-time readings in Karen's 197 and 223 target deployments produced opposite economic headroom despite a genuine practice hearing error in both. Raw JSON, ask_hearing.py, historical_ask_hearing.py and corresponding check scripts in the external dual-role-word archive. Verified each recorded net from the four scored paths against independently calculated expected coordinates across the four hypothesized lexicons.

### Source-fitted acoustic reader choice across voices

Before new voice enrollment, fit a table of mean net returns for full, isolated, and buy using the 12 archived Karen/Moira practice deployments (181,197,223 wpm); group only by trajectory disagreement of the readers on practice. Equal deployment weight, ties prefer full then isolated then buy. Training uses all three replayed outcomes per source case and thus counterfactual supervision; report acquisition cost separately. Freeze table and compare to each fixed policy, switch-on-disagreement, buy-on-disagreement, and evaluator-best action per placement. On Tessa and Rishi enroll eight labelled isolated words and the usual six labelled connected phrases per voice, leaving semantics and selection frozen. At 185 and 219 wpm cross practice (1,2),(2,2),(1,1),(2,1), four longer same-prefix jobs. No target feedback before action. Report separate returns and opportunity counts per voice. Require at least one unique paid win per voice before claiming economic evidence for learning to buy, and source-fitted selection ≥.20 over the strongest fixed choice per voice for a transfer gain. Target selection among free readers is separately scored.

Both target full readers were perfect with enrollment. Separate zero-target-acoustic-exposure diagnostic: use the Karen enrollment bank for both voices without changing a threshold or the recordings, and score the same 32 outputs per voice for full and isolated banks. Contrast the contribution of the 14 voice-specific recordings with the established installed vocabulary and listener.

On the 12 source deployments the table learned full on agreement (mean full/isolated/buy 4/2.8/3.5 across 10) and isolated on disagreement (2/4/3.5 across 2), reproducing the earlier switch heuristic. Source fit used 36 replay arms and 12 teacher receipts costing 6 simulated units. All 16 target practice cases produced agreement. Full and isolated readers each correctly executed 32/32 on each target voice; all 32 corresponding recording pairs differ between voices. Target net per deployment: both free readers, fitted and buy-on-disagreement 4, forced buy 3.5, evaluator ceiling 4. Zero uniquely profitable buys, zero scope for a transfer gain over a fixed reader. No evidence here for superior paid inquiry or reader choice. Without any target enrollment, the Karen full bank yielded 0/32 Rishi and 1/32 Tessa, the Karen isolated bank 0/32 on both. Eight labelled target isolated recordings by themselves sufficed for 32/32 on each; no evidence that eight are necessary or minimal. This small task uses combinations of only two spoken names in an existing language and repeated simulated jobs from four practice prefixes; it bounds a claim of efficient acoustic enrollment, not acquisition of new meanings. External reader_transfer.py, reader_no_enrollment.py, reader-source-fit.json, associated JSON and check_reader_transfer.py. No new weights, thresholds or engine changes downstream of the target measurements.

### Targeted enrollment from a grounded name demonstration

Give each policy one spoken two-name practice command and its two movements (.5 teaching fee), a known bare-name construction and three known distinct name-to-object bindings. Recover both intended names by trying all nine ordered pairs against the observed path, without supplying word boundaries. Start from eight isolated Karen examples; when the current hearing misses the demonstrated pair, request the earliest mismatched intended name whose target pronunciation has not yet been acquired, replace that one Karen exemplar, and listen again. At most two target samples, .5 each. Cases where the inferred hearing has wrong length use positional comparison, then the first remaining intended name if necessary. Include zero purchases, all eight target samples, and an unconditional policy buying all distinct demonstrated names. Cross the six ordered unequal pairs of three names at 193 wpm on Rishi and Tessa; later apply each pair to four new jobs built by appending the first name, second name, reversed pair or original pair. Compare full trajectories with both vertices and order grounded. Report average labels/fees, no-label accuracy, attainable two-label accuracy and interference. Target ≥85% accuracy with ≤2 isolated labels (plus the movement demonstration) per voice and ≥.20 net per four jobs over buying all eight. Separate whether adaptive stopping improves over buying the demonstrated names. This is an evaluation of supplied acquisition logic and a familiar construction on new speech, not learning its semantics.

When all later jobs used the demonstrated pair, buying both relevant name samples achieved 24/24 correct on each voice, equal to all eight isolated examples, with mean net 2.5 versus −.5 after the common .5 practice fee. Karen-only reference 0/24 Rishi and 1/24 Tessa. The adaptive policy requested both names every time on Rishi (24/24, net 2.5), but only six samples across six Tessa placements (10/24, net .667). All six Tessa practice commands were heard correctly when the policy stopped; this in-sample check failed to predict performance in longer phrases. On Tessa the only two-label adaptive placement earned 4/4; on the five earlier stops it earned 6/20, versus 20/20 on the very same jobs with both labels. Hence buying the relevant pair is supported, early stopping misses the accuracy threshold on Tessa. The two labels include additional evidence beyond the charged grounded practice. Do not infer they are a minimal number of samples.

Separate coverage diagnostic after freezing: third name repeated three times, preceding the practiced pair, or following it (18 cases per voice). No samples / adaptive / pair / all eight produced 0/0/0/18 correct Rishi and 2/8/11/18 Tessa. No previously correct third-name case was lost versus the initial bank; narrow enrollment left substantial coverage missing. These installations reset per pair and the same-word triple repeats per missing name. Source bank had all eight old pronunciations even when acquiring only one or two new ones. Scripts focused_enroll.py, focused_retain.py, check_focused_enroll.py and per-voice JSON in dual-role-word; acoustic recognition and enrollment through the registered service, learner choices replayed externally. Costs are modeled units.

### A second spoken phrase before stopping pronunciation enrollment

Freeze the earlier name-selection rule and prices. After an early stop with fewer than two isolated names acquired, buy one further grounded recording at .5. For practice A,B the second command is B,B,A, distinct from all four future jobs within that installation. Infer its intended sequence from the three movements by exhaustive comparison of three-name bare constructions. Request a remaining name on a mismatch using the existing earliest-mismatch rule; replay the same check until correct or both samples purchased. No peek at the future commands. Compare an equally priced replay of A,B, original early stop and unconditional both labels. Test on the historical 193-wpm deployments first and separately at fresh 209 wpm on both voices. Require ≥85% final action accuracy per voice, +.20 net vs original early stop where original accuracy <85%, and report whether the method beats simply buying both. Measure diagnostic sensitivity/specificity for whether buying the remaining samples would actually increase future hits at the pre-check state. Report gross gains alongside cost and conditional value of the extra check. Six reset installations per voice per rate.

Results identical at 193 and 209 wpm. All 24 corresponding terminal WAV files differ between rates per voice, but they are the same strings and voices. Tessa: original stop 10/24, same-phrase paid replay 10/24, second phrase 23/24, buying both names directly 24/24. Mean net per four outputs respectively .667, .25, 2.00 and 2.50. Second phrase triggered on all five early stops at each rate; each had a recognition mismatch and benefited from buying remaining name evidence (sensitivity 5/5, no nonbenefiting early stops to estimate specificity). Resulting 11 isolated samples versus 12 for unconditional both, plus five extra demonstrations. Even a free second recording would only raise net to 2.417. The missed job on Tessa was A,B,A,B for pair (0,2); the listener passed the B,B,A check with only the sample for B, then misheard the four-name job as two names at 209 wpm. Rishi reached 24/24 with both names in every placement, never reached an early stop, and paid for zero second checks. Thus a novel phrase identified inadequate coverage on Tessa and the gain met the accuracy and vs-early-stop thresholds, while unconditional purchase remained the stronger economic control. Independently recomputed receipts accounting and terminal coordinates and reproduced both previous 193-wpm controls exactly. External probe_enroll.py, check_probe_enroll.py and probe-enroll-{rishi,tessa}-{193,209}.json; compulsory practice and optional validation feedback are both charged, semantic construction and probe form supplied.

### Enrolling unfamiliar pronunciations from grounded phrases alone

Start each installation with Karen's eight isolated examples, familiar three-name bare construction, bindings and board dynamics. Infer sound handles in order only by matching a demonstrated path against all possible name sequences having one name per observed movement. Supply no target isolated recording, transcript or acoustic boundary to the phrase-only arms. Use either A,B alone (.5 fee) or A,B plus B,B,A (1 total), align the word boundaries through registered connected teaching, compare position-specific versus position-shared exemplars. Primary two-phrase shared arm must execute ≥85% of 24 held tests per voice and exceed Karen-only by ≥20 points. Controls: buy two isolated names, substitute the reversed name assignments in the two grounded lessons at identical fees, and force equal-duration boundaries in shared two-phrase training. All six ordered distinct name pairs; at fresh 201 wpm test four new recordings per placement: A,A,B; B,A,B; B,A,A,B; A,B,B,A,B. All differ from both lessons within the placement. Primary includes the three tests with novel prefixes and one with the taught prefix. Report one-phrase results separately and success/net comparisons to isolated purchase; apply no target tuning.

With one grounded phrase and the aligned excerpts shared across positions, Tessa solved 24/24 and Rishi 12/24. With both phrases, the same procedure scored 24/24 Tessa (including 18/18 new-prefix jobs) and 6/24 Rishi (5/18 new-prefix). Thus the prespecified primary threshold passed only on Tessa. Wrong name assignments yielded 0/24 on both. Position-specific excerpts reached only 9/24 Tessa and 0/24 Rishi from two demonstrations. Holding both spoken lessons and inferred name sequences fixed, forcing equal-duration cuts gave 24/24 both voices (18/18 new-prefix jobs each), versus 0/24 with the original bank and 24/24 with two isolated samples. Two-phrase net .0 Rishi / 3 Tessa, equal-duration diagnostic 3/3 and isolated 2.5/2.5; the single shared phrase gave 1.5/3.5. The discrepancy localizes the Rishi failure to exemplar extraction at source-template-selected boundaries under this recognizer. Equal slicing is a supplied timing assumption tested on synthetic speech at one rate, not measured physical word boundaries. Training recordings are reused across reset placements; a phrase can reappear across installations. Verified transcripts, target trajectory coordinates and within-installation string disjointness using phrase_enroll.py and check_phrase_enroll.py with raw per-voice phrase-enroll JSON in the external dual-role-word archive.

### Cross-phrase consistency under changed timing

Freeze the grounded two-phrase A,B and B,B,A task, both voices and six reset name pairs. Warp only the two lessons' acoustic feature frames by sampling at normalized time u^g, with exponents (1,1), (2,.5) or (.5,2). No word-level edit, boundary or timing metadata supplied to the selector. Test all four longer jobs per pair on fresh 213-wpm recordings without warping. Enumerate cut candidates at eighths plus equal-word cuts, with at least four frames per segment. For each joint segmentation, compare each word in the first phrase to its matching occurrences in the second via mean squared distance after uniform resampling to 24 frames and scaling by the source bank; minimize mean cross-phrase same-name distance, tie by distance to equal cuts. Compare equal-duration, source-bank alignment, and a diagnostic transforming the old equal-duration cuts by the known warp (no physical boundary labels). Fit shared-position acoustic exemplars with each method, separate ordinary and both warped regimes. Require ≥80% complete trajectories and ≥3/24 gain over equal durations per voice per warped regime before claiming robust learned boundary placement. The experiment measures timing perturbations of features and downstream actions, not natural prosody or true boundary error.

Additional attribution control before examining scores: invert which identities are matched only inside the boundary-selection objective, keeping the true labels on the resulting excerpts during final enrollment. This tests whether correspondence contributes beyond choosing non-equal cuts. Report all timing regimes.

Complete trajectories /24 under uniform, (2,.5), (.5,2) respectively. Rishi: joint recurrence 24/15/11, equal 24/1/0, source alignment 8/1/6, warp-aware equal-cut reference 24/18/24, inverted correspondence 14/1/1. Tessa: joint 24/11/5, equal 24/3/3, source alignment 24/19/6, reference 24/19/17, inverted 6/0/2. Under uniform timing recurrence retained all ordinary accuracy. Correct correspondence improved downstream decisions over the inverted control in every cell, even though final exemplar labels were correct in both arms. Under distortion it exceeded equal slicing in all four cells, by only two jobs in the weakest Tessa cell; it missed the 80% target in every distorted cell. Source alignment also surpassed recurrence on both distorted Tessa panels. Warp-aware reference restores the assumed equal split in the original time coordinate, not a true-boundary oracle. Remaining gaps may include landmark-grid resolution, descriptor robustness to within-segment warping, and insufficiency of same-name similarity; not localized among these. Independent coordinate verification checked all final decisions; Rishi transcript and action scores matched, two Tessa distorted conditions contained a trajectory-equivalent alternative transcript. Six reset placements with four jobs each, same test recordings across all timing forks. External rhythm_enroll.py, rhythm_inverted.py, check_rhythm.py and per-voice/warp JSON in dual-role-word.

### Candidate resolution versus scoring in grounded acoustic alignment

On the opened two warped panels, compare the identical recurrence objective on eighth and sixteenth cut grids (include equal cuts in both, same four-frame floor and tie rule). Within each grid, a diagnostic uses the candidate nearest the known warp-transformed equal boundaries in total absolute frame distance, ties by lexicographic cut order. That candidate is a feasibility witness, not a ceiling or input to the listener. Report preselection recurrence rank of the witness and paired differences in action hits vs the chosen same-grid candidate. Positive difference proves at least that much attainable improvement in that placement; worse witness cannot certify absence of headroom. Additionally test a frozen alternative score on the coarse grid: ratio of average same-name cross-phrase squared distance to average different-name cross-phrase squared distance, with 1e-12 denominator floor. Only the denominator distinguishes this policy from recurrence in its evidence; assess separately from diagnosis. Require >=3 additional correct jobs and no regressions across the four cells for a consistent scoring improvement. No use of held-out outputs when ranking candidates. Verify exact reproduction of historical coarse choices.

Separate recording-level confirmation after coarse/fine diagnosis: keep recurrence, both grids and all splits fixed. Change teaching recordings to 187 wpm, terminal recordings to 227 wpm and use unseen distortion exponents (1.8,.6) and (.6,1.8). Same word strings, voices, placements and source bank. Compare coarse versus fine without reference information in selection. Confirm consistent transfer only with ≥3/24 additional correct in each of four cells and report absolute 80% separately. Multiple synthetic rates of the same voice are not independent speakers.

Original warped panels (first timing direction, then reverse) from 252 to 2010 candidate cut combinations with unchanged objective: Rishi 15→21 and 11→20 /24; Tessa 11→21 and 5→16. Independently reproduced all 24 coarse segmentation choices and their saved outputs. Reference-nearest coarse candidate achieved 9/15 Rishi, 4/7 Tessa (scores for the two directions respectively); fine candidate 17/23 and 18/11. In reverse timing, attainable improvements over selected coarse within individual placements total at least 5 jobs Rishi and 3 Tessa (sum of positive witness differences); corresponding first direction no positive witness. Thus candidate density mattered, and recurrence ranking sometimes left usable candidates on the table. Per-placement recurrence ranks and grid feasibility verified in raw log. Contrastive score on coarse grid 10/13 Rishi, 0/5 Tessa; no consistent improvement.

Fresh 187→227-wpm recordings with the alternative warps: coarse→fine Rishi 21→23, 19→22; Tessa 18→20, 12→22. All fine cells ≥80% but only two of four gained ≥3, missing the stricter transfer criterion. Recovered/lost individual outputs were 3/1, 5/2, 2/0, 11/1 respectively. Finer search improved net counts everywhere but cannot be assumed individually monotone; about eightfold candidate enumeration. Same two synthetic voices and word strings, hash-distinct recordings, evaluator-known warp used only to form diagnostic witnesses. External cut_grid.py, verify_cut_grid.py, confirm_grid.py, check_confirm_grid.py and cut-grid/cut-confirm JSON. No target enrollment labels or downstream trajectories entered candidate ranking.

### Choosing a segmentation using a third spoken lesson

On both opened 187→227-wpm distortion panels keep the top fine-grid recurrence candidate and up to three further candidates in rank order, requiring at least one cut to differ by ≥1/8 of its lesson duration from every already retained candidate. No warp reference in proposal or selection. Preview two new sounds (B,A and A,B,A,A at 217 wpm) and have all retained listeners predict their trajectories. Choose the sound with most pairwise disagreements, first on ties; if there is disagreement buy the shown movement and take the earliest-ranked candidate predicting exactly that movement, falling back to the original when none match. Charge .05 per preview and .5 per purchase; base two demonstrations cost 1 for all arms. Compare original recurrence, free preview-majority diagnostic, same-spend withheld and alternative valid paid movement, and evaluator-only best proposed candidate per installation. No target labels or outcomes for proposal, stopping or matching. Require ≥3/24 net newly correct outputs and ≥.20 net reward per four jobs vs original in each voice/direction for robust improvement. Distinguish unproposed, undistinguished and misranked alternatives where a better candidate exists. This is a diagnostic with replayed placements, two synthetic voices and supplied chooser.

All 24 reset deployments purchased the third movement, three changed segmentation. Complete actions /24 per voice and direction (Rishi then Tessa, each 1.8/.6 then .6/1.8): original 23/22/20/22, true confirmation 23/23/20/21, alternative and withheld both 23/22/20/22, preview-majority 23/20/20/16, evaluator-best of four candidates 24/24/21/23. The one added and one lost correct action canceled; 87/96 both before and after. Incremental preview/teaching fees 14.4 over 24 deployments, net −.60 per deployment vs original. Even best possible candidate selection on every placement yields only 92/96, five gross recoverable jobs. After paid selection, four of nine residual job losses are unavailable to a fixed proposed candidate per placement, one is reachable by an equally compatible lower-ranked candidate, four reflect an optimal candidate not surviving the selected true receipt. These comparisons concern total jobs per placement; they do not assert every individual missed job is impossible.

Post hoc menu audit with unchanged tie rule: perfect hindsight choice of which single query to buy could yield 89/96 gross, as could requiring both correct receipts and falling back to original when no survivor. Only two of 24 placements had any one-query action whose predicted benefit measured in hindsight exceeded its .60 fees. Hypothetical clairvoyant buy-or-skip net improvement upper bound for this menu and update rule was .8 total (about .033 per deployment), below the .20 criterion. Disagreement often split weak candidates while supplying little evidence about which competent listener would transfer. No positive economic or causal superiority claim. Pairwise prediction splits and matching, exact baseline cuts and transcripts, and scored path coordinates independently checked. Reproduce via boundary_lesson.py, check_boundary_lesson.py, audit_boundary_menu.py and boundary-lesson JSON in the external dual-role-word archive.

### Constructing a pronunciation request from competing segmentations

Freeze both fine-grid leaders (rank one plus the first candidate differing by at least 1/8 lesson duration in any cut) from the 187-wpm lessons. Test the reversed distortion (.6,1.8) first on both voices. Generate all two- and three-name strings over the demonstrated pair, excluding the two lessons. For each leader synthesize a feature-level prediction for each string by concatenating its own extracted spans, selecting the original position most similar to the requested occurrence (initial, internal, final; ties in lesson order). Decode the imagined practice with both leaders using the installed acoustic scorer. Rank by number of simulated voices in which exactly one listener reads the requested sequence correctly; ties prefer shorter, then construction order. With positive discrimination, pay .5 for one actual 221-wpm rendition of that request; pick the second listener only when it alone reads it correctly, else retain the first. No teacher audio before selection. Compare paying for a random same-menu request (average all alternatives), fixed reverse phrase B,A, never asking, and oracle menu/leader headroom. Evaluate eight fresh five-word command strings, A/B first two names unequal, then ABA/ABB/BAA/BAB; use 231 wpm. Report prediction vs actual discrimination, changes, gross/net return per eight after both initial lessons, and remaining candidate headroom. Require +.25 net over skipping and +.10 over same-spend random per voice before claiming useful construction. The teacher supplies pronunciation of a requested sequence; labels, acoustic handles and two-name generation alphabet are grounded by the earlier demonstration, and feature concatenation is a supplied prediction mechanism. Uniform synth timing is not assumed to reproduce real coarticulation.

Separate use-of-receipt diagnostic frozen before opening primary scores: after paying for the requested pronunciation, retain its audio as new reusable excerpts. Align the requested handles against the selected listener by scoring every feasible segmentation on the existing eighth grid (plus equal cuts), then install these exact cuts via registered connected teaching. All new excerpts shared across positions. Compare selecting alone and assimilating the identical paid audio; include assimilating fixed reverse B,A at matched buy decisions. This isolates whether new target examples can help where the two old listeners cannot. Evaluate on same jobs, no additional fee or teaching. Model fitting uses no job labels.

The constructed request discriminated the two listeners on actual sound in 5/6 Rishi installations (32/60 across the candidate menu), 3/6 Tessa (24/60 menu). All 12 bought a rendition. Selecting only changed the Rishi listener once, in a tie on eventual job accuracy. Full original / selected / evaluator-best-of-two outputs: Rishi 46/46/46 of 48, Tessa 45/45/45. On eight jobs per installation the original net was 6.667/6.50 respectively, chosen/random/fixed each 6.167/6.00 after .5 purchase. No selection gain was attainable even with hindsight: the original listener always equalled or exceeded the other on eventual outputs. Distinguishing candidate interpretations was insufficient.

Retaining excerpts aligned from exactly the same paid utterance recovered both Rishi errors and all three Tessa errors without a lost output, reaching 48/48 per voice (6.5 net on each). Assimilating the fixed reverse utterance reached 48/48 Rishi and 47/48 Tessa. Thus evidence for using the new example exceeds evidence for the invented query policy; the one-output advantage on Tessa is insufficient for a robust selection claim. Retaining the chosen example's break-even fee relative to skipping at this horizon is 2/6=.333 per installation on Rishi and 3/6=.50 on Tessa. The probes are requested known names, and the returned acoustic example is used with that known sequence; physical onsets and final job outcomes are not disclosed to the listener. Fresh five-word recordings in this same synthetic family, not independent speaker cohorts. External constructed_speech.py, check_constructed.py, assimilate_speech.py and audit_assimilated.py and associated JSON. Independent verification reproduced both candidates' archived cuts and recomputed every action outcome from the board dynamics.

### Adding an unseen spoken name between two grounded anchors

Rebuild each previous selected-and-assimilated two-name installation. Introduce the same new spoken form (physical acoustic handle 5 in the recording fixture, absent from all searchable banks throughout this test) to denote the remaining third object. Exclude 4,5,6 source templates and use the earlier legal inventory plus a newly allocated lexical handle 8 after teaching. Known A and B keep their meanings; the bare-name construction and board-to-path dynamics are supplied. Show a single continuous A,C,B lesson at 215 wpm with its path. Recover referents by enumerating three-name paths; assign the newly allocated handle to the demonstrated third object. Infer its extent by selecting eighth-grid cuts minimizing average normalized frame-template distances on known A,B spans, with the unknown span unscored and ties near equal lengths. Store only the new span at shared position. Compare equal cuts, old bank without C, target isolated C at identical .5 fee with its binding supplied, and erasing the learned A,B templates before testing while keeping new C. No existing handle-5 template or target C timing supplied to the anchor method. Four mixed tests C,A,B,A; B,C,A,B; A,B,A,C; C,B,C,A at 225 wpm, plus the archived eight familiar jobs. Require ≥80% correct mixed paths and ≥95% retained old paths per voice; report across six reset mappings, same new sound changes referent across installations. Total costs include the three prior .5 lessons; report incremental outcomes separately. Stop if base replay changes when inaccessible templates are removed.

Separate identifiability check before opening voice scores: the three-object primary allows inferring the remaining referent by elimination. Add a fourth visible object at (128,40) identically in both forks. Keep the same practice waveform, anchors, cuts and extracted new handle; have the middle movement ground the new sound to either of the two remaining objects. Infer each binding by enumerating all 64 known three-name movement sequences on that same four-point board, then execute all archived acoustic readings under each inferred binding. Score both-forks-correct and swapped movement feedback at fixed waveform/cost. This is a semantic replay assuming known bare-name dynamics, not fresh acoustic training.

Full mixed paths /24 Rishi/Tessa: no new exemplar 0/0; known-anchor extraction 20/24; equal cuts 18/24; target isolated exemplar and supplied binding 24/24; retaining the new span but erasing acquired two-name templates 0/5. All three full-bank teaching conditions retained 48/48 old commands per voice; erasing the two-name examples yielded 0/48 per voice. Both prespecified thresholds passed. Exact mixed transcripts under anchor extraction were 20/24 per voice; four Tessa trajectories admitted alternative readings. Across each four mixed jobs with .5 new lesson and 1.5 sunk teaching, mean total net 1.333 Rishi and 2.0 Tessa versus −1.5 skipping the new lesson, assuming all three previous receipts are charged to these four jobs. Third-name acoustic template 5 excluded as a searchable candidate and source waveform never used to align this lesson; source bank's normalization still derives from eight original isolated Karen sounds, including 5. New slot allocation and single-novel-name construction supplied.

Four-object fork: correct in both possible referent worlds on the same recordings for 20/24 Rishi and 24/24 Tessa with anchor extraction. Swapping the two demonstrated paths while keeping test targets fixed yielded zero both-world successes. Every old job correct under both new bindings. This validates grounding using the observed movement even when two referents remain available, conditional on known dynamics and the supplied expectation of one new name. Rebuilt old listener reproduced all archived prelesson decisions. External new_name.py, check_new_name.py, fourth_referent.py, new-name JSON and recordings in dual-role-word; independently checked all old and mixed path scores and number of unique compatible demonstrated sequences on the four-point board.

### Whether to reuse a familiar name or allocate a fourth referent

Keep waveform A,X,B and the same grounded two-name bank/anchor spans in each pair of worlds. A fourth visible object overlaps A or B exactly in the initial board; X denotes either that familiar object or the fourth, equiprobably. Public known phrase anchors, bare-name dynamics, known object IDs, two developed familiar name bindings, four possible middle referents, and a mechanism to store a new lexical handle or another exemplar under an old handle are supplied. Enumerate all four middle bindings against the first observed path. Two paid re-demonstrations of the same recording are available: move A alone or B alone, with fourth fixed. Rank by expected improvement over four applications times separated hypothesis mass (4 times reduction in modal binding error); choose only if strictly above fee (.5 and overpriced 3). Resolve ties without additional evidence by reusing the familiar label. Hidden world never enters the selector. After observing the path, actually register the new excerpt under the existing handle vs handle 8 in the local recognition bank; decode all old and mixed commands, tracking slot count. Same heard form with a familiar meaning might also be a synonym, so this tests operational reuse vs growth, not the linguistic distinction of synonym vs pronunciation variant. Score joint correct outputs across matched worlds and both overlap contexts, not simply correct allocation. Require true query to produce ≥80% joint mixed paths and ≥95% retention per voice, surpass withholding by ≥20 points and net +.25 per four mixed outputs vs committing early. Equal-fee irrelevant layout, reversed valid receipt, and overpriced abstention controls. Prior cases in the last experiment used already distinct coordinates. This follow-up uses archived audio; geometry and ambiguity are new.

The first public trajectory was identical for the two balanced meanings at every placement. Moving the overlapping familiar object separated them; moving the other familiar object never did. The cost-aware public enumeration bought the separating layout at .5 and abstained at 3 in every placement. After the separating receipt, actual acoustic banks reused an existing handle in 12/24 forks per voice and allocated handle 8 in the other 12, exactly matching the operational criterion. Both-world mixed path accuracy was 40/48 Rishi, 48/48 Tessa. Existing jobs correct in both forks 96/96 per voice. Early familiar commitment, withholding the paid path, moving the irrelevant object, and reversing the receipt all had 0/48 joint successes; the early arm still achieved 40/48 or 48/48 in its familiar-meaning branch. Mean reward from four jobs less optional inquiry fee: true vs early 2.833 vs 1.667 Rishi, 3.50 vs 2.00 Tessa; charge an additional common 2.0 units to include the preceding three two-name lessons and ambiguous demonstration, with differences unchanged. Each pair of worlds reuses identical audio and two overlap contexts; 48 counts are paired decisions, not 48 independent recordings. The selector's prior, construction, deterministic transition model, one handle per known referent preference and action costs are supplied. A separate synonymous handle bound to the same referent may also produce correct behavior; these outputs alone cannot identify a speaker's linguistic distinction between synonym and pronunciation variation. Independently recomputed both-world executable actions and equal initial public paths. External reuse_or_grow.py, check_reuse_or_grow.py and reuse-or-grow-{rishi,tessa}.json.

### Grounding a speaker-relative reference in spoken commands

Reuse the saved frozen acoustic readings with new handle 8 on both voices. Expose object coordinates, speaker position and persistent object identities. Hypothesis class: four constant identities, min/max horizontal and vertical coordinate, and nearest/farthest object to speaker (squared distance, uniform hypothesis prior). Meaning of 8 is fitted only from demonstrated paths. Start paired fixed-name and nearest-reference worlds with the exact same recorded A,X,B and movement, speaker near object k and fixed name k, balanced over all four identities. From the surviving hypotheses select up to two further demonstrations by maximum expected number of hypotheses eliminated on a finite menu of generated boards and speaker relocations, stop if all hypotheses agree on every offer. Charge .5 per demonstration. Test on disjoint random boards with speaker near two different objects and unchanged audio/display across those two placements. Freeze seeds 39100–39123 for 24 installations, independent test board seed offset 100000; each installation tests the four old mixed recordings. Require ≥80% correct jointly across both speaker positions AND both word conventions per voice, ≥20-point gain over initial demonstration only, ≥95% retention on old no-X commands. Include wrong-convention demonstration receipts, exact-hearing diagnostic, and worlds with uninformative repeat lessons. Fixed referents preserve object identity across layouts; explicitly disclose the supplied candidate relations and semantic replay. Not a claim that spatial predicates were invented or that speakers used natural words for deixis.

Separate post-fit scope check: retain each saved interpretation, add an unseen fifth object with no name and choose two speaker positions from a dense grid on fresh boards, each ≥15 units from its nearest object. One position must select the fifth object as nearest and the other a familiar object different from the fixed referent. Score both worlds/positions with unchanged acoustic receipts, and no further teaching. This tests transfer over the set of visible objects beyond the teaching cardinality; only active fits are used for the capability outcome.

Active demonstrations: 80/96 Rishi and 96/96 Tessa complete four-way sets; 192/192 no-X sets preserved per voice. Initial only, two repeats or swapped distinguishing pointers gave 0/96 joint successes; these controls can still succeed on individual branches. Exact hearing reached 96/96 per voice. Of 48 semantic forks per voice (same semantic fixtures in both), 46 used one further demonstration, two used two; selected intended interpretation in 48/48, singleton survivor in 47/48, remaining tie resolved by fixed ordering. Mean net per eight outputs after all demonstration fees: active vs first only 5.646 vs 3.604 Rishi, 6.979 vs 4.500 Tessa. Five-object transfer on independent layouts: 80/96 and 96/96 respectively, 192/192 old sets each, with same saved selections. Primary four-object implementation initially iterated over four candidates for spatial extrema; generalized iteration to the actual board length, reran original suite identically and ran the five-object check with the same executable refer/execute functions and independently computed distances/paths. Constant identity candidates remain the four original identities. A new unnamed fifth object was reached only through the spatial selector. No newly captured speech; 24 placements reuse six trained acoustic installations per voice. Claim is active grounding of a supplied relational primitive under counterfactual speaker movement, not induction of the distance primitive itself. External deictic.py, fifth_deictic.py and associated JSON contain reproducible fits, probes and outcomes.

### Inducing the geometry of a spoken reference from raw coordinates

Remove named spatial relations from the learner. Supply numeric 2D coordinates for each object, distinguishable speaker and addressee coordinates, a generic candidate-object ranking interface and all degree-one/two monomials on the six coordinates. Fit the sparsest-in-L1 small-integer ranking weights (each −4..4) that place the demonstrated object strictly above every alternative. No coordinate differences, distances, axis sharing, invariance or known role convention supplied. Derive the winning object solely by matching observed A,X,B trajectory to the known bare-name dynamics. Test four independent conventions on identical teaching scenes: nearest/farthest to speaker/addressee. Compare real-valued minimum-L1 ranking with identical features, a supplied exact-rule menu, and a listener lacking quadratic interactions. Train on nested 8/32/128 demonstrations on four objects for 6 seeds (61200–61205). Frozen test 12 five-object scenes per seed (test seed offset 10000), with speaker and addressee interchanged; require distinct targets between positions separately for near and far. Repeat test under common translation (x+43,y−31) and 90-degree rotation of the scene. Require ≥90% correct referents individually in each convention, ≥80% complete four-convention/two-pose/three-transform sets at 128 lessons, plus ≥75% joint spoken execution per voice including novel object contexts. Separate learning curves, exact algebra recovery up to positive scale, and archive-acoustic vs semantic errors. Compare supervision requirements with the supplied-rule menu. Task evaluator alone uses distance; no held-out selection. Declare any optimizer timeout and score incomplete fits explicitly.

Separate post-primary transfer challenge: keep the frozen monomials, solver and bounds. Teach a speaker-relative anisotropic reference with quadratic cost dx²+2dxdy+2dy² and its mirrored version dx²−2dxdy+2dy²; both fit the existing integer range. Include fresh 6 seeds 61400–61405, 128 demonstrations per rule. Test 24 fresh five-object scenes per seed on which the two rules and Euclidean nearest give three different unique winners. Repeat under translation only; the anisotropic rules are tied to world axes. Require exact symbolic recovery in ≥10/12 fits and ≥90% joint two-rule/two-layout semantic accuracy. Check fit from path receipts, not evaluator labels. No result from this challenge used to select the original feature map.

Primary integer learner at 8/32/128 demonstrations per convention: exact ranking coefficients 1/24, 21/24, 24/24 fits across the six independent training splits. Complete four-convention/two-role-position/three-transform scenes 0/72, 36/72, 72/72; at 128 each convention correct on all 432 transformed pose decisions, including 46/72 scenes where at least one reference selected the new fifth object. Spoken complete sets 240/288 Rishi and 288/288 Tessa at 128, matching the supplied exact menu already after eight demos. Real-valued minimum-L1 ranking achieved only 2/72 complete scenes at 128 despite per-rule scores 364,370,363,363 /432; training-scale finite-margin solutions need not translate or rotate correctly. The hard-margin linear-only control was infeasible in every fit; it has no trained prediction, counted as no complete successes, not a measure of best approximate linear accuracy. All integer and real quadratic optimizations finished optimally; no timeouts. Original speaker and addressee are explicitly tagged coordinate inputs. The spatial score learned for near is equivalent for ranking to −x²−y²+2x·rx+2y·ry; the omitted reference-only term is equal for every candidate. Verified the recovered vectors independently on 97 fresh real-valued points per rule. For a seed with an early nonoptimal-rule fit a NumPy boolean could not initially be serialized; fixed result serialization and reran that seed without changing the experiment.

Tilted-distance challenge: exactly 12/12 coefficient vectors recovered, 144/144 two-rule/two-layout sets on disagreement scenes, all fits solved optimally. This demonstrates induction beyond choosing among the four named teacher rules, conditional on the supplied quadratic basis, bounded integer coefficients, known movement dynamics and many demonstrations. It is not evidence for spontaneous discovery of coordinates, multiplication or role identities. The challenge is a semantic replay; no additional acoustic claim from it. External induce_geometry.py, verify_induced_geometry.py, induced-geometry JSON, tilted_geometry.py and tilted-geometry.json. The 72 semantic scenes and their pose/transform forks are paired tests, and the acoustic readings reuse the previous synthetic voice banks.

### Reusing induced spatial meanings in a new combination

Use per-seed archived 128-example coefficient fits for four speaker/addressee near/far rules and the two tilted speaker rules (768 source demonstrations for one library). Deduplicate signed-equivalent columns. Permit a target scorer to combine stored rankings and raw monomials; fit by minimum sum of absolute integer coefficient magnitudes. Bound each coefficient and each resulting raw coefficient to ±4 in all fit arms. Controls: erase library, permute learned coefficients among object-dependent monomial dimensions with fixed seed preserving library size/sparsity, choose only a single stored rule. Two target interpretations absent as complete source scores: minimize tilted-plus speaker distance plus squared addressee distance, or tilted-minus speaker distance minus squared addressee distance. Fresh seeds 61800–61805; nested 2,4,8,16,32,64,128 ordered movement demonstrations per convention. Same 3-second solver cap per fit; disclose feasible time-limited and missing solutions separately. Test independently at each checkpoint on 48 fresh five-object layouts plus translated copies (48 paired both-correct units); tests never alter training. Mastery = ≥90% at two successive probe blocks; report number of demos at confirming checkpoint, unreached censored beyond 128, and exact recovered coefficients. Require ≥25% saving vs erased on each target among paired complete cases AND at least equal mastery count. Report retained vs miswired/per-rule controls and total source cost; one-shot few-example success must not be called general language acquisition. This stage isolates semantic transfer with known dynamics and no target acoustic fitting.

Post-primary specificity audit, unchanged solver and library: fresh seeds 62100–62105, target 0 minimizes only horizontal separation from the listener; target 1 minimizes horizontal separation from the speaker plus vertical separation from the listener (both squared). Both target formulas are representable by the raw basis; independently check that neither is in the linear span of saved scores. Use the same checkpoint, mastery and fresh-test protocols. This checks positive or negative transfer outside exact library combinations, not another primary comparison.

Primary confirmation checkpoints retained for target 0: 8,16,8,16,8,8; erased 128,>128,128,128,64,>128. Paired complete median saving 90.625% (4 pairs), mastery 6/6 vs 4/6. Target 1 retained 16,16,32,4,16,16; erased 128,64,128,64,128,128. Median paired saving 87.5% (6 pairs), mastery 6/6 both. Retained exact full formula in all six seeds by 8 demos on target 0 and 16 on target 1. The erased learner got both formulas exactly by 128 in every seed; the two censored deployments needed a second successive successful checkpoint. Miswired library confirmed 5/6 and 6/6 respectively, typically at 128; single-best stored formula never confirmed. One of 84 primary miswired solver runs hit its three-second limit; all retained/erased primary fits finished optimally. Complete pairs saved 408 and 540 target demonstrations across 4 and 6 matched training splits respectively, versus the 768 source demonstrations per independently trained library. These two target meanings alone did not amortize that cost; no observed total break-even. Tests are held-out semantic layouts, not independently taught human languages. Generic feature composition and small-integer simplicity bias supplied; source and target relations deliberately overlap. No acoustic learner was changed.

Outside-span controls verified by nonzero projection residual: median paired teaching saving 0% for both. Axis-only target retained/erased medians both 16; one seed required 32 vs 16 with retention. Cross-role axis target matched on five seeds and saved 32 demonstrations on one. All reached mastery by 64. Positive primary result concerns reusable structure in specific learned relations, not a universal speedup from keeping more weights. Raw and reproducible scripts reuse_geometry.py, outside_geometry.py, coverage_geometry.py, check_reuse_geometry.py and per-seed JSON under dual-role-word. Exact recovered formulas independently verified against held-out paired successes.

### Spoken connectors on unheard combinations of learned relations

Supply a three-part construction (left relation, connector, right relation) and stable form-to-atom mappings. Use the six archived source libraries; left acoustic handles 0/1 denote the two tilted speaker scores, right 2/7 denote nearest speaker/addressee; connector forms 3/4 have opposing additive/subtractive conventions, reversed in a matched speaker fork. Eight enrolled isolated pronunciations per synthetic voice, no connected pronunciation teaching for this test. Decode whole recordings through registered connected scoring; primary search uses the supplied slot grammar, secondary open 1–6 word search. Teach only diagonal content pairings (0,2),(1,7), each connector on 12 distinct four-object boards per term at 197 wpm, by observed pointer. Fit a primitive integer coefficient pair in [−2,2]² excluding the zero vector and positive duplicates, via most correct predictions on the heard terms, then smallest L1 and fixed lexicographic tie rule. Supply neither the connector meanings nor labels/boundaries on the continuous commands. Hold off-diagonal pairings (0,7),(1,2) out of teaching and score on five-object scenes at 223 wpm. For each of six seed libraries and each held pairing use 24 boards selected solely for unique and different additive/subtractive targets; require joint correctness over swapping only connector sound and only teaching convention, 80% per voice, 20-point gain over swapped teaching pointers. Report exact hearing, per-string accuracy and evaluator-only coverage of copying the two taught whole-phrase meanings with the same connector. Disclose separately whether scorers, their relative scale and the combination family are supplied. This tests application of a learned spoken connector in a known construction, not grammar induction.

Post-fit scale audit: positive rescaling of an individual score preserves its standalone meaning but can change the combination. Replay unchanged cases doubling both right-atom scores, with teacher meanings fixed at their original physical geometry. Compare freezing old connector weights with refitting the same small coefficient family on the same 48 pointed demonstrations. Exact compensating coefficient ratios are expressible (2,±1); report finite-data accuracy even if the ratio is not identifiable on a particular source split.

Both voices passed 288/288 matched held-board sets (6 source libraries × 2 withheld pairings × 24 contrast boards). Each set requires both connector sounds under both teaching conventions. Replacing teaching receipts with those of the opposite convention gave 0/288. Even an evaluator choosing between the two previously taught whole-phrase rules for the corresponding connector on each individual branch could cover only 129/288 complete sets. All six fits recovered the two correct signed ratios in each convention. The isolated-only acoustic bank transcribed 8/8 distinct whole recordings on each voice under both open and slot-constrained acoustic search (4 taught and 4 withheld strings per voice). Many boards reuse the same four withheld recordings; not 288 independent utterances. Scaling both right atoms by 2 with frozen old connector coefficients reduced joint success to 221/288; after refitting from the teaching receipts, recovered (2,+1)/(2,−1) ratios in every seed and restored 288/288. Core facts supplied include the atom lexicon with learned numeric scores, three-slot interpretation, bounded signed linear-combination hypothesis family, and familiar object dynamics. No claim of spontaneous word order or arithmetic discovery. External connector_audio.py, connector_ground.py, connector_scale.py and corresponding raw JSON in dual-role-word. Independently checked joint labels, reversed-convention pairing and comparator coverage from saved individual choices.

### Discovering the connector position when all sounds can play both roles

Four anonymous sounds (physical 0,1,2,7) each have a supplied previously learned standalone scoring vector and an initially unknown connector weight pair. Every sound occurs equally in every surface position, both in teaching and withheld utterances. In three matched conventions any one of the three positions is the connector; the other two operands are read in surface order. True per-form weights (1,1),(1,−1),(2,1),(1,−2) shared across positions in the three teacher forks. Learner searches all three positions and independently fits each form's coefficient pair from the earlier generic coefficient space by maximizing correct demonstrated object choices. Open one-to-six-word acoustic recognition; no positional candidate masks. Of 24 ordered distinct-token triples, withhold all four cyclic relabelings of (0,1,2) and (0,2,1) using abstract IDs 0..3. Teach remaining 16 sounds at 191 wpm, 6 public four-object movements per sound per convention, 6 seeds 66300–66305. All three teaching histories use identical sounds/boards and differ only in movement outcomes. Test at 229 wpm on 16 unfiltered five-object test boards per unheard string and seed, unique maxima required for all three teachers, no requirement their choices differ. Report joint three-world success, contrast-board denominator/results where at least two teachers disagree, correct inferred position and exact-hearing control. Require ≥80% joint correct on contrasts per voice and +20 points over forced-middle on that subset. Compare swapped teaching histories and a diagnostic with form weights given. This is induction among position hypotheses in a supplied three-part construction; no claim of learning the number of operators or free syntax.

Post-primary diagnosis: for the learned position, retain every equally best coefficient pair per form under the 96 initial pointers. Generate eight candidate four-object boards for each taught string using independent seeds 86300+ix, without conditioning on a hidden target. Select at most one new query per convention by maximum fraction of disagreeing ordered pairs among the remaining coefficients for its connector form. Break ties in fixed construction order; do not ask if no public disagreement. Update only that form using one actual pointer, keep the initially chosen position. Record tie incidence, questions and original errors attributable to the ambiguity. Replay on an independent 16-board-per-withheld-string set (seeds 96300+ix); do not report this as independently discovered syntax.

Both voices recognized 24/24 whole recordings exactly under the open acoustic search. All 18 fitted conventions (6 seed libraries × 3 positions, shared fixtures across the two voices) recovered the intended position with 96/96 correct training pointers each. Joint over all three teacher conventions on 768 unseen-string boards: 761/768, including 716/723 on boards where the conventions differed. Fixed-middle refit 127/768 overall, 105/723 contrasts. Swapped teaching histories 45/768 overall, 0/723 contrasts. Supplying true weights and fitting only position gave 768/768. Exact hearing reproduced the primary scores. Eight held recordings per voice are reused across boards; separate scores do not represent independent semantic cohorts. Verified disjoint strings and equal marginals for every form at every position.

Version-space diagnosis found 3 unresolved form weights among 72 fitted position/form combinations. At most one selected extra demonstration in each of these three convention fits, using only a taught string; only one tie-break changed the selected coefficient. Joint success improved 761→768/768 on the original boards and 761→768/768 on fresh boards, with the spoken recordings unchanged. This isolates finite evidence about a connector's meaning after the surface position was already identified. Each of the four forms could serve as operator or operand across utterances. The learner still assumes exactly one connector, a fixed position within each convention, and surface order for its two arguments. ### Inferring whether a three-word utterance contains a connector

Same four grounded content scores and all distinct-token triples. Across matched conventions switch which two forms are licensed (even vs odd abstract handles), cross with first/middle/last position. Separate transfer check on all 36 triples with exactly two distinct sounds at 213 wpm, never heard in teaching or earlier distinct-string testing. No additional target teaching.

Both voices decoded 24/24 distinct strings using open acoustic search. In each of 36 semantic fits (6 source libraries × 3 positions × 2 licensing conventions), the learner inferred the correct position and classified all held strings correctly. Both convention outcomes on decisive boards: 426/426 first, 426/426 middle, 448/448 last per voice. Always-operator refit: 280/426, 269/426, 263/448; always-ordinary and swapped teaching both zero on all decisive boards. Overall joint per position 768/768 each. On 2,249 disjoint-target paired placements across positions, Rishi 2,173 and Tessa 2,249 complete. When the connector form also appeared as an operand, Rishi 1,637/1,661, Tessa 1,661/1,661. Exact-word inputs yielded all correct for both. All five objects tied in 432/5,184 operator evaluations per voice; allowed any maximizer and excluded overlapping target sets from the decisive denominator. ### Transferring the scope of a heard correction

Ask for the movement on the first old/free disagreement, skipping if none. Accept later edits using the identical difference rule only after actual prelesson reading error on the selected command. Compare withheld receipt, unrestricted and prefix-only control. Primary diagnostic on all eight archived 241-wpm deployments; confirm zero-ending lesson on two voices at 251 wpm with same strings and compare 64 guards, eight pairs and frozen mixed-command meanings. This tests an engineered disagreement strategy and correction alignment; no optimality or autonomous comprehension claim. First 241-wpm analysis already opened; report 251 separately.
