### Learning the direction of a spoken conditional

Use continuous seven-form commands (known three-form quantified clause, one anonymous connector, another clause). In two miniature conventions the connector sounds 4 and 5 exchange material implication and its converse. Listener gets all sixteen possible binary Boolean functions per sound, as a four-cell truth table with equal prior; no implication direction, logical duality or relation between the two sounds supplied. One training clause pair on 3×3 boards gives four truth judgments per connector, empty worlds excluded. Reserve four other unordered clause pairs and both spoken orders on 4×4 boards. Feasibility before audio: in seeded shuffled order of clause pairs choose the first five with all four joint truth values at one edge density on both board sizes; reserve the first for teaching. Seed 134801, 16 held equal-density quartets per test pair without within-quartet duplication. Require ≥85% complete mixed sets across both orders, connectors, worlds (01/10) and conventions per voice; report equal-truth controls. Compare open duration search with known-frame and exact tokens, withholding the teaching for one truth cell (report probability rather than inventing certainty), and exchanging only the two contrasting teacher judgments. The fixed clause construction and previous quantified-clause meanings are supplied; everyday English, counterfactuals and causation are outside this test.

Pre-audio feasibility amendment: exhaustive testing found zero eligible unordered clause pairs at fixed density on 3×3, 22 on 3×4 and 46 on 4×4. Use 3×4 teaching graphs. All other fixtures, held board size, endpoints and recording rates remain as specified. This was discovered before any target audio decoding.

Results: Rishi decoded 18/18 recordings and passed 64/64 complete mixed and equal-truth sets with four judgments per connector (1024/1024 individual mixed decisions). On Tessa, 15/18 recordings decoded exactly; three held reversed-order sentences with marker 4 lost words. Complete mixed/equal sets 16/64 each, individual mixed 832/1024; primary acoustic criterion failed. Fixed-frame hearing and exact tokens gave 64/64 mixed and equal sets per voice. Swapping only the 01 and 10 teaching judgments gave 0/1024 correct mixed decisions under exact hearing, preserving 64/64 equal-truth sets. Withholding the 01 example left each corresponding posterior at .5, and the remaining mixed cell was correct on all 512 branches under exact hearing. Symmetric pooling cannot resolve direction. The archived square-board quantity replay hard-coded four in both reductions; its pre-scoring equality guard caught this on the rectangular teaching boards. Applied the original frozen quantity coefficients with actual public row/column counts, exhaustively compared the resulting truth values to reference on both sizes, and independently reconstructed every held label by bit indexing. Only four held clause pairings, sixteen distinct held utterances per voice, matched semantic scenes reused. This is grounded truth-functional direction given a fixed construction, a supplied two-input function family and prior quantifier learning, not natural pragmatic understanding of conditionals. External conditionals.py, conditional_audio.py, check_conditionals.py, per-voice audio and judgment traces in dual-role-word. Registered local acoustic calls, external semantic replay; no deployment change.

### Deducing and withholding conclusions after two spoken conditionals

Freeze the preceding grounded connector tables. Give two successive truthful conditional utterances, a signed fact about one endpoint, and a query about the other. No such triple supported all eight patterns at equal graph density on 4×4, so edge counts may vary. Identical displays, audio and factual statements across convention forks. New conditional recordings 217 wpm, atomic fact/query recordings 207; audible binary polarity via previously enrolled yes/no forms. Rishi unrestricted hearing 16/16 distinct recordings, 36/36 complete story sets (three clause triples, 12 graph displays each), comprising 288/288 determinate, 864/864 genuinely unresolved and 288/288 inconsistent histories. Tessa unrestricted 14/16 exact, with two marker-4 conditional utterances shortened: 12/36 complete and 192/288, 576/864, 192/288 respective individual outcomes. With supplied acoustic frames or exact words both voices reached 36/36. Reversing only the first connective interpretation also lost every determinate and conflict decision. Independently verified every reference verdict via a directed-chain criterion, including contrapositive and mixed-direction cases. These are shuffled displays using the same small set of recordings, not independent conversations. The agent is supplied all candidate worlds, state intersection, statement boundaries and the interface for a signed fact. ### Grounding a spoken correction that reopens possible worlds

Supply a two-premise working record and four generic possibilities for how much prior evidence a new utterance retains: any subset of the first and second records. No mapping from a correction sound to a retention pattern. On the first clause triple from the chain study, teach using two positive atomic premises and a marked third positive clause; the teacher judges two displayed candidate graphs, one violating only the first old premise and one violating only the second, both satisfying the new fact. Infer the compatible retention mask for each heard marker. Two counterfactual speakers swap the meanings of two prefix sounds; control deployments teach keep-both and keep-neither. Use the other two triples as held compositions: now the old records are grounded conditionals, followed by a marked factual clause. Vary which endpoint or middle clause is corrected, antecedent direction and premise order. Score the exact surviving graph set, newly admitted worlds and exclusion of worlds violating still-retained statements. Also query an independent endpoint. Compare monotone intersection, discarding the entire history, and swapped versus withheld membership answers. Require ≥85% correct on paired speaker forks where the two meanings demand different sets, and ≥20-point gain over swapped answers per voice; report syntax-assisted and true-token diagnostics. Acoustic clauses reused for prior conditionals; new marked facts 221 wpm and unmarked atomics 211 wpm; 12 independent displays per held triple on seed 136801. The memory representation, truthful judgment interface, and four permissible masks are provided; transfer from factual demonstrations to conditional premises is the new test.

Both voices heard all 21 new atomic/marked utterances exactly. Rishi inferred the correct revision and surviving possibilities on 1056/1056 qualifying paired speaker forks (24 displays, 48 arrangements each, 96 pairs without a difference excluded). Reopened precisely the warranted worlds on 1344/1344 applicable branches; still excluded violations of retained evidence on 1536/1536. Tessa open 768/1056 pairs (72.7%, below criterion), 960/1344 reopening and 1152/1536 exclusion. The 576 unresolved individual branches followed a malformed earlier conditional premise, despite correct hearing of the revision. Fixed-frame and exact-symbol inputs reached 1056/1056 paired cases on both voices. Exchanging only the two membership replies gave zero exact paired replacements; 864/1056 endpoint-query pairs still happened to be correct with exact hearing, showing why the whole belief state matters. Withheld replies, always append and always clear each gave zero exact paired replacements; keep-all/clear-all positive control learned on 1056/1056 exact-hearing pairs. Independently reconstructed paired-decision and reopening denominators from all eight Boolean assignments and both opposite speaker conventions. Effects are conditional on the supplied retain-mask family, previous clause groundings and perfect access to the two old records. Grouping across 24 displays and only two held clause triples precludes interpreting 1056 as independent conversations. ### Which prior utterance needs a second hearing after a correction?

Audit the opened correction replay: it marked a whole response unresolved if either earlier hearing was malformed, before consulting the inferred retention mask. Freeze four policies: previous all-premises-valid guard, evaluate just retained premises, request one repeat for every malformed premise, or request one only for a malformed retained premise. A request returns a fresh open acoustic reading of the same spoken statement, no transcript or true word count; syntactically valid but wrong readings may persist. Charge .15 per request per branch, unit reward for the exact revised set, no reward for abstention. Also isolate the diagnostic of reprocessing stored original audio with the known frame, with no claim that such computation is free. Reuse the calibrated correction sounds and exact original scene draws; score all individual branches and qualifying paired forks separately. Before generating repeat audio: predict the guard correction alone resolves all branches whose sole malformed premise was discarded; selective vs eager should reduce repeat calls by ≥40% with identical individual correctness. Repeat at 203 wpm for the opened 217 panel and add an independent 237-wpm first hearing of the same utterance strings with the same 203 repeat; these are new recordings of familiar constructions. Report whether repeats actually recover meaning and distinguish an invalid first hearing from a genuinely ambiguous revision.

Follow-up frozen after the open repeat failed: when a retained malformed premise matters, ask the speaker to present first clause, connector, and second clause in separate consecutive turns. Use 209 wpm, open recognition per segment (1–9 forms), no within-segment slots or known word counts. Concatenate actual heard outputs; use identical learned connector semantics. Charge three .15 units per requested premise; compare wrong connector receipt at the same price. Predict ≥80% recovery of still unresolved branches on each impaired first-hearing panel; score overhead and no-headroom voice. The speaker supplies the two boundaries, making this a different operation from independent discovery of segmentation.

Tessa, per 2,304 individual replacement branches at either initial rate: strict replay 1,728 correct; evaluating retained records alone 2,016, resolving all 288 branches where only a discarded record was malformed. Qualifying paired success stayed 768/1056 because the opposite fork retained the problematic premise. Eager versus selective ordinary repeat 576 vs 288 calls, both still 2,016 correct: 203-wpm open decoding repeated the errors on the relevant sentences. Net/output .83750/.85625 vs .875 with no calls. Frame-constrained reprocessing of stored audio gave 2304/2304 after 288 reanalyses (computed separately from repeat service). Three-part restatement decoded all nine distinct segment recordings correctly on both voices. Tessa selective restatement restored every remaining branch, 2304/2304 and 1056/1056 complete pairs at either initial rate. At three fees per requested premise, simulated net .94375, vs .875 without clarification. Replacing just the connector receipt by the opposite valid sound reduced this to 2016/2304 and .81875 net at identical cost. Rishi stayed at 2304/2304 without requests. Keep-all/clear-all controls produced identical aggregate counts. Most branches reuse a single problematic sentence, so this quantifies mechanism and fees on a matched replay rather than an independent 288-command improvement. Fixed selective trigger, speaker-supplied clause boundaries, synthetic audio and external semantic replay. Source/checks in recall_premise_audio.py, recall_premise.py, check_recall_premise.py, repair_segment_audio.py, repair_segment.py and check_repair_segment.py in dual-role-word. No deployed change.

### Choosing a form of spoken clarification from verified outcomes

Source: 12 archived two-clause recordings per voice, initial 217 wpm, whole repeat 203 and three-part reply 209. Add an initial transmission ending at 60% of its acoustic frames; decode that partial signal with unrestricted length search, with no corruption flag for the chooser. At each source recording/channel, assign stop, whole repeat and three-part request in shuffled order across three separate replay deployments, observing just the executed reading's semantic success (equivalence of the judged conditional over the full 4×4 graph universe). Learn a success rate by action, voice and observed hearing class (valid conditional, malformed under five forms, other malformed). Use Beta(1,1) smoothing, falling back to pooled voices for an empty class/action. Compare predicted net under .10 and .40 fees per transmitted turn (0/1/3 turns). Freeze the table before hearing 16 new strings per voice: eight previously unused clause pairings, both connector forms, seed 137801. First recording 229 wpm, whole repeat 197, segmented reply 211, same clear/partial conditions. Source and target phrase construction and teacher-verified scalar objective supplied. Compare fixed actions, best fixed per price, a malformed→segmented rule (or stop if three fees exceed reward), and evaluator-only best available action. Require positive pooled net vs strongest fixed per fee on each voice, report clear/partial and prices separately, all calls, accuracy and source expense. A selector that wins through abstention alone does not demonstrate learning when to prefer a segmented reply.

Each voice used 72 source deployments across 12 strings and two channel states; all 3 actions were exercised on each, 96 extra transmitted turns per voice (9.6 or 38.4 simulated units by fee), correct 60 Rishi/54 Tessa. Frozen held results per 32 deployments: at .10, Rishi chose stop/whole/parts 14/18/0, 31 correct, net .9125; Tessa 14/16/2, 32 correct, .93125. At .40, Rishi 23/9/0, 23 correct, .60625; Tessa 16/16/0, 30 correct, .7375. The strongest fixed policy was always repeat at both prices. Paired learner gain over it at low/high price: Rishi +.075/+.06875, eight-pair bootstrap intervals [.0375,.140625]/[−.05,.225]; Tessa +.03125/+.1375, intervals [−.00625,.05]/[.0125,.20]. On clear/clipped inputs at .10 the learner earned .9875/.8375 Rishi and .9625/.9000 Tessa; at .40, .875/.3375 and .875/.6000. A stronger simple control (repeat every malformed hearing) matched the learner on Rishi at .10 and beat it in all other voice/fee cells. Tessa's two cheap segmented requests both succeeded but an ordinary repeat would have succeeded for less. The only held placement where segmentation alone repaired the meaning was Rishi; the selector missed it. Source contained only three Tessa and one Rishi long-malformed examples per action. Thus learned selective clarification beats a fixed action in point estimates, while selecting cost-effective segmentation on unfamiliar strings remains unproven. Independent graph-truth verification reproduced all chosen and comparator rewards. Local synthetic audio and registered hearing, task-specific semantic replay and simulated costs. No deployed changes. Scripts clarify_choice.py, clarify_choice_audio.py, check_clarify_choice.py, frozen source-action table, candidate readings and evaluated choices under dual-role-word.

### Asking which clause was missed

Train from 12 familiar sentences per voice, each heard intact, with only the first 68% of frames, or with only the last 68%. Freeze legal actions stop, request the first clause, request the last clause, or request the whole utterance. A partial response is inserted only if the original hearing contains exactly one compatible contiguous complementary clause plus the connector, using the known conditional construction; no prefix/suffix channel flag, intended transcript, or teacher boundary is given for the first hearing. State features: whole hearing well formed, recoverable first-clause-plus-link, recoverable link-plus-last-clause. Fit Beta(1,1) estimates of semantic success by public state, action and voice on balanced randomized chosen-action replays, with pooled fallback for absent states. Use modeled price .04 per transmitted form (0/3/3/7); separately compare flat .10 per request. Freeze before evaluation on the 16 previously unused reversed versions of the last study's targets with first/repeat/clause audio at 233/201/215 wpm. Compare fixed requests, repeat on malformed, and a simple parser selecting the complementary clause when uniquely indicated. Measure paired direction switches under opposite losses of the exact same utterance, actual judged meanings, inverted paid clause and net return. Require ≥70% both-direction correct requests on all 16 clipped pairs per voice (missing anchors count as failures), positive net vs repeat-on-malformed under per-form fees, and report conditional performance when both anchors are available. Clarification and pricing are task interfaces; no general conversational claim.

Per-form pricing: Rishi 47/48 correct at mean net .87667, Tessa 46/48 at .87333. The learned policy requested the correct opposite clause on both clipped versions of 10/16 Rishi and 15/16 Tessa sentences (62.5% and 93.75%); the 70% criterion passed only on Tessa. All 10/10 and 15/15 pairs with both required public anchors recovered showed the switch; remaining Rishi failures were missing anchors rather than wrong choices among these paired states. Mean advantages vs whole repeat on malformed hearings: +.13167 [.08325,.22167] and +.43750 [.20998,.66756] by eight-clause-pair bootstrap. Strong direct parser net .87667/.88250; matched on Rishi and exceeded learned on Tessa. Swapping the two clause receipts at the same price reduced 47→20 and 46→12 correct. Tessa flat-fee result 47/48, .90417 net; Rishi 47/48, .91042, with 27 tied best action values under flat fees and fixed menu order. No tied best action at per-form fees. Per voice 144 source deployments, 108 requests transmitting 468 forms total; separate simulated training request expense 18.72 units at per-form fee or 10.80 at flat fee. No whole-study economic break-even measured. Target strings were unseen reversals of previously used pairs, not an unseen grammar. Full semantic correctness and every cost checked against separately calculated truth on all 65,536 graphs. Registered local acoustic decoding; externally supplied legal splice and task-level query operations, with action values learned in replay. No deployment change. ### Learning where an acoustic reply belongs

Hold the prior acoustic recognizer and quantified semantics fixed. Two opaque request handles return a speaker's first or last clause, with the handle assignments reversed between two matched installations. With no word identities, grammar or request names in the composer, enumerate programs that retain a contiguous run of 1–7 heard symbols from either end (offset 0–2), and concatenate it before or after the heard reply. Fit a single program per handle, pooling voices, using one observed binary scene judgment per source fragment/request, and an independent source-only random tie order (seed 139801). Mark syntactically uninterpretable predictions as unsuccessful. Compare syntax-only scoring, no cropping (concatenate the entire fragment), and the supplied grammar-aware splice. Fits were not unique: four maximizing programs per constant convention and three per alternating convention, including longer programs with different future predictions. On fresh length-five/six waveforms, complete all-convention sets Rishi 2/8 (62/96 individual judgments), Tessa 7/8 (84/96); fixed full sharing, fixed reset and longest-period tie control 0/8 on each. Tessa passed both primary endpoints; Rishi missed both. Exact held symbols with frozen acoustic-source fits yielded 8/8 and 96/96 each. Exact earlier statements alone 6/8 and 7/8; exact quotes alone 3/8 and 7/8. Rishi transcribed 17/24 earlier statements and 6/8 quotes; Tessa 23/24 and 7/8. This separates learning the supplied schedule family from hearing the long examples; parsimony was consequential on these particular speakers.

Matched-period diagnostic: the period-two and period-three speakers gave identical intended answers on every short teaching presentation. The distinguishing grade on the one length-five lesson (heard exactly by each voice) selected the correct schedule in both speakers, and exchanging its answer exchanged the fits. On new length-six junction-four pairs, jointly correct under both speakers: withheld/true/inverted 0/1/0 out of 4 for Rishi, 0/3/0 for Tessa. With exact source/target symbols 0/4/0 out of 4 each. Cost was one extra five-symbol teacher judgment, no deployment net measured. Distinct temporal acoustic alignment across longer speech remains a bottleneck. All held labels and actual predictions verified with an independent enumeration of pair starting positions, plus multiset, split disjointness and single-broken-junction checks. Two synthetic voices, evaluator-designed contrasts, explicit periodicity/continuity primitives, enrolled inventory, registered local acoustic decoding and external semantic replay. Scripts join_echo_audio.py VOICE source/target, join_echo.py VOICE and join_echo_check.py via mac.sh in dual-role-word; source fits, receipts, audio and held decisions saved outside the checkout.

### Hearing long sequences after enrollment on short connected speech

All nine wrong Rishi open transcripts on the previous long panel and both Tessa errors were shorter than intended. Freeze the semantic readers and acoustic length penalty. Compare the original isolated eight-word bank with the same bank plus eight labeled four-word recordings at 189 wpm, rotating a seeded permutation so every symbol occurs once in each position (seed 149801). The registered connected-teaching operation must infer the acoustic cuts; labels give the word order, with no times. Preserve all old exemplars. Run both banks on the opened 213-wpm panel, then on a separately seeded fresh panel at 227 wpm (same paired grammar and counts, seed 149802); no model selection on either. Compare exact hearing, whether a correct transcript is the winning candidate at its own count, and complete-set meanings over all speakers. The true count is evaluator-only. Require ≥2/8 fresh Rishi complete-set gain and ≤1/8 Tessa loss, report ceiling effects and per-utterance losses. Report connected-text supervision and elapsed local decode; do not attribute improvements to unsupervised speech acquisition.

Secondary source-only calibration, fixed before inspecting primary accuracy. For both banks use all 24 earlier labeled source strings of length ≥3 at 191 wpm (actual count to be verified), disjoint from evaluation. Score candidates with multipliers 0 through 2 in .05 increments on the existing length penalty; select most exact source transcripts, then lowest total word edit distance, then nearest to original multiplier. Reuse held acoustic candidates, without refitting any exemplar. This distinguishes errors from a mismatched count penalty versus an incorrect same-length candidate. Include untuned and true-count controls.

Primary fresh 227-wpm results: Rishi exact transcripts isolated→connected 22→30/32 (10 repairs and two regressions), complete all-convention judgments 3→7/8, individual 66→90/96. Tessa 30→32/32 (two repairs, no losses), 7→8/8, 90→96/96. On the opened 213-wpm panel Rishi 23→32/32, 2→8/8 and 62→96/96; Tessa 30→30/32 (one repaired and one spoiled), 7→7/8 and 84→84/96. Original opened decodings reproduced exactly. Both prospective margins passed. The fresh connected banks contained the right transcript as their best candidate at its true length on 32/32 in each voice; isolated banks 30/32 and 32/32. Source-only multiplier calibration on 24 separate development utterances returned the unchanged length penalty in all four fits, no extra gain. No string overlap between the opened and fresh panels. Complete-set gains reuse eight synthetic word sets; not a speaker-level population estimate. Local per-panel invocation measurements (training additions, feature extraction and 32 decodes, also uncached synthesis as needed, concurrent processes) 17.8–50.8 s across all primary arms; these timings do not isolate decoder speed. Eight ordered four-symbol enrollment examples plus the old isolated recordings were supplied per voice. The experiment does not identify a minimal teaching set or the physical word boundaries.

Saved fitted acoustic banks independently of the scripts as connected-long-bank-rishi.npz (97,474 bytes) and connected-long-bank-tessa.npz (97,359 bytes). Fresh Python processes restored all 40 retained templates per voice and the unchanged length penalty, and reproduced every stored length-specific candidate and score on an eight-word fresh probe. The registered expert ran locally, conditional rules and test semantics remained frozen; no default reader replaced. ### Does finer acoustic boundary placement resolve the remaining count errors?

Both remaining connected-bank errors on the 227-wpm replay inserted a ninth form into an eight-form hearing. Freeze banks, cost and penalty. Score identical frames and candidate lengths 1–9 on a four-frame and nested two-frame boundary grid, preserving the four-frame minimum segment. On both voices evaluate all 32 old hearings and 32 fresh hearings (seed 150802, 239 wpm, same eight semantic blocks); preregister exact transcripts, paired changes and complete semantic sets. Separately reprocess only when the coarse best-vs-second score gap is at or below the source development 25th percentile, fixed per voice before held scoring, plus a matched-rate random expectation. Require ≥1 fresh complete-set gain on a voice with available headroom and zero complete-set losses on the other; report ceiling, cost and any semantic regressions independently. Separate measured decoding time from synthesis. A negative result distinguishes grid resolution from candidate ranking and possible new regressions. No target transcript, known count, retuning or extra teacher reply is available to live reprocessing.

Resource diagnostic before opening completed results: on low-margin hearings, rerun only the two highest-ranked coarse candidate lengths at finer spacing. Retain the other coarse scores and select the lowest among the merged scores. Compare with full nine-length selective reprocessing. Derive exact outputs from logged per-length calls, charge the actual count of refined lengths, and distinguish this from measured wall time (the two-length shortcut is replayed, not timed). Independent check: the nested grid cannot increase a same-length best cost when both calls return a candidate.

Opened 227-wpm Rishi: fine spacing repaired both original 8→9 overcounts but introduced two new overcounts (8→9 and 6→7). Exact transcripts stayed at 30/32 and complete sets at 7/8; individual judgments fell 90→88/96. On the fresh 239-wpm panel the original bank/spacing already achieved 32/32 transcripts and 8/8 complete sets per voice. Refinement introduced one Rishi 8→9 error with no downstream verdict change; both voices still 96/96 individual, 8/8 complete. Tessa exactly preserved every transcript in both panels. Thus no available fresh semantic gain and the opened mechanism failed retention. In every panel both grids gave the correct hearing at the correct length on all 32 utterances; the finer grid never increased the minimum score at any shared length. All changed transcripts reflect length ranking. No decoder default change justified.

Source-quartile low-margin rule triggered 24/32 and 20/32 Rishi opened/fresh and 25/32 and 18/32 Tessa; it reproduced all full-fine outcomes despite a much larger trigger fraction than in the shorter development set. Matched-count uniform random refinement on Rishi opened expected 88.887/96 (7.036/8) versus 88/96 (7/8) selective; all other panels fixed 96/96 for both. Refining just the top two coarse lengths on the triggered waveforms reproduced every full-gate transcript (48/40 and 50/36 additional length-specific searches versus 216/180 and 225/162). True-count comparator evaluator-only. Fresh summed elapsed decode times over 32 utterances coarse/fine 27.0/99.9 s Rishi, 30.7/116.3 s Tessa under concurrent local load, excluding synthesis and feature extraction. These are cumulative call times, not single-session latency or a direct timing of the two-length shortcut. Prior coarse rankings reproduced exactly, fresh 32 strings disjoint from both earlier panels. Registered acoustic calls, frozen semantic reader and independently enumerated truth labels. ### Transferring the long-quotation listener to unfamiliar voices

Freeze the Rishi connected bank, its scale/penalty and previously fitted sequence rules. Target voices Daniel (UK) and Allison (Enhanced, US), new to this experiment. Use 32 existing 239-wpm panel strings at a fresh rate of 223 wpm (same semantics; unseen voices). Compare zero enrollment, two and eight ordered four-symbol target phrases at 189 wpm, eight isolated target words at 155 wpm, and eight isolated plus eight connected as a target-trained reference. For the two-phrase budget choose opposite rotations 0 and 4 so all eight sounds occur once. For cross-voice phrase enrollment, align target waveforms using the old isolated seed templates via registered teaching. Keep the old isolated and connected entries; add target excerpts across positions to avoid hiding a once-taught sound in one position. Same-data control forces equal-duration cuts at the three rounded quarter points while preserving all other entries. No target feedback from test strings. Require ≥2/8 joint-set gain over zero enrollment and ≥6/8 complete sets on each target for a successful low-teaching transfer. Report exact hearing, conditional on true count, individual truths, source alignment versus equal cuts and the target reference. State cost in labeled recordings and symbols. No general human-speaker claim.

Attribution control before held scores: after source-guided extraction of two/eight labeled target phrases, remove all old exemplars from the runtime bank, keeping only the newly extracted samples and the same scale, count penalty and recognition algorithm. This tests whether competing source pronunciations help or interfere independently of their role during alignment. It is not a from-scratch learner.

Post-primary localization after observing poor Allison results, before seeing the teaching replay: run the full target-trained reference on its own eight isolated and eight connected enrollment recordings, plus the same eight connected strings synthesized at the 223-wpm test rate. Report open and known-length accuracy separately. This same-string test probes reproduction and rate transfer; it cannot estimate generalization to new strings.

After the reference reproduced all enrollment strings at both phrase rates, run a separate matched rate-223 localization. For each of the eight original rotations take its original four symbols or the full eight-symbol cycle beginning at the same point, then speak either that sequence or its reverse. Forward eight-word sequences preserve taught neighboring pairs while extending beyond the taught length; reversal preserves symbol counts while changing neighbors and edge positions. Compare open transcription and evaluator-given-count per cell, separately from the generalization benchmark. No retuning or reuse of these outcomes for primary selection.

Primary transfer failed the ≥6/8 per-voice criterion. Open exact transcripts / complete sets / individual judgments (32/8/96 denominators): Daniel zero 0/0/24, two connected 6/0/56, eight connected 15/3/42, two equal-cut 0/0/42, eight equal-cut 19/0/60, eight isolated 23/3/74, target reference 23/3/76. Allison respectively 0/0/28, 0/0/34, 0/0/36, 0/0/30, 1/0/24, 0/0/36, 3/0/34. Thus more individually correct transcripts need not yield more complete comprehension sets. Removing old exemplars after alignment left exact and complete counts unchanged for both phrase budgets and both voices. With evaluator-supplied length the eight-phrase banks reached 20/32 and 4/8 Daniel, 1/32 and 0/8 Allison; full references 24/32 and 5/8 Daniel, 3/32 and 1/8 Allison. Eight-phrase results therefore cannot be attributed just to choosing incorrect lengths. Two/four-symbol-phrase lessons cost 2 recordings, 8 labels (3.40/3.32 seconds Daniel/Allison); eight phrases 8 recordings, 32 labels (13.75/12.93 seconds); isolated bank 8 recordings, 8 labels (5.50/5.64 seconds). All 32 target recordings differed physically between voices; verified every winner and frozen semantic judgment against an independent position enumeration. Actual per-arm decode across 32 recordings 16.5–34.2 cumulative seconds under concurrent local load. Two synthetic voices and eight reused word sets at a new rate. No core/default acoustic change.

Target-trained references recognized all eight isolated recordings and all eight teaching phrases at both 189 and 223 wpm, for both voices. Matched 223-wpm cycle test, open exact and given-count exact identical in every cell: Daniel forward/reversed four words 8/7 of 8, eight words 8/7 of 8. Allison four words 8/3 of 8, eight words 8/0 of 8. Thus eight-word duration alone was insufficient to break Allison's reference, and changing order/phonetic neighbors already broke five short phrases with unchanged symbol counts. This locates sensitivity to the surrounding sequence but does not separate coarticulation, prosody and segmentation. Only one circular teaching order was used. ### Does the diversity of neighboring sounds improve held-out voice comprehension?

Keep eight target isolated examples, eight four-word labeled phrases, one presentation per word per position, 189-wpm phrase teaching, the same acoustic learner and the same frozen literal quotation rule. In the original cycle every within-phrase directed transition advances one in the underlying eight-symbol ring. Diverse teaching uses offsets [0,1,3,6] and thus 24 distinct directed transitions versus 8. Reserve every other directed pair for the main test. Freeze seed 151801, eight positive/negative matched eight-word permutations and their three-word quotations at 231 wpm; each adjacent pair in every test recording is absent from both teaching arms. Require the foil to contain both first and last query symbols and all words with identical multiplicity; one adjacent subpair may match. Compare complete truth pairs and exact transcripts, with true count only as a diagnostic. Prespecify ≥2/8 paired-set gain on Allison without >1/8 loss on Daniel, and report the known mixed 32-string benchmark separately. Include learner-free exact acoustics and the bank trained on only the eight isolated words. This is one curriculum construction, not a population comparison across random curricula.

Secondary matched decoder comparison before opening scores: pool all teaching excerpts across positions, retaining identical templates, scale, isolated exemplars and the fixed length penalty. Cross pooling versus original position restrictions with both curricula on the primary panel to test whether context coverage is being limited by access to only same-position examples. No extra recordings or labels. Report separately from the primary comparison.

Post-primary localization: on the same paired test retain the heard short quote and substitute exact earlier statements, then reverse that intervention. Count complete pairs separately; report whether a matching positive quote exists as a contiguous substring of the true-count earlier hearing. On the opened mixed benchmark count transcript gains/losses grouped by whether the word sequence contains a transition added by diverse teaching. These are diagnostic associations, not fresh tests.

On the primary unseen-neighbor panel, cycle→diverse complete positive/negative pairs Daniel 8→7/8 (individual 16→15/16), Allison 3→1/8 (11→9/16). Paired eight-block bootstrap differences −.125 [−.375,0] and −.25 [−.75,.25]. All foils were rejected in both arms; the change was in recognizing true quotations. Exact transcripts out of 24 Daniel 17→15, Allison 6→7. Allison's diverse bank heard 7/8 quotes but 0/16 complete long statements exactly. Supplying only the correct earlier statements moved the diverse listener from 1→7/8; supplying only the correct quote left 1/8. Both streams exact gave 8/8. Evaluator-known counts by themselves gave Allison 4→5/8 across curricula (Daniel 7→7/8). Position pooling gave Daniel cycle/diverse 7/7 of 8 and Allison 4/3 of 8; eight isolated exemplars alone gave 6/8 Daniel, 0/8 Allison. Thus the preregistered Allison improvement failed; coverage of more directed pairs did not establish reliable generalization to entirely withheld neighbors. Same-order curriculum comparison at one budget and on two voices, no universal curriculum ranking.

On the previously opened mixed benchmark, however, Daniel exact 23→28/32, 3→5/8 full sets and 76→88/96 judgments, Allison 3→18/32, 0→0/8 and 34→68/96. With true count the diverse banks reached 6/8 and 3/8 there. Of the 32 mixed transcripts, 28 contained a newly taught directed pair: Daniel gained five correct and lost zero in those 28, no change in the remaining four; Allison gained 15 and lost one among 28, gained one among four. These associations do not isolate the effect of one particular neighbor. Word positions, enrollment symbol counts, isolated samples, and decoder settings matched; phrase teaching duration cycle/diverse 13.75/13.68 s Daniel, 12.93/12.84 s Allison. Verified zero shared teaching/test edges on all primary recordings, matched word counts, every acoustic winner, positive/foil behavior with independent substring evaluation, and the semantic checks on every mixed decision. No deployed change. ### Matching a spoken quotation directly to a passage

Freeze a query-by-example acoustic reader using the registered speech-envelope frames. Normalize feature channels with mean and standard deviation from the voice's eight four-word diverse teaching recordings. Use subsequence dynamic time warping with free start/end, local steps (1,1),(1,2),(2,1), mean squared frame distance and score normalized by query frames. Select an absolute accept threshold only on eight development quote/true/foil triplets from the original teaching cycle, with independent three-word query recordings at 189 wpm. Compare absolute joint yes/no accuracy with forced choice (which passage fits better); the latter is strictly easier. Run separately on earlier test and eight fresh triplets at 219 wpm, seed 152801, excluding previous test strings and all teaching transitions. Each true/foil shares all words; foil contains at least one adjacent two-word piece of the quote. Compare original transcribe-then-search diverse reference on the same fresh audio, nearest-frame bag (also source calibrated), and time-shuffling the query with unchanged threshold. Test an independently voiced query with each recording normalized according to its voice, no cross-voice threshold refit. Prespecify ≥6/8 complete absolute decisions on each voice and ≥2/8 gain over transcription on Allison, with zero claim of full transcript improvement. Report when thresholds or ranking fail independently.

Secondary calibration diagnosis after the primary open: for cross-voice queries, estimate the acceptance threshold using the same original development examples but with cross-voice query audio, then replay without additional target tuning. Report a separate hindsight ceiling over all possible single thresholds on each evaluated panel, distinguishing perfect paired ranking from globally separable scores. These post-primary comparisons do not change the original no-refit result.

Same-voice direct matching fresh: accepted all 8 true passages per voice, rejected 7/8 Daniel and 5/8 Allison foils, giving 7/8 and 5/8 complete yes/no pairs. Diverse transcribe-then-search 4/8 and 1/8 on those identical new recordings (15/24 and 7/24 exact transcripts). Gain criteria on Allison passed (+4 pairs), absolute criterion passed only on Daniel. Forced-choice ranked the true passage first in 8/8 on each voice on both fresh and opened panels. On the opened panel absolute direct results were only 1/8 Daniel, 2/8 Allison (seven/six false acceptances). Order-free nearest-frame baseline fresh 1/8 and 5/8 joint, 7/8 forced-choice each. Scrambling query frames at frozen thresholds reduced direct absolute joint scores to 0/8 both voices, with 5/8 and 7/8 forced choices; the order-free distances were preserved to numerical precision. Development direct joint 8/8 on each voice (eight labeled triplets but only four distinct query strings). Original same-voice thresholds .6323 Daniel and .9114 Allison. On fresh independently voiced queries, zero acceptances for either threshold despite 7/8 and 6/8 forced choices. Source cross-voice recalibration (1.2112 Daniel, 1.3545 Allison) yielded 2/8 and 0/8 fresh joint pairs. Hindsight best single threshold on fresh same-voice scores 8/8 and 7/8, cross-voice only 4/8 and 3/8. Thus direct hearing recovered order information missed by transcription; making an absolute decision independently of a competing foil and transferring across voices remain limited. Rates and phonetic contents differed between the opened and fresh tests, so no isolated speaking-rate attribution.

Generic acoustic envelopes were computed by the registered local service; dynamic alignment and threshold estimation were external. The known quote/passage boundary and the literal question were supplied. No test transcript fed the direct matcher. Independently verified alignment costs with forward path relaxation, all stored joint decisions, recording disjointness, hidden-edge exclusion and foil construction. ### Learning an acoustic correspondence between two voices

Freeze the passage aligner and previous feature normalization. Fit an affine map from the questioner's voice to the passage voice by ridge regression toward the identity (penalty .10 times sample count). Supervision is just the eight paired 155-wpm isolated sound identities, resampled at 24 relative times per recording; exact frame correspondence within each sound is assumed by the fit. Compare identity, cyclically mispaired anchors, and an anchor-distance representation (four temporal prototype bins per isolated word per voice, cosine-normalized distances). Fit a separate decision threshold for each method on the same eight prior cross-voice source triplets; the competing test passage is never used in an absolute decision. Evaluate both directions on the previous 219-wpm panel and 12 previously unused triplets at 225 wpm (seed 153801, no earlier test strings or training edges). Foils keep both words of at least one adjacent subpair. Require ≥9/12 complete absolute pairs on each direction and ≥2/12 gain over identity for an affine transfer claim. Report forced-choice, positive/negative decisions and same-voice baseline. Mispaired-anchor and representation outcomes separate. No held model selection.

Additional prospective calibration control, before scoring new recordings: for the affine reader split only the public quote waveform into three equal frame runs (no labeled word boundaries); use the actual match cost minus the minimum cost among all five reordered-run alternatives on that same passage. Refit one source threshold, compare with raw affine distance. This local contrast uses neither a second test passage nor a target label; actual words need not occupy equal durations. Keep this separately scored from the primary map.

After primary scoring, preregister independent replication of the local contrast. Freeze both affine maps, untransformed and transformed thresholds, and equal-third correction thresholds from the original development. Generate eight further triplets (seed 153901; exclude all previously evaluated quote and passage strings). Record every new triplet at both 209 and 237 wpm. Require ≥6/8 absolute pairs per direction and rate plus ≥1/8 advantage over raw affine in every cell to claim replicated benefit of local calibration. Report matched-rate observations on identical strings. No adjustment on replication.

Primary 12 fresh triplets at 225 wpm, direction indexed by passage voice: Daniel-passage identity→paired affine 1→6/12 complete absolute pairs, forced-choice 6→9/12; Allison-passage 4→8/12 absolute and 10→12/12 forced. Both gained ≥2 pairs but neither reached 9/12. Paired bootstrap absolute gain intervals [.083,.750] and [−.083,.750] respectively. Mispaired affine anchors yielded 1/12 and 0/12 absolute. Anchor-distance representation yielded 6/12 and 7/12, corresponding mispairings 2/12 and 1/12. Same-voice baseline absolute 7/12 and 5/12 while ranking 12/12 each. On the opened 219-wpm panel affine absolute improved Daniel 2→5/8 and Allison 0→7/8 over source-calibrated cross-voice identity. Hindsight optimal single affine thresholds on the primary panel achieved at most 8/12 and 9/12; scalar calibration cannot explain all the residual ranking/score overlap. All conditions use the same small supplied synthetic language, paired isolated names, and public enrollment samples for voice normalization. No mapping for an unpaired third voice was demonstrated.

Equal-third temporal contrast: 9/12 and 10/12 primary absolute pairs, but only 2/8 and 6/8 on the opened panel. Frozen replication with eight further triplets spoken at both rates: Daniel passages at 209/237 wpm identity 2/2, affine 6/4, contrast 5/4 of 8; Allison passages identity 1/1, affine 3/2, contrast 6/7. Both mapped methods ranked the correct passage first on all 8 triplets in all four cells. Local contrast's replication criterion failed on Daniel and passed both cells on Allison; contrast-minus-affine bootstrap intervals Daniel [−.50,.25]/[−.50,.50], Allison [.125,.75]/[.25,.875]. The two rate arms reuse the same phrases. This establishes transfer of relative matching beyond unmapped acoustic costs on these examples, with absolute thresholds still unreliable. Verified complete exclusion of previous triplet strings and of taught transitions, exact reproduction of earlier unmapped scores and agreement of raw costs between both contrast implementations. Both direction-specific affine checkpoints (3,722 bytes each) reconstructed the frozen original score in a fresh process. ### Calibrating a single cross-voice passage against familiar recordings

Keep prior affine maps and acoustic aligner. Use previous labeled encounters from 219/225 wpm and the two-rate replication (209/237) as development: 28 distinct triplets, giving the repeated eight half weight per rate. For each independent query/passage compute raw affine distance, its contrast against five within-query chunk reorderings, and two reference differences: raw minus median distance to the eight diverse four-word enrollment passages for this query, and raw minus median distance of four distinct original development quotes to this passage. References are fixed prior recordings, never the competing current passage. Fit equal-weight class-balanced logistic readouts with source-standardized inputs, fixed ridge .10 on mean log loss, unpenalized intercept. Compare raw-only, raw+temporal contrast, and full with both references. Use threshold .5 without target prior adjustment. Freeze ten further distinct triplets, seed 154801, all previous test strings and taught edges excluded. Test each twice with query/passage speeds 203/235 and 235/203, in both voice directions. Prespecify ≥7/10 joint accept/reject pairs and ≥.02 Brier improvement over the strongest development-selected reduced model in each direction/speed cell. Report sensitivities, false accepts, forced ranking, teaching and reference cost. An oracle paired choice is not an absolute answer.

Exposure control before opening the new scores: apply the earlier unchanged affine and chunk-contrast thresholds learned from eight original development triplets to the exact new recordings. Compare joint pair counts against the larger-history fits, with model cost/teaching differences explicit. Report the previous-threshold results separately from the matched-data primary feature comparison.

Fresh matched passages, ten distinct triplets reused at query/passage rates 203/235 then 235/203. Raw-only / raw plus temporal contrast / full with familiar references, complete independent accept/reject pairs: Daniel passages 3/5/5 then 5/4/5; Allison 4/8/6 then 5/3/3 (each out of ten). Full-model positives accepted and foils falsely accepted were 7/2, 8/4, 8/2, 6/3 respectively. Within-triplet forced ranking was 9/10, 9/10, 9/10, 10/10. Both prespecified endpoints failed. Against the source-selected two-feature control, paired Brier advantages with 95% triplet-bootstrap intervals were −.0083 [−.0197,.0022], −.0086 [−.0267,.0082], +.0124 [−.0056,.0332], +.0057 [−.0206,.0322]. Earlier eight-triplet raw/contrast thresholds achieved 4/5, 4/4, 4/5, 5/4 complete pairs. More source exposures did not consistently improve absolute decisions. Full features used eight prior passage comparisons per distinct query and four prior quote comparisons per passage in addition to the six temporal alignments; shared references were cached.

Post hoc diagnostic: even choosing the single best acceptance threshold with all held labels available yields at most 5, 5, 7, 6 complete pairs for the full model. With unchanged words, exchanging the two voice-specific speaking rates changed 3/20 Daniel-passage and 5/20 Allison-passage binary decisions. Strong within-pair ranking therefore coexists with substantial cross-item score overlap. These findings concern two synthetic voices, eight paired anchor identities and one ten-triplet panel. Verified no previous test strings or teaching transitions, paired inventories and substring truth, source weighting, and a scalar recomputation of the first truth/foil probabilities in each cell. Local registered acoustic features, external alignment and logistic readout. ### Checking the weakest interval of a cross-voice quotation

Freeze eight additional matched triplets (seed 155801), excluding all previous quote/passage strings and taught directed edges, with a shared two-word subquote in each foil. ### Isolating where the cross-voice matcher listens

Development diagnostic on the eight opened weakest-interval quotes: build a three-chunk passage from independent connected recordings (three, three and two words). Concatenate registered normalized feature sequences, with no boundary input to the frozen two-feature reader. Supply a three-component report plan and known grounded classes of its operands (viewpoint cue, named participant, referring cue). Across six toy conventions, a fourth spoken form turns a report into a request when inserted immediately before or after one of those three classes. The learner is given generic one-symbol insertion hypotheses: any of eight enrolled sounds at a fixed gap or adjacent to a grounded class; no designated question sound, anchor or side. Teach three paired spoken reports and accepted requests in cyclic orders, plus a one-pair comparison. Fit by maximizing exact transformations of the decoded pairs, tie-break by fixed-gap then lexical order. Hold out the three reversed orders and the name 2 in production; marker/role sounds have overlapping training and test coverage. Use synthetic voice-specific registered open recognition on source phrases and on produced utterances; test base report supplied as ordered grounded handles, no target question demo. Factor identification, exact authored question, and exact teacher hearing separately. Compare fixed gap learned on the same examples, exact-source-hearing diagnostic, and oracle production. Preregister ≥80% correct authored forms and ≥75% correct end-to-end requests for every convention per voice; report learned same-base switches before versus after. This is a bounded morphology/construction experiment with paired reformulations and one new particle from the existing bank. The teacher requires exact words and order; no claim about unconstrained syntax or articulatory synthesis.

Post-primary hearing diagnosis on the same test recordings: give the receiver the supplied three operand classes and the fact that an utterance is either a three-word report or has one added particle 7 in any gap. Search every class order and every particle position, including all invalid placements for the particular teacher, without the intended base words. Compare open hearing to this narrowed search on the oracle-produced test requests; score false recognition as the intended request on the three other gaps for each matched report/convention. This is a supplied receiver vocabulary/grammar diagnostic on opened audio.

Three-pair learner authored/heard correctly on Daniel 26/36 and 22/36, Allison 36/36 and 22/36. In teacher order before/after viewpoint, name and referring cue, Daniel authored 6,2,6,6,0,6 of six and exactly delivered 6,2,4,6,0,4; Allison authored six throughout, delivered 3,4,5,2,4,4. Thus all-convention authoring passed only on Allison, end-to-end passed neither. Learned rule matched the intended class/side on four of six Daniel and six of six Allison fits. Best fixed gap authored 12/36 on both (delivered 12/10 Daniel/Allison); one-pair fit authored 12/8 and delivered 10/6. For the same base requiring both before and after across each anchor, three-pair learning delivered 6/18 Daniel and 7/18 Allison. Exact source readings uniquely identify the correct transferable edits (oracle authoring 36/36 per voice); oracle productions heard 32/36 and 22/36. Each cohort used six base plans, three unseen word orders, one held name and overlapping authored strings across conventions, not 36 independent speakers. Source lesson fit is one bottleneck on Daniel; acoustic delivery alone caps Allison. In the separate receiver diagnosis on oracle productions, search over only reports or single-particle requests raised exact hearing 32→34/36 Daniel and 22→28/36 Allison, with zero substitutions into the intended valid request on 108 wrong-gap and 36 bare-control judgments per voice in both arms. No claim of a learned receiver grammar for this narrowing. Verified intended gaps independently and source/held name separation. Reproduce question_edit.py VOICE, question_edit_check.py and question_edit_listen.py VOICE via mac.sh. Local registered acoustic scoring, ordinary Mac voice synthesis for output, external edit induction. All recordings and learned edit decisions saved locally. No default grammar change.

### Joint acoustic inference of a question construction

For a source report/request pair, enumerate all three-word report interpretations containing one sound from each supplied grounded class in any order. For each known one-symbol edit hypothesis, find the base minimizing mean per-word acoustic cost of the report plus mean per-word cost of the resulting request. Sum these minima across three source pairs to fit one edit; ties use the previous fixed ordering. No transcript of either recording supplied. Compare old open-transcript fit, one-best within the same constrained base/request candidate spaces followed by discrete edit fitting, joint inference with source reports permuted across the three requests, and oracle edit. Freeze a fresh panel with three different cyclic teaching plans and six reversed-order held plans; source 207 and production 237 wpm. Cross all six class-relative rules with inserted sound 7 and with sound 3, which already has a viewpoint meaning and can recur within a question. Score the two particle regimes separately; require 5/6 correct rules and 85% authored requests per regime and voice, and report exact teacher hearing with open decoding. Also reproduce original source fit on the previous panel for diagnosis. Validate batched costs against the registered scorer on independent candidate samples and every selected paired witness.

Before inspecting the new outcomes, add a request-only control: remove the report waveform term entirely while preserving the legal base set and all edit hypotheses. This distinguishes evidence for matching the two turns from exploiting constraints visible in the accepted questions alone.

Joint acoustic scoring recovered all six old Daniel rules, improving old open-fit authorship 26→36/36 and delivery 22→32/36. One-best decoding within the same three/four-word candidate sets also achieved those gains. Old Allison stayed 36 authored, 22 heard. On fresh material every voice × particle regime produced 6/6 intended rules and 36/36 authored questions, meeting the prospective authorship criteria; open fit and constrained one-best both tied. Exact hearing of those productions was Daniel 29/36 distinct particle, 24/36 homophonous particle; Allison 18/36 and 20/36. These equal the oracle-edit delivery ceilings on the same audio. Although shuffling reports across requests recovered zero correct rules per cell, removing the reports altogether recovered all rules on both particles and voices. For these clean synthetic examples, the distribution of accepted question forms alone identifies the edits under the supplied candidate restrictions; incompatible pairings actively mislead the joint model. No incremental value for uncertainty propagation was established. When inserting the homophonous particle beside viewpoint marker 3, before/after yield identical strings for one of three teaching and three of six held plans; the other marker supplies the distinguishing evidence. Authorship counts share base plans and some identical questions across conventions.

Registered scorer reproduced the numeric acoustic costs for each best pair witness and separate sampled candidates; old open decisions reproduced saved recordings/results. The vectorized enumerator used the existing package's template stretching outside the core. Source inference used three lesson pairs per installation, 72 possible grounded bases and 80 possible edit rules. Future work on the listener can now be tested with intended production held fixed. Reproduce joint_edit.py VOICE old/new, joint_edit_check.py, joint_edit_unpaired.py VOICE and joint_edit_identify.py through mac.sh. Wavs, rule scores and every held production/hearing retained locally; no deployed acoustic changes.

### Listening for a learned request without accepting malformed questions

Freeze the prior source-learned class-relative edit per voice and convention. Compare open hearing, closed class/particle search with equal rule bias, and a learned bias for well-formed requests versus both ordinary reports and wrong-gap utterances. Receiver considers every ordered base over the known three classes, not just test plans. Fit one bias per voice and particle on the three source plans at 207 wpm, pooling six conventions, with grid {0, ±.02, ±.05, ±.10, ±.20} in existing mean template-cost units, source length penalty unchanged. Maximize equally weighted accuracy on exact valid request, rejecting bare report, and rejecting wrong-gap input, ties smallest absolute bias. Score the previously opened 237-wpm recordings separately from a fresh 219-wpm panel using held name 2 and three cyclic orders of each of two compositions. Enumerate all distinct one-particle insertions per base; classify phonologically identical alternatives according to every valid generating base under the teacher. Accepting a different valid request on a negative is a false alarm. Require ≥3/36 extra exact valid requests over open decoding and at most 5% false alarms in each negative category in both voices and particle regimes on the fresh panel. Report added utility over zero bias separately; isolate any ceiling where identical sequences have multiple parses.

Fresh exact hearing of the 36 intended requests per cell, open→restricted listener: distinct particle Daniel 31→36, Allison 18→26; homophonous particle Daniel 31→33, Allison 27→31. The ≥3 gain criterion passed three of four cells. All 144 bare-report judgments were rejected. Wrong-gap false alarms: Daniel 0/108 distinct and 0/82 homophonous; Allison 1/108 and 0/82. These are crossed utterance/convention judgments, not 524 unique negative recordings. For the homophonous sound, eight additional constructed gap variants per voice are legal requests for a different base under the same convention; none of the evaluated strings has two legal base parses under a single convention. Counting all defensible requests gives Daniel 39→41/44 and Allison 34→38/44. Paired 95% bootstrap across the six base plans for all-valid accuracy improvements: Daniel distinct [.028,.306], homophonous [0,.111]; Allison [.028,.472], [0,.215]. Development zero bias was already perfect across the three strata and all fits retained it. The gain is evidence for constraining acoustic candidates using the enrolled particle and known operand classes, not for learning a special numerical request prior. The learned placement rule interprets the winning words as a request or other utterance. On opened intended requests distinct/homophonous results Daniel 29→36 and 24→30, Allison 18→25 and 20→28; Daniels's opened homophonous bare-report false alarms rose 1→3/36.

Error localization on fresh Allison distinct-particle cases: among 36 genuine requests, the correct string had the best valid-request acoustic score on only 27; one additional case was blocked by the report/invalid competitor. Thus even oracle threshold selection respecting the false-alarm limit can recover at most one beyond 26 here without improving relative acoustic ranking. Independently verified grammar membership, reconstruction of original open hearings, sampled competing sequence costs with the registered scorer, authored-only denominators and hindsight ceiling. Two synthetic voices, previously enrolled eight forms, known finite report vocabulary/shape, six shared test plans and six teacher conventions. Reproduce listen_bias.py VOICE dev/fit/opened/fresh/check, listen_bias_check.py, listen_planned.py and listen_ceiling.py locally via mac.sh. Saved every candidate winner, competing score difference, rule and waveform; no default receiver change.

### Teaching the contrast between two names inside a spoken question

On the opened 219-wpm panel all eight distinct Allison confusions among valid questions replaced name 2 with 1. Test six labeled connected three-word reports at 201 wpm: (X,4,6), (5,X,3), (4,5,X), each with X=1 or 2. Register the existing isolated-template alignment operation and append just the six learned name segments with their position labels to the frozen diverse bank. No changed question rules or acceptance bias. Compare original bank, two freshly spoken isolated names, and the same six connected segments with the two name labels interchanged. On fresh 225-wpm recordings use three distinct reports per name: (3,X,6), (X,5,4), (6,3,X), with X=0,1,2; none is a teaching phrase. Hold name 0 as a retention control. Cross six speaker placement conventions and both particles. Score each matched name-1/name-2 pair only when both intended requests decode exactly, plus bare and wrong-gap false alarms. Require ≥3 more complete pairs out of 18 on Allison per particle and no more than one previously correct name-0 request spoiled per 18; report Daniel separately. Replay the original name-2-only distinct-particle panel as an opened diagnostic. This tests supervised contrastive enrollment, not autonomous acquisition of name meanings.

Before fresh scoring, add a single-name ablation retaining only the three new examples of “foda” (name 2), the originally missed word. This tests whether teaching its competitor “kemi” also matters for retaining the two-way distinction. Score the same matched pairs and losses, with no selection on target outcomes.

Six connected teaching recordings lasted 7.35 s Allison / 7.59 s Daniel and supplied 18 word labels per voice (six kept name segments). On the fresh-rate 225-wpm Allison distinct-particle panel, baseline/isolated/connected/foda-only/swapped complete pairs were 12/12/14/14/10 of 18. For the homophonous particle: 15/15/15/15/7. The three-pair gain threshold failed both regimes. Kemi stayed at 18/18 in each correctly labeled arm; foda improved 12→14 and stayed 15/18 respectively. None of the 16 and 18 formerly correct pavo guards regressed, and the corrected bank raised no false alarms (0/54 bare per regime; 0/162 and 0/108 wrong gaps). Daniel's correctly labeled arms were unchanged at 18/18 and 15/18 complete pairs (swapped 5/18 both). On opened distinct-particle requests Allison improved 26→32/36 (four distinct audio strings), Daniel stayed 36/36. Allison's two fresh-rate judgment gains were a single recording, “foda teka vashi mepo”, heard under two conventions; the same string improved at the earlier rate. All 18 fresh-rate foda strings per regime were represented in previously opened listening panels, though none of the three-word test plans used the new teaching phrases. This is cadence transfer on a limited inventory, not independent lexical replication. The three retained foda excerpts alone reproduced the gains, while a freshly isolated rendition did not. Force-swapping names damaged both voices. Initial preparation exposed an isolated-bank input shape error in the research harness, corrected before any scoring. All opened base decisions verified against saved zero-bias results. Reproduce name_contrast.py VOICE prep then base/isolated/connected/foda/swapped and name_contrast_check.py in dual-role-word via mac.sh. Local registered teaching, external exhaustive scoring as previously verified, saved all arms and WAVs. No default bank change.

### Do the saved name lessons transfer to previously unrecorded question strings?

### Selecting which pronunciation to enroll from corrected practice on two additional voices

Freeze before synthesis: Samantha (Enhanced) and Joelle (Enhanced), two installed US-English synthetic voices not used to select this policy. Each gets the common eight isolated forms at 155 wpm and eight labeled four-word enrollment phrases at 189, then eight practice frames (24 questions) from the existing 28-frame inventory. Stratify by particle with seed 169801, one of four distinct and seven of 24 homophonous frames for practice; other 20 frames/60 individual questions are held. At 225 wpm, compare the name in the original best valid acoustic candidate to a supplied corrected name on each practice turn. Ask for three connected renditions of the most often misheard name if it has at least two such errors (ties smaller identifier), otherwise skip. Align and retain only that name's three segments. This is a supplied selection policy using public corrections; calibration from speech and choosing a new target name are the test. All names have the same three available teaching contexts. Each new rendition costs .5 reward units; test utility is one unit per exactly accepted request minus total new fees. Compare every fixed target name, skip, matched random choice and buying all names. Require a strict net gain over skip on each new voice and pooled gain over the best common fixed action. Report gross recoveries/losses, possible unused gain and historical enrollment/practice separately. Evaluate controls in independent offline branches with the chooser frozen before any held scoring. Known placement rules and all-valid workload as in prior minimal-pair panel.

Samantha heard 24/24 practice requests exactly and made zero best-candidate name errors; Joelle heard 23/24 exactly with one kemi name error. The frozen policy skipped both, unchanged on every leave-one-practice-frame-out audit. Held 60 decisions per voice: Samantha skip 54 correct/54 net; targeted pavo, kemi, foda gross 53/54/55 (net 51.5/52.5/53.5); all 55 gross/50.5 net. Joelle skip 55/55; targets gross 55/56/55 (53.5/54.5/53.5 net); all 56/51.5. Thus no offered lesson pack could repay its 1.5-unit price on either voice, even in hindsight. At most one extra correct output per voice; total package fees must fall below one unit for positive margin on these particular 60 outputs. Pooled gate 109 net ties skip vs 107 best common single-name policy, 102 buying all. Neither strict criterion passed. Spending-matched random on zero purchases is identical to skip. Common enrollment 40 labels in 16 recordings per voice, 18.55 and 18.96 s respectively, plus 24 corrected practice names. No incremental audio acquired in the selected branches.

Across practice and held turns there were zero wrong names in Samantha's best valid candidate and just one on Joelle, so no same-size practice split could trigger the two-error buying rule. Nine of eleven held baseline failures had the correct complete string as best valid candidate but lost to a report or invalid competitor (four Samantha and five Joelle). This points to question-versus-nonquestion evidence on these two voices, rather than frequent confusion between names. Only positive requests were scored here, so simply accepting more questions cannot be recommended without matched negative controls. Verified all 84 paired cross-voice WAVs physically differ, practice/held strings disjoint, actions fixed before held scores and all utilities reconstructed. Reproduce choose_enroll.py VOICE prepare practice/teach/held, choose then none/0/1/2/all; choose_enroll_check.py, choose_enroll_audit.py and choose_enroll_coverage.py in dual-role-word via mac.sh. ### Learning when to accept a question on two additional voices

Reuse the frozen 8/20 practice/test frame split from speaker-specific enrollment and its original acoustic banks. For each positive recording, also present the identical audio under a different supplied placement convention for which no valid base produces that string, plus the ordinary three-word report under the positive convention. Choose the invalid convention by cycling through the remaining five and taking the first truly nonlicensing one. For held bare-report assessment exclude bare strings present in practice. Train at 225 wpm; evaluate the opened positive panel with new counterfactual labels and a separately reported new rendition at 213 wpm using the same held strings. Numerical competition: compare (i) unchanged zero bias, (ii) one offset for accepting the best legal request, and (iii) separate offsets against the cheapest bare report and wrong-gap candidate. Fit per voice on practice by equal-weighting exact intended question, rejecting same-waveform invalid convention, and rejecting bare report. Freeze grid 0, ±.02, ±.05, ±.10, ±.20, ±.40 with smallest total absolute bias favored on ties. Independently report hindsight feasible performance. Require at least two additional exact questions above zero and ≤5% false accepts per negative type on fresh-rate recordings for each voice, and report both-correct matched convention pairs. The existence of the operand classes and the relevant placement rule is common knowledge. This is calibration of competing acoustic readings, not discovery of speaker intent from tone alone.

Secondary evidence-coverage diagnostic after practice selected zero for both voices but before 213-wpm outcomes: use opened 225-wpm labels from the other 19 held frames plus original practice to fit the same two offsets separately for each held frame; exclude every calibration example sharing any waveform string with that frame (including bare reports). Score only its 213-wpm rendition with that fit, aggregate all 20 folds. This costs extra labels and is separate from the frozen 24-question practice result. No 213-wpm labels select offsets.

The small practice set selected zero offsets on both voices. Practice exact valid / wrong-convention rejected / bare rejected: Samantha 24/24, 24/24, 24/24; Joelle 23/24, 24/24, 24/24. At 225 wpm baseline positives reproduced 54/60 and 55/60. At the fresh 213 wpm, original and calibrated scores tied at 56/60 Samantha and 55/60 Joelle, with all 60 matched invalid-convention forks and all 30 bare-report judgments (18 distinct report waveforms) rejected per voice. The primary improvement criterion failed in each. The correct exact question was the best valid candidate on 59 and 60 of the 60 fresh positives respectively; hindsight offset ceilings under the false-alarm bounds were the same counts.

With expanded out-of-frame calibration (204–216 labeled judgments per training fold including the initial 72), both voices reached 59/60 exact fresh questions and 59/60 complete convention pairs, retaining 60/60 and 30/30 correct rejections. Different folds use different fitted thresholds; this is cross-validation over a known set of rules and frames at a new rate, not performance from a single small frozen calibration. Nineteen of 20 folds used bare/wrong-gap offsets .05/.02 for Samantha and .10/0 for Joelle. Post hoc matched-fold single-offset fits reached 59/60 and 56/60, with one Joelle wrong-convention false alarm. Allowing only a bare-report offset matched 59/60 on both voices with zero observed false alarms. The evidence favors learning how strongly to weight a competing ordinary report from more varied labeled examples. These results do not show how to select a minimal informative lesson set. Verified phrase disjointness per primary split and calibration fold, matched physical waveform across opposing conventions, differing recordings across rates, exact grammar membership and sampled acoustic scores against the registered kernel service. ### Buying at most four teacher judgments to distinguish questions from reports

Use the archived 225-wpm candidate margins and freeze two cross-fit deployments per voice (shuffle the prior 20 held frame IDs, seed 170801; halves of ten). Each lesson pool uses the other ten frames and original practice candidates, with all shared word strings excluded. No free labels from either source. Compare eleven bare-report offsets from the previous grid, wrong-gap offset fixed to zero. Given only unique-best status and the two acoustic margins on shuffled candidates, choose the example splitting the current minimum-past-mistakes offsets closest to half; ask if its intended speech act is a question, refit by fewest binary errors with smallest absolute offset on ties. Stop at unanimity on the pool or four answers. This supplied disagreement strategy learns a scalar receiver preference. Controls: skip, first disagreement, 256 same-count random samples using identical fit, and all available binary labels. Freeze policies before synthesizing a 233-wpm test rendition, reuse the held meanings/placement rules and charge .25 correctness units per queried label. Score accuracy on intended questions, wrong-convention judgments of identical audio and previously separated bare controls. Primary per voice: ≥2 more exact accepted requests over skip across 60 questions without additional false alarms, plus net advantage over same-count random after fees; report denominators and per-fold actual bills. The same two policies can be replayed on the opened 213-wpm recordings as a separate diagnostic. Enumeration/preview work is measured separately, not included in the label fee. No claim for independent languages.

On fresh 233-wpm recordings Samantha improved 56→60/60 exact accepted requests and Joelle 51→56/60. Both rejected 60/60 wrong-convention presentations and 30/30 bare reports before and after. Per-fold available labels 135/141; purchased 3/3 for Samantha and 3/4 for Joelle, versus 276 per voice across folds for exhaustive teaching, which selected exactly the same offsets. Gross minus .25 per label over the two folds (150 outputs per voice), split / first disagreement / matched random / skip / all: Samantha 148.50 / 144 / 144.63 / 146 / 81; Joelle 144.25 / 144 / 139.54 / 141 / 77. First disagreement matched gross performance only for Joelle. Both voices met the preregistered point-estimate criteria. Stratified paired bootstrap over ten frames in each frozen fold, conditional on those fits, 95% intervals for additional exact questions: Samantha [0,9], Joelle [1,10]. These are reused frames on two synthetic voices. At the opened 213-wpm rate the same fits gained three/two correct requests over skip, with 90/90 rejections each.

The first two replies in both Samantha fits left the deployed threshold unchanged; the third made the difference. On Joelle the remaining four errors consisted of two requests losing to ordinary reports alone, one losing to both an ordinary report and another marker placement, and one accepted with the wrong words. Complementing every unqueried lesson label preserved the complete selection trace and offset in all four fits and both disagreement arms. The algorithm previews cached acoustic margins on every eligible candidate; median measured pool conversion and selection 0.12 ms per deployment on this Mac, excluding acquisition of those margins and all synthesis. Label savings are conditional on that preview access and the supplied offset family and query rule.
