Thus boundary ambiguity and over-splitting both matter; speaker-specific peak/gap tuning alone did not close the gap. Practice sequences reuse the same 20 source clips and synthetic joins; no natural conversation claim. Local reproduction: human_gap.py fit, human_gap.py test and human_gap_check.py via mac.sh in dual-role-word, with calibration scores, policies, passage identities, inferred words, and paired pause readings saved externally.

### Using familiar pronunciations to choose between candidate word boundaries

Freeze FSDD clips 5/6 as the speaker-specific enrollment. Form 25 new passages per speaker, five each of lengths 2–6, shuffle all 100 clips at indices 40–49 without replacement (seed 74111), joins 0/80/240 ms. These clips were previously scored in isolated-word experiments; these passages are new. Propose internal boundaries at centers of at least 20 ms of low energy at three relative thresholds .02/.07/.12. Include recording endpoints. Search over these identical candidates for a duration-only or a duration-plus-pronunciation path. Each edge has at least 50 ms of waveform and at most 1.8 seconds; use squared log ratio of active envelope duration to median enrolled duration, plus (joint arm only) nearest enrolled template squared distance. Weight costs by elapsed edge duration. No count, transcript or join input. Compare the archived pooled pause rule (.12/60 ms), and exact joins on the same audio. Before testing, require ≥10 percentage points in complete sequences over both matched duration and pooled pause control, positive speaker-paired 95% bootstrap lower bounds versus each, and fewer total word edits. Verify selected words with registered speech-match. Separate sequence recognition from word meanings.

Whole sequences across the 150 new passages (600 words): joint search 104 exact / 65 word edits, duration-only 77 / 115, pooled pause rule 56 / 146, exact joins 116 / 49. Paired speaker bootstrap (50,000 draws): joint minus duration +18.0 percentage points [6.7,29.3], joint minus pooled +32.0 [15.3,48.0]. All prespecified aggregate criteria passed. Joint versus duration exact of 25 by speaker: George 24/16, Jackson 24/20, Lucas 18/17, Nicolas 4/4, Theo 22/12, Yweweler 12/8. Nicolas's exact-join reader itself achieved only 5/25. The six speakers share passage recipes; uncertain transfer to naturally connected speech, novel speakers and larger lexicons. Two labeled isolated exemplars per word per speaker; no transcript/count used for search.

Implemented speech-match-unframed as an optional registered operation in the independent acoustic expert package (94ee169), taking an enrolled bank, waveform and public search limits/candidate cuts. Returns a sequence or abstention on infeasible or equally good conflicting readings. Package tests 14 passed. A fresh-process replay with the registered operation exactly reproduced all 150 joint outputs. Scripts human_wordpath.py SPEAKER and human_wordpath_check.py via mac.sh in dual-role-word, saved raw per-passage proposals and recognition paths. No core runtime or default chooser modification.

### Applying the English digit boundary search to whole Hebrew recordings

Keep the segment cost, thresholds, duration limits and operation from the preceding study fixed. Use equal-width enrollment from the same eight ordered Hebrew training sequences, and median active envelope length from their 64 inferred words. Run duration-only and joint search on the original complete waveforms with recording endpoints; compare archived equal-enrollment free-gap baseline and pause-compressed duration-only result on identical 40 opened recordings. Count missing paths as all deletions. Diagnostic on the same recordings: trim external silence to the previously used activity-range boundaries before search, keeping all internal samples. Require at least four additional exact sequences over matched duration-only and superiority to the archived 33/40 best exact count. All results here are regression evidence.

Whole Hebrew recordings: untrimmed duration-only and joint both got 0/40 exact with 113 word edits each despite a complete path in every case. Trimming just the external silence raised duration-only to 28/40 (17 edits) and joint to 31/40 (13 edits); archived equal-enrollment gap was 27/40 (22), pause-compressed duration-only 33/40 (38). Neither prespecified exact criterion passed. This interaction points to elapsed-sample edge costs being sensitive to leading/trailing silence. Registered operation reproduced every independently searched joint output. Reproduce with hebrew_wordpath.py via mac.sh.

### Following a spoken room and power command in an unheard combination

Use the pinned Fluent Speech Commands mirror at f8cdd495d08d4d1e101af714a18475c8d67ddeb0, upstream data by Fluent.ai. Choose first eight sorted speaker IDs with at least two recordings of each bedroom/kitchen × on/off form (“ROOM lights STATE”). Sort recordings by path and use first two per cell. Within each speaker rotate the reserved combination, expose only the other three combinations' full waveforms and paired room/state outcomes. No transcript or timings in model fit/test. The task supplies the two output fields and the family of two possible edge assignments. Learn which end carries each field by leave-one-recording-out classification across the six teaching utterances, using first/last 40% of active spectral frames, fixed direct assignment on ties. Compare whole-utterance per-field nearest template, inverted edges, and position-blind guessing. Test on both new utterances of each reserved cell, 64 trials (eight correlated trials per speaker). Require ≥75% jointly correct, ≥20 points over whole-utterance, and ≥6/8 speakers achieving at least 6/8. No general syntax or cross-speaker claim. Fit labels model a verified device state; no physical device is connected.

Primary scored before redesign. The whole-utterance nearest-example control necessarily returns a taught pair because both fields see identical distances; a missing-cell-only guess can solve a panel consisting exclusively of held cells. Freeze a balanced confirmation on the next eight eligible sorted speaker IDs. Use the first recording of each of three taught cells, withhold the fourth entirely; same leave-one-out edge selector on these three teaching recordings. Test the second recording of ALL four cells with that unchanged listener (four rotations per speaker). Report unseen accuracy and taught-combination accuracy separately and four-command panels all correct; fixed missing-cell guess gets one of four. Require ≥75% unseen, ≥75% taught, and ≥50% complete panels. No further calibration.

Primary: edge-conditioned factorization 50/64 (78.1%) jointly correct, room 57/64 and power 56/64. Individual scores 5/7/8/2/7/7/7/7 of eight; six reached 6/8. Both inverted-edge and whole-utterance arms 0/64 joint. All fits chose initial room/final power; one of 32 fits tied. Uniform random reference 25% expected, fixed absent-pair guess 100% by design on this primary. Balanced confirmation: 20/32 (62.5%) held combinations, 74/96 (77.1%) taught combinations on new audio, 15/32 (46.9%) complete panels. Whole-utterance 0/32, 63/96, zero panels; inverted edges 0/32, 35/96, zero panels. Uniform or missing-cell-only gets 25% averaged over balanced alternatives. Unseen room 25/32 and power 26/32; taught room 79/96 and power 90/96. Fixing the true edge assignment changes no output, localizing remaining errors to acoustic readout given this edge model. Only taught-combination confirmation criterion passed. Identical second recordings reused across four rotation fits per speaker (32 distinct confirmation test WAVs). Verified speaker separation, missing training pair and disjoint train/test file paths. This uses real complete human utterances, externally supplied field labels and two positional hypotheses; no unconstrained grammar, actual actuator or unseen-voice comprehension claim. Reproduce fluent_subset.py, fluent_room.py and fluent_room_balanced.py via mac.sh; raw audio, metadata, per-decision predictions and references stored locally outside Git. Source: Fluent.ai, Fluent Speech Commands, and Lugosch et al. (Interspeech 2019).

### Locating the sound that distinguishes two grounded commands

Before fitting: contrast two independently evaluated English constructions, “ROOM lights STATE” and “Lights STATE in the ROOM”. For each use six eligible speakers outside the sixteen from earlier studies (first six sorted with two audios per cell), disclosing overlap between construction cohorts. For each whole command calculate sliding spectral windows, lengths 20% and 40%, on a 10% position grid. For each field compute log ratio of acoustic distance for different field/same other field to same field/different other field on three training commands; choose the window with maximal contrast. Use equal-weight mean of local contrasts and a source prior estimated on first recordings of four cells from up to eight eligible earlier speakers per construction, normalized to equal weights by speaker. Source examples supply paired meanings and which construction is being used. Compare local only, source only and the earlier learned two-edge 40% control. Each target listener receives three recordings and hears all four commands on new audio. Require ≥75% unseen and ≥50% complete panels on EACH construction, and at least two additional complete panels over the 40% control per 24 with a positive speaker-bootstrap lower bound for a gain claim. A withheld participant and utterance do not imply a withheld lexicon. Report positions and source vs local banks; no transcription, time labels or evaluator outcome in selection.

Room-first results, each arm unseen/24, familiar/72, all-four panels/24: fixed 13/43/4, local contrast 14/41/2, source contrast 16/47/4, combined 15/44/4. Power-first: fixed 4/26/0, local 7/35/1, source 9/40/2, combined 7/39/2. Eight source speakers and 32 source recordings per construction; three target examples per fitted listener. Six target speakers in each cohort, two shared between constructions. The source-selected room and power regions were, respectively, 0–20% and 80–100% for room-first; 70–90% and 30–50% for power-first (fractions of active spectral frames). The source prior located the power contrast inside the second construction. Identical candidate windows were allowed for both fields; no enforced separation. Combined-minus-fixed gain in complete panels, six-speaker paired bootstrap 95% intervals: [0,0] and [0,16.7] percentage points. No primary gain or mastery threshold passed.

Evaluator-only diagnostic: choose the most accurate window independently per field after seeing all four actual outcomes in each panel, requiring one common pair of windows for the whole panel. The attainable number of complete panels was 7/24 and 4/24. On room-first, some available window solved the room throughout 20/24 panels but power in only 9/24; power-first, 18/24 and 7/24 respectively. This bounds what a better selector over this exact window menu and three-example template rule could accomplish, not what audio can reveal. Fixed-position window choice itself cannot attain the preregistered 12 panels in either construction. Each listener saw one construction throughout; construction ID routed the source prior. Test recordings repeat across the four held-cell rotations. Verified the training/test path separation, class balance and attainability by enumeration. Local reproduction with fluent_window.py room/state and fluent_window_diag.py via mac.sh. Registered envelope/fit/match operations, external selector and scoring. No deployed reader changed.

### Following a short sound across differently timed human commands

Freeze original source-selected fractional windows separately for each construction. Test all eligible FSC official test-split speakers (six per construction), with the analogous validation-split speakers (five) reported independently, no fitting or selection there. Keep three within-speaker examples and the same four balanced test commands per omitted-cell rotation. Compare time-normalized linear matching versus whole-utterance dynamic time warping: squared, teaching-frame variance scaled spectral distances, monotone diagonal/horizontal/vertical moves, endpoints fixed. For both, score only frames in the teaching word's source-selected region, average within each teaching frame then across those frames; choose the nearest recording for each meaning independently. Same teaching scale, labels, region and exemplars in both arms. Retain previous registered fixed-region reader as separate comparator. Require at least 18/24 exact unheard, 12/24 complete panels and at least two additional panels versus BOTH linear and archived fixed-region on each test construction for a general benefit claim. For the intervention, exchange just the power labels among the two values with acoustics fixed and verify recovered power flips while room is retained. No word boundaries, transcript, or true duration in the reader. Test split speakers receive personal teaching; split names describe source files, not a zero-shot speaker test.

Test room-first, exact on unseen/24, familiar/72, all-four panels/24: linear 19/60/19, warped 19/63/19, previous fixed 19/61/19. Test power-first: linear 17/58/10, warped 18/63/17, previous fixed 17/60/11. Warping met both absolute thresholds for both constructions; superiority criterion passed only for power-first. Seven extra power-first panels over linear came from two speakers (+3 and +4); other four tied. Validation room-first (denominators 20/60/20) linear 10/36/5, warped 12/44/8, fixed 11/39/5; power-first linear 11/45/9, warped 15/51/13, fixed 12/46/8. Same six test and same five validation speakers across constructions; split cohorts disjoint. Independent readback checked all label decisions against stored distances, every balanced panel and the held-cell/training exclusion. All power-label intervention checks passed, a label-channel verification only.

Post-primary timing intervention: double the duration of each spectral frame in the first half or in the second half of each original test recording while preserving frame values/order. Require a correct answer to the original AND both edited hearings, 96 decisions per construction. Room-first linear 64/96 vs warped 80/96, power-first 32/96 vs 78/96. Original-only counts 79 vs 82 and 75 vs 81. Reuses 24 distinct test waveforms per construction across held-cell fits; feature-space intervention, not human re-recordings. Only source regions came from the archived eight speakers; warping is a supplied timing algorithm, not a learned grammar. Local registered spectral features and fixed-region comparator, external warping and decision readout. Reproduce fluent_warp.py valid/test room/state and fluent_warp_stress.py via mac.sh. Per-template costs and decisions saved outside the repository. No production reader changed.

### Recognizing a construction when the speaker mixes two ways of giving a command

Freeze a mixed session using the saved official validation and test recordings. Hold the same room/power combination out of BOTH forms. Six teaching clips give two renditions per observed outcome; supply the prior that there are two constructions and one example per outcome per construction. Group six recordings without form labels by minimizing within-group symmetric whole-utterance warped distance. Name/order groups by which assignment maximizes their three-example acoustic contrasts at the two previously source-learned pairs of regions. At test pick the group of the single nearest whole recording. Inputs are spectral frames and training outcome pairs; neither transcript, filename nor test outcomes may influence grouping. Infer field labels by the earlier per-group warped reader. Compare supplied grouping alone, supplied grouping and routing, and a duration-only partition/router under the same assignment method. Randomize training order and check permutation stability. Require ≥90% correct live form assignments and ≥75% correct joint meanings on jointly withheld cells in both validation and test, and within five percentage points of named grouping/routing on all test commands. Use identical recordings to isolate removing the cue; previously opened panels. Include a new, uniformly time-normalized input control if raw duration alone succeeds.

An initial check stopped before scoring when duration-only partition ties depended on presentation order. Equal group costs (rounded to twelve decimals) now break ties by a canonical partition signature of the public spectral contents. Verified the same named partitions under reversed input order for every live fit. On validation acoustic grouping correctly recovered both named teaching groups in 20/20 fits, routed 155/160 commands, answered both fields in 120/160, and 26/40 withheld combinations. Test: 24/24 fits, 188/192 routed correctly, 159/192 meanings correct, 35/48 withheld (72.9%). Supplied groups without supplied live routing gave identical outcomes. Fully supplied routing gave 122/160 and 163/192 meanings, including 27/40 and 37/48 withheld. Duration grouping/routing gave 127/160 and 146/192 correct routes, 107/160 and 140/192 meanings, 23/40 and 33/48 withheld. Both routing criteria and within-five-points test criterion passed; withheld mastery missed on both splits, including validation with oracle routing. Grouping got six annotated-outcome but construction-unlabelled teaching utterances per installation, with a supplied balanced two-construction prior. These findings do not establish induction of the number of constructions.

Diagnostic: time-normalize every reference and query to 80 frames for grouping and routing; preserve the trained region assignment procedure. Route accuracy 156/160 validation and 188/192 test; using mean spectra of those same normalized frames gave 114/160 and 145/192. The ordered acoustic comparison retains discrimination at equal length. Meaning readout stayed outside this diagnostic. The six test speakers share 48 distinct query recordings across 24 correlated teaching forks; validation has five speakers and 40 distinct query recordings. Local external replay fluent_mixed.py valid/test and fluent_mixed_check.py via mac.sh. Per-decision routes, outcomes and controls saved locally; known-route outputs checked against every previously saved warped answer. No change to core or deployed reader.

### Transferring two competing room names across spoken constructions

Freeze a three-room balanced hearing test. For each direction teach three on commands, one per room, in one form and two kitchen commands (on/off) in the receiving form; no bedroom or washroom example in the receiving form. Test one new utterance for every receiving-form room × state. Include all eligible official validation/test speakers (5/5 validation, 7/6 test, for receiving room-first/state-first). Use only the word “washroom” for the new location. Compare a lexical bank of time-normalized spectral patches at the previous source-selected room position in each form, a fixed broad 40% edge, and a bank that translates donor patches by the difference between the two kitchen-on anchor patches. Primary method is unshifted source-selected regions with same-form kitchen examples included. Predict power from the two receiving-form kitchen examples by the previous warped reader. Explicitly supply construction identity to isolate word transfer. Test relabelling the two transferred room memories (power and query held fixed), and the advantage available to an evaluator given only the two disputed rooms. Require ≥75% on BOTH individual room and joint decisions on transferred-room trials separately by direction, ≥75% on kitchen room controls, and ≥50% complete four-command transferred-room panels on both validation and test. Every eligible speaker contributes a single panel per direction. No source-driven adjustment after seeing either split.

Separate follow-up after inspecting both opened cohorts: fit paired windows across the two command forms using only bedroom and kitchen, without any washroom audio, from eight earlier source speakers. For every region pair compare each on/off presentation with the two room candidates spoken in the other form; use number correct then summed relative nearest-room margins averaged across source speakers to select one pair. Freeze all 16×16 windows. Compare this alignment to the original within-form regions and broad edges on up to the first eight eligible train-split speakers in each direction outside prior_ids, all previously used window-study cohorts, and the cross-form source. Preregister independent ≥75% transferred-room accuracy and +10 points over broad edges in each direction. Report opened validation/test separately if replayed. Broad task now tests a third word outside the acoustic region-selection vocabulary.

Primary transferred-room / complete meaning: validation room-first 6/20 and 6/20, state-first 6/20 and 6/20; test room-first 9/28 and 7/28, state-first 7/24 and 7/24. Correct test kitchen rooms 14/14 and 12/12, power alone on transferred rooms 25/28 and 22/24. Zero complete primary panels throughout. Broad 40% windows improved joint test to 16/28 and 13/24 (zero and two complete panels). With only the two transferred rooms offered, broad room scores reached 26/28 and 21/24. Enforcing a shared shift from the kitchen-on correspondence gave zero transferred-room hits in every split/direction. The alternate memory-label check recomputed selections from saved distances and switched exactly the two associated names while retaining kitchen and the separate power readout; this verifies dependence on supplied teaching labels. Diagnostic with broad windows and only the three donor-form exemplars in competition: 23/28 and 18/24 transferred-room hits but only 5/14 and 11/12 correct kitchen controls on test, versus 17/28 and 13/24 transferred-room hits with all five exemplars. Same-form distractors affect identification. This diagnostic changes both template count and form coverage.

Cross-form fit on 64 source recordings from eight speakers chose room-first frames 0–20%, state-first 60–80% (formerly 70–90%). Fresh train-split speakers after exclusions: room-first eight speakers, learned-pair room/joint 17/32 and 17/32 vs broad 18/32 and 18/32; state-first three eligible speakers, learned 7/12 and 7/12 vs broad 8/12 and 7/12. Forced two-room trained comparison reached 26/32 and 10/12. All kitchen room controls correct with trained pair (16/16, 6/6); zero complete four-command panels. No superiority or mastery threshold passed. Cross-form training improved on the narrow within-form patches (6/32 and 4/12 room hits), but no gain relative to broad edges. Verify using fluent_carry.py valid/test 0/1, fluent_carry_fit.py, fluent_carry_fresh.py train 0/1 via mac.sh; raw outcomes, costs, source grid and clip identities in dual-role-word. Spectral envelopes registered locally; comparisons and interventions external. Speaker-level installations and conditional power scores do not imply unrestricted vocabulary learning. No deployed model change.

### Calibrating cross-construction evidence with a changed familiar room

Freeze before fitting. Compare the broad 40% region with a local search over word durations of 20%, 30%, 40%, or 50% of either recording anchored to the same outer edge. Take the minimum scaled squared envelope distance across width pairs. Separately calibrate both readers by multiplying same-construction distances by a source-chosen factor in {1,1.25,1.5,2,3,4,8}. Select factor on equally weighted familiar-room and transferred-room accuracy, ties nearest one. Source uses only bedroom/kitchen, first eight sorted train speakers with two recordings of all four meanings in both forms, alternating the familiar room, training on donor-on and familiar receiver-on/off and evaluating distinct second receiver recordings. Target uses five examples and all six balanced commands, repeating the prior kitchen-anchor task then a new washroom-anchor task with bedroom and kitchen never demonstrated in the receiving form. Evaluate validation and test separately, both directions; hold rendered state fixed across ablations and charge acoustic or classification errors. Primary is local-search plus calibration. Require ≥75% transferred rooms, ≥75% familiar rooms and ≥10 percentage points in overall room accuracy above uncalibrated broad patches per test direction with the changed anchor. Keep source selection blind to target measures. Raw source/target word timings unavailable.

Separate repair test after primary measurement. Simulate asking for the same instruction in the donor wording. Supply a real other-form utterance by that participant with the same intended room and state; for “on” use a distinct take from the donor-on teaching clip, for “off” use first available. This is evaluator-routed prerecorded clarification, not interactive human behavior. Exclude entire speaker/direction panels if any of the six replies are unavailable or overlap a teaching/query recording. Listen to the reply with the existing local-width donor-only bank, retain the original power readout for a room-only clarification. Charge .15 correct-room units per question across every live output. Fit a threshold on the two-room source from {never, .1,.25,.5,1,always} using (second-best minus best) / second-best cost across distinct room labels from the calibrated primary; maximize correct rooms less fees on complete source panels, ties fewer requests. Compare selective, never, always and within-panel spend-matched uniform expected reward. Require +.03 net per decision versus the strongest comparison for EACH changed-anchor test direction. Treat a zero-question threshold as a substantive result.

Source selected multipliers 2 for broad windows and 1.5 for local-width search (103 and 109 correct of 128 source trials vs 88 and 99 with multiplier 1). Test with kitchen familiar: primary transferred/familiar room correct 24/28 and 13/14 room-first, 19/24 and 11/12 state-first. With washroom familiar, primary 21/28 and 13/14 room-first vs uncalibrated broad 14/28 and 14/14; overall 34/42 vs 28/42, meeting the three room-first tests. State-first primary 8/20 and 10/10 vs broad 8/20 and 10/10, missing transfer and gain. Seven of ten bedroom recordings in this latter panel selected washroom. Changed-anchor validation 15/20 and 10/10 room-first; 3/12 and 5/6 state-first. These are room decisions, conditional on supplied construction. No timing annotations or isolation of the phonetic cause.

Rephrasing: source threshold .25, 119/128 room hits after 49 purchases (111.65 net). Changed-anchor test complete panels: room-first never/selective/always 31/36, 29/36, 28/36 hits, with 0/18/36 calls and net 31/26.3/22.6; spend-matched random expected 26.63. State-first 14/24, 20/24, 22/24 with 0/12/24 calls; net 14/18.2/18.4 vs random expected 16.53. Selected state-first replies corrected six and broke none; room-first corrected one and broke three. Validation room-first 19/24,17/24,13/24 with 0/14/24 calls; state-first 8/18,10/18,9/18 with 0/14/18 calls. Selective failed the strongest-control economic criterion. Verified SHA-256 separation of each changed-anchor reply from its query and five teaching recordings. Excluding incomplete panels changes denominators relative to the original study. No human was asked live, and the evaluator supplies the matching intended command in an alternate construction. Reproduce fluent_calibrate.py fit/valid/test and fluent_rephrase.py via mac.sh in dual-role-word. Frozen source grids, per-command predictions, costs and receipt identities saved externally. No production change.

### Deciding how strongly to revise a room judgment after a rephrasing

Freeze the prior margin-.25 purchases and fee .15. Reconstruct per-room acoustic distances from both hearings. On a paid trial minimize log first distance + weight × log second distance for each room. Select weight from 0,.25,.5,1,2,4,reply-only using correct paid source decisions in each direction, ties closest to equal weight (then smaller); fit only the archived two-room/eight-speaker source. Compare equal weighting, retaining first, blindly taking reply, and always asking with equal weighting. Report corrected vs broken first judgments, complete deployment return including skipped jobs, and a diagnostic paid pointer intervention that exchanges reply recordings across rooms at fixed speaker, power, wording, price and first evidence. This alternative is a deliberately conflicting utterance. Require fewer paid errors than both keep and replace combined across the two changed-washroom directions, net superiority ≥.03 per output to best fixed purchase strategy in each direction, report separately on opened validation/test. Independently run a new three-room cohort using first eight eligible train speakers per direction outside every earlier train-split study's source/target IDs, or all available if fewer. Do not change fit or acquisition after evaluating the cohorts.

The preregistered unused-speaker complete-panel cohort had zero eligible speakers in both directions. Separate, explicitly incomplete-coverage check: retain the same speaker exclusions, learned rules, five teaching clips and washroom anchor. Take every unused eligible train-split speaker with those five clips; score each available room/state probe only if its query is distinct from all teaching clips and its reply distinct from both. Use first legal recordings in sorted path order. Report per-direction exposure, which rooms/states are represented, conditional decisions and speaker counts. Missing individual cells preclude the balanced deployment or six-command mastery claim.

On source paid trials the room-first rule selected reply-only (22/22; equal fusion 20, keep 13); state-first selected equal fusion (21/27 vs 20 keep). On the three-room test paid room-first trials keep/take/equal/fitted got 14/12/14/12 of 18, and paid state-first 4/10/10/10 of 12. Fitted therefore tied blind replacement on the 30 paid trials, while fixed equal fusion achieved 24/30 vs fitted 22/30. Test room-first fitted corrected one first decision and broke three, state-first corrected six and broke none. Whole deployment room-first 29/36 minus 2.70 fees = 26.30 vs best fixed return 31; state-first 20/24 minus 1.80 = 18.20 vs 18.40. Validation fitted paid correct 7/14 and 9/14; complete net 14.9 vs best fixed 19, and 7.9 vs 8. Validation/test criteria failed. At fixed fee and timing, substituting a conflicting other-room recording changed fitted choices in 9/14 and 10/14 paid validation cases and 12/18 and 10/12 on test; this identifies receipt dependence, not correctness under conflicting speech. Verified both extreme fusion weights reproduce the stored first/reply choices.

Incomplete-panel independent check: 15 distinct new speakers overall. Room-first 40 available trials from 15 speakers, 22 purchases, first/take/equal/fitted 32/33/33/33 correct; fitted net 29.7 vs 32 for never. State-first 30 from 12 speakers, 20 purchases, first/take/equal/fitted 18/20/22/22; net 19 vs 18 for never. With state-first, 24 of 30 were off commands, only three queries named the familiar washroom; availability is informative and this is not a replication on balanced deployments. Verified per-speaker query uniqueness, distinct query/reply content hashes, and per-room minima against both original readings. Selective fusion gave modest independent state-first gains but no general superiority to fixed equal weighting. Local reproduction: fluent_integrate.py fit/valid/test/fresh and fluent_integrate_partial.py via mac.sh; score vectors, paid decisions and receipt identities archived externally. No core or deployed expert changes.

### Learning whether a paid spoken reply is clear enough to revise the first hearing

Freeze the prior purchases and fee. For every paid original, fork its identical WAV into clean and 0 dB additive white noise (RMS over entire clip); only the reply changes. Use deterministic noise from WAV digest. Also test held corruption type at the same SNR (low-pass noise, causal eight-sample average). Receiver observes only waveform-derived distances and dynamic range (90th / 10th percentile of 20-ms RMS in dB); no SNR flag or target label. Jointly select one dynamic-range threshold from quartiles of source observations and two reply weights in {0,.25,1,4,reply-only} by source paid-fork accuracy, ties prefer equal weights then proximity to one. Compare constant weight selected on identical source, fixed equal, keep and reply. Report paid correctness separately for both wording directions, clean-vs-degraded matched switches, denominators, and source-speaker vs official split. Require ≥2 additional correct pooled paid forks over the better constant/equal on both validation and test, no more than one clean loss versus equal per split, and a positive gain on the held noise type. This evaluates revision after buying; acquisition unchanged.

The source fit (49 paid calls × two receipts) chose to keep the original below 24.74 dB measured dynamic range and use the reply alone above it. The best source-constant reply weight was .25. On validation clean/white matched forks the learned rule got 32/56, fixed .25 got 34/56 and equal fusion got 32/56. On test the same counts were 40/60, 35/60 and 38/60. Clean test alone was 22/30 vs 24/30 equal, exceeding the allowed clean loss. Smoothed-noise test alone: learned 18/30, constant 16/30 and equal 14/30; validation 16/28, 14/28 and 15/28. The clean+white validation and clean-loss gates failed. Conditions reuse the same recorded reply and purchases; added noise is engineered, not natural recording variation. Reproduce fluent_quality.py fit/valid/test via mac.sh in dual-role-word. Saved settings, candidate decisions and per-utterance quality externally. No deployed policy change.

### Finding a spoken power distinction inside unfamiliar English commands

Teach each eligible FSC speaker with two “Lights on” and two “Lights off” recordings, supplying binary outcomes only. For every teaching waveform align its spectral frames against the other three utterances. Select the contiguous 35% of active frames with the largest mean different-outcome distance minus same-outcome distance. Spot the chosen fragments anywhere in each entire probe by sliding a time-normalized matcher at .65/1/1.5 of its teaching duration, every two frames including last start. Predict by closest example. Compare same matcher using full teaching utterance or fixed final 35%, and contrast fragments restricted to the final probe position. Same training scale across arms. Test four paired carriers: “turn [on/off] the lights”, “switch [on/off] the lights”, “turn the lights [on/off]”, “switch the lights [on/off]”. Include every eligible speaker, first sorted path per complete on/off pair, equal count within each observed pair. Separate validation and test; no tuning. Require both opposite commands right on ≥70% of eligible pairs and ≥10 percentage points above full-utterance spotting on each split, report internal vs final position separately. No test form, transcript, polarity or word position passed to classifier. This tests a binary distinction learned from contrasting utterances; a single explanation for the meaning of arbitrary words is not implied.

Before examining interventions, freeze a post-primary feature-level check on originally joint-correct opposite pairs. Exchange the winner's selected query interval between the two actual recordings from the same speaker and wording, interpolating donor frames to preserve receiver length. Exchange the farthest disjoint interval of identical length in each recording as a matched control. Exclude pairs lacking a disjoint interval. Rerun the complete free-position contrast search. Count a paired reversal only if BOTH instructions change to the donor output. This tests dependence of the classifier on local sound; true human word boundaries and perceptual effects of resynthesis remain unknown.

Validation (six speakers/24 opposite-command pairs): contrast 15/24 joint and 37/48 individual, tail 15/24 and 37/48, full spotting 10/24 and 34/48, end-only 13/24 and 33/48. Internal vs final contrast 4/12 vs 11/12 joint. Test (seven speakers/28 pairs): contrast 22/28 and 50/56, tail 20/28 and 47/56, whole 17/28 and 45/56, end-only 15/28 and 42/56. Internal vs final contrast 10/14 vs 12/14. Ten-point gain over whole on both splits; 70% absolute passed test but missed validation. Limited evidence that choosing the contrast beats always using the tail of these particular short teaching commands. Confirmed unique probe WAVs, disjoint enrollment and saved decisions from distances. Wording provided only to assemble matched evaluator pairs; enrolled word/command identity here means a supplied on/off action outcome. Frozen settings and all distances/regions in external fluent_spot.py valid/test.

Feature swap on eligible initially correct pairs: 13/15 validation and 17/20 test reversed BOTH judgments when exchanging the selected spans; no paired reversals from the equal-length disjoint control on either split. Individual flips 28/30 vs 0/30 and 37/40 vs 1/40. Two initially correct test pairs lacked a disjoint control span. Additional waveform intervention: map selected feature indices to PCM using speech-envelope active-frame offset and 160-sample hop, interpolate the opposite segment to the receiver's length and blend its edges over 5 ms. Fresh envelope extraction and full search gave paired reversals in 4/15 validation and 9/20 test vs zero for controls, individual flips 15/30 vs zero and 28/40 vs two. Stronger feature-splice results cannot substitute for physical waveform results. No human verified what the edited recordings sounded like; a raw splice can change pitch or cross a phoneme boundary. Local registered envelopes, external selection and search. Reproduce fluent_spot_swap.py and fluent_spot_pcm.py through mac.sh. No deployed reader changes.

### Contrasting word positions during teaching

Freeze before scoring. Match teaching cost at four utterances: repeated short form (two per power state, “lights STATE”) versus one short and one internal-position command (“turn STATE the lights”) per state. In each teaching clip enumerate local fragments of shared length 35% of the shortest teaching utterance at two-frame steps. For each training fragment compute the nearest fragment of the other same-outcome clip and nearest fragment of either opposite-outcome clip; select the one maximizing the normalized margin (negative minus positive distance divided by their sum). No teacher-supplied word position. Search the full probe as before. Compare recurrent fragment versus tail with identical width and varied teaching, and recurrence versus repeated-only teaching. Independently score four excluded wordings (“switch STATE the washroom lights”, “turn STATE the kitchen lights”, “switch the bedroom lights STATE”, “turn the washroom lights STATE”), both members of each pair correctly. Require ≥70% complete pairs plus ≥10 points over both controls on validation and test. Matched contrasts use only complete speaker/wording pairs; no new source fitting from either split. Inspect teaching-only recovery by evaluator and retain acoustic-distance traces. Speech recognition remains conditional on the supplied binary action labels.

After the primary, freeze a separate teaching-content comparison: change only the second teaching pair from “turn STATE the lights” to “turn STATE the lamp”, keeping “lights STATE”, four examples, the learning/search code and probe recordings fixed. Balance the three arms by requiring availability of all teaching phrases. Score an independent train-split cohort, first twelve eligible sorted speakers disjoint from official validation/test (not necessarily unseen in earlier unrelated studies); previously opened official splits are regressions. Require the lamp pair to gain ≥10 percentage points of complete pairs over BOTH varied-lights and repeat-only on the new cohort, and no loss over varied-lights on either opened split. Different command recordings can differ in pronunciation as well as noun, so this is an intervention on teaching examples rather than a phoneme-isolated causal attribution.

Primary complete pairs on validation: repeat fragment 13/23, repeat tail 16, varied fragment 11, varied tail 10. Test: repeat fragment 17/27, repeat tail 16, varied fragment 19, varied tail 7. Varied fragment passed 70% on test only and improved on repeat by two pairs (7.4 points), below the ten-point criterion. Its 19 test successes included nine internal-position pairs versus one for varied tail. The independent speaker cohort follow-up gave lamp 19/47 versus varied-lights 14 and repeat 20. Internal-position successes on precisely the same internal pairs were 11, 5 and 4, respectively, so performance on final-position commands fell as internal transfer improved. Lamp on opened validation reached 15/23 versus varied-lights 11 and repeat 13; on opened test 15/27 versus 19 and 17. Neither study demonstrated the specified reliable gain. Four labeled teaching recordings per arm per speaker. These are paired recordings from the same person saying each opposite command, not evidence for arbitrary grammatical position.

A concurrent duplicate fetch of a shared teaching file interrupted the first follow-up before scoring; deduplicated fetch paths and reran. Verified disjoint speaker splits, identical probe WAVs across arms, both labels present in every counted pair, four distinct teaching files disjoint from each query, and predictions recovered from saved winning distances/locations. Local reproduction: fluent_motif.py valid/test, fluent_contrast_noun.py train/valid/test, fluent_motif_check.py via mac.sh. Registered spectral envelopes with external fragment selection and sequence evaluation. No deployed reader or core changes.

### Retaining both spoken power readers and selecting by acoustic context

Freeze existing repeat and lamp motif banks (six distinct labeled recordings per target speaker in total). At test compute the two distance margins and an acoustic context vector: mean spectral frames in four equal temporal quarters, divided by coefficient scale from the six teaching clips. Source: 12 train speakers' saved probe outcomes. Predict which reader is more likely to be right by mean correctness difference among the 11 nearest normalized context vectors from source, with a tie falling back to the more confident reader (relative distance margin). No test phrase identity, word position, outcome, or same-speaker source neighbor is available. Compare both single readers, margin choice, and equal addition of signed log distance ratios; all have access to the same saved receipts. Report a per-command hindsight ceiling. Evaluate unchanged on original official validation/test recordings and independently on excluded carriers “switch STATE the lights in the bedroom”, “turn STATE the lights in the bedroom”, “turn the bedroom lights STATE”, “turn the kitchen lights STATE” (only actual paired forms). Prespecify ≥2 more complete opposite pairs than the strongest fixed single or fusion arm per cohort and no loss over repeat on final-position pairs. Track internal-position gains independently; mark unavailable pairs and correlated tests. Never fabricate a missing spoken alternative.

On the previously studied validation panels (23 pairs) repeat/lamp/equal fusion/context got 13/15/17/14 complete pairs; on original test (27), 17/15/17/17. On newly excluded wording pairs, validation (17) scored 8/8/7/7; test (21) scored 14/12/16/13. Fusion and larger absolute margin gave identical outputs, as expected for the sign of two summed binary log odds. Novel test internal/final: repeat 7/7 successes out of 14/7 pairs, lamp 9/3, fusion 10/6, context 9/4. Thus the context selector failed its superiority and retention criteria. Even simple fusion lost one previously covered final-position pair while gaining three internal pairs. On novel validation the same fusion gain did not replicate. The kitchen-final carrier had no complete pair on either split (only the on command exists in this inventory); results concern three novel phrasings. Novel probe WAVs differ from the original four evaluation carriers, using the same six validation/seven test participants and enrollment clips.

With evaluator access to the correct answer only when the readers disagree, recoverable complete pairs were 20/23 and 23/27 original validation/test, 11/17 and 17/21 novel validation/test. On novel test context selected the correct reader in 5/9 disagreements vs 8/9 for fusion (verify from saved individual decisions); headroom above fusion only one complete pair, so the prespecified two-pair superiority there is unattainable by selecting these two readers alone. Separate source arrays, ranking, score differences and all test decisions can be recomputed from fluent_retain.py valid/test old/new via mac.sh with prior archives. Registered spectral envelopes and external selector. No production change.

### Learning a local acoustic detector from whole human command outcomes

Freeze a speaker-independent binary detector before fitting. Select the first 24 sorted official train speakers with complete opposing commands in four source wordings (“lights STATE”, “turn STATE the lights”, “lamp STATE”, “switch STATE the lamp”), or all eligible if fewer. Use first path per speaker/wording/outcome. Identical-split acoustic controls: local temporal convolutions with max evidence per output, same network with mean pooling, and a framewise max detector without temporal context. Use the package's 13 spectral features, source feature scaling, two 32-channel ReLU convolutions (width 5, then width 5 dilation 3), two output scores. Mask padding at every hidden layer. Adam .002, 32 epochs, batches of 16, seed 85192, no target fitting or model selection. Freeze before opening evaluation. Tests use official validation/test speakers, no personal examples; balanced pairs for each available wording including former four room commands plus “switch STATE the lights in the kitchen”, “turn the lights STATE in the kitchen”, “turn STATE the washroom lights”. Require ≥75% complete pairs on EACH split and at least 5 points over strongest pooling/framewise control for a mechanism gain. Separate accuracy and paired correctness; report coverage, source cost, and physical feature/model size. Corpus text used only to arrange matched recordings and exclude entire test wordings from source. No transcript, timestamps, locations or true order passed to model. Not a zero-label vocabulary learner.

Before reading held scores, freeze a localization check for the local detector. On originally correct opposite pairs, choose a 17-feature-frame interval (the convolution's receptive field) independently per utterance by the largest drop in its predicted-class margin when that interval is replaced with the clip mean; stride four frames plus the final start. Compare exchanging the chosen PCM intervals between the two original recordings to exchanging their farthest disjoint equal-length intervals. Both have matched editing steps. No human labels guide interval selection. Report eligible denominators, individual flips and doubled reversals separately; a waveform intervention does not establish a human phonetic boundary.

192 source recordings (24 speakers, eight balanced commands each); three deterministic CPU fits took 2.70/2.38/1.64 s for local/mean/framewise, excluding download and feature preparation. The local expert has 7,330 parameters (29,320 float32 parameter bytes); the saved three-model checkpoint including scale and source file identities is 86,839 bytes. Registered envelope uses 13 coefficient values per frame (104 bytes as float64; converted to 52 bytes in float32 for training). On nine validation speakers: 13/54 complete pairs (61/108 commands) local, 16 (66) mean and 8 (55) framewise. Nine test speakers: 18/62 (77/124) local, 15 (76) mean and 14 (71) framewise. Both absolute criteria failed, as did the comparative gain on validation. No trained detector installed.

Frozen-model attribution: 192/192 exact on original fitting waveforms. Other takes of taught wordings from source speakers: 33/61 complete pairs (93/122 individual). New wordings with those same speakers: 27/155 (177/310). For official test speakers, taught wordings yielded 9/37 (44/74) and new wordings 18/62 (77/124). Eligibility differs among these strata, so they localize fragility without isolating effect sizes for individual nuisance variables. Opposite pairs correctly ranked by the on-minus-off logit on 33/54 validation, 42/62 test. Post-primary thresholds fitted on validation and frozen for test: common threshold 13/62, separate externally supplied carrier thresholds 18/62. Threshold recalibration alone did not improve test pair accuracy.

Waveform intervention on all originally correct pairs: validation 6/13 paired reversals (13/26 individual flips) moving selected intervals, 0/13 (1/26) for disjoint controls. Test 3/18 (16/36) and 0/18 (4/36). This shows localized acoustic dependence on a selected subset of successful decisions; it does not demonstrate broad word discovery. Fresh processes reloaded the frozen checkpoint and reproduced original logits within 1e-4 across batch shapes. Local scripts fluent_detector.py fit/valid/test, fluent_detector_check.py valid/test, fluent_detector_transfer.py and fluent_detector_threshold.py via mac.sh in dual-role-word. Corpus labels supplied binary outcomes, no test-speaker calibration, and no general open-vocabulary result. No production code change.

### Two demonstrations from a new speaker before hearing unfamiliar wordings

Freeze the preceding detector and its official validation/test cohorts. Give each eligible target speaker exactly the first “lights off” and “lights on” recording, disjoint from every probe. Compare unchanged outputs; a logit midpoint calibrated on these two examples (preserve original polarity); and spotting each example's most discriminating 35% interval after whole-pair acoustic time alignment against its opposite. Compare identical segment menus and spot scoring with original acoustic frames versus frozen learned second-layer features. Derive selected teaching boundaries separately in each representation. Include the simple final-35% acoustic matcher as a strong supplied-position control. Rotate demonstrations across speakers as a negative control for personal calibration. Primary requires at least 10 percentage points greater complete-pair accuracy for the personalized hidden-feature matcher than the unchanged model, raw contrast and wrong-speaker hidden matcher on BOTH splits, with no probe feedback. Also compare against the fixed-tail raw matcher before crediting the learned representation. Record individual predictions, retained intervals and relative costs. No new model fitting or choice based on the opened target outcomes.

Independent second fit: train a few-shot listener for the actual task of acquiring two labels from a speaker. Reuse precisely the 192 source examples. Every training episode compares one person's two short support commands against their six other utterances. Represent each local region by a unit-normalized convolutional embedding; compute support-to-query similarities at every pair of time indices, use a smooth maximum (temperature .1, normalized by pair count), then predict from the two resulting scores. Alternate which of the two short source wordings supplies the supports across epochs; include only other wordings as queries. Two 32-channel layers as before, seed 85192, Adam .002 for 32 epochs, speaker batches of four, cross entropy with similarity logits scaled by 15. Validation/test use exactly the same two “lights” supports and speakers as primary. Compare to the identical seeded random model, acoustic fixed tail, prior frozen hidden matcher, and cyclically rotated speaker supports. No target gradient updates. Require ≥10 points in complete-pair accuracy above random and wrong-speaker support plus superiority to acoustic fixed tail on each split. Run label-exchange equivariance and check that batched padding does not alter a probe answer. This learns a local comparison rule from weak utterance labels; numerical label meaning remains supplied.

With two personal demonstrations per speaker and no probe feedback, complete opposite pairs (validation /54, test /62): unchanged 13/18, midpoint 10/16, personal raw contrast 25/26, personal raw tail 25/31, personal frozen hidden contrast 14/17, hidden tail 14/21. Wrong-speaker raw contrast 7/9, wrong-speaker raw tail 7/10, wrong-speaker hidden contrast 17/5. Best simple personal tail individually correct 79/108 validation and 90/124 test, versus unchanged 61/108 and 77/124. Threshold calibration alone was insufficient. The frozen hidden contrast missed all joint superiority requirements. Raw contrast selected a region ending exactly on the last frame in 11/18 validation and 5/18 test teaching clips. Confirmed teaching/test content-hash disjointness and predictions from each saved pair of spot costs. Nine target speakers per split; speaker-mismatch uses one deterministic cyclic donor, not an average across interchangeable donors.

The few-shot fit used the same 192 source clips, 24 source speakers, 3.60 seconds CPU training on the Mac (feature preparation excluded). With the true two target examples it got 24/54 and 25/62 complete pairs, versus random initialization 9/54 and 7/62, or frozen learned model with other-speaker examples 12/54 and 23/62. The gain above random transferred to both splits; the personal-over-other 10-point margin passed validation only. Raw fixed-tail matching stayed stronger on both splits, by one and six pairs. Within each split the episodic listener scored 78/108 and 86/124 individual decisions. Verified support-order swap exchanges output scores and independent batch-size replay preserves the first query logits per speaker. These results separate learning to compare sounds from superiority over an acoustic template, and identify a speaker-specific transfer benefit for the simple baseline. External fluent_personal.py valid/test and fluent_episodes.py fit/valid/test via mac.sh; local fit, decisions and per-utterance costs saved. No production reader changes.

### Few-shot power learning with varied teaching position

Keep the source examples, optimizer, seed and 32-epoch training budget fixed. Cycle the support pair through all four source wordings instead of the two terminal “lights/lamp STATE” wordings; every query in each episode uses a different wording. Compare unweighted local similarity against contrast-weighted matching that gives higher support weight to frames whose best similarity in the opposite teaching recording is low (temperature .1, masked softmax). Freeze fits before testing. Each new speaker gives either “lights STATE” or “turn STATE the lights”, with identical previous seven probe carriers in matched forks. Include prior terminal-support model, raw trailing 35% and highest acoustic contrast controls, and exact interchange of class assignments. No positions or carrier IDs in the live classifier. Require ≥60% pairs correct under each teaching condition on each split, and ≥5 points over the strongest simple comparator for both teaching conditions to claim position-robust progress. Report how often the SAME pair is correct under BOTH lessons and an evaluator ceiling for choosing the better teaching pair. Different teaching forms also differ in length and vocabulary.

New-contrast test frozen after power study: without any fitting on increase/decrease recordings, reuse BOTH frozen readers and original acoustic controls. Replace teaching alternatives with “heat down/up” or “turn down/up the heat”. Test on real new recordings in six wordings: “volume STATE”, “turn STATE the volume”, “turn the volume STATE”, “turn the kitchen temperature STATE”, “turn STATE the washroom temperature”, and “turn the temperature STATE in the bathroom”. Supply direction only via each pair of teaching examples; count both words correct separately for each carrier and report volume separately from temperature. Include seeded random embedding. For evidence of reusable learning rather than random acoustic similarity require ≥10 points over random and ≥5 points over better acoustic control, and ≥60% joint accuracy per teaching position on each split. No target utterance scored before specifying this panel. “Up/down” is a genuinely different spoken distinction; a positive result would still be bounded matching across phrases, not semantic understanding of synonyms or unsupervised actuator discovery.

Position study, pairs correct with terminal/internal teaching, respectively: validation (47 matched pairs, seven speakers) previous learner 22/20, varied plain 27/23, contrast-weighted 16/9, acoustic contrast 25/25, acoustic tail 25/8. Test (55 pairs, eight speakers) previous 18/14, varied plain 20/18, contrast-weighted 24/19, acoustic contrast 24/15, tail 25/6. With weighting 13/55 test pairs were correct under both lessons; even the better teaching form per pair would reach only 30/55. Neither learned method passed both primary thresholds. New split denominators reflect requiring both teaching wordings from the same person. Checked equal probe identities, distinct teaching and test files and exact support-order symmetry to tolerance. Reproduce fluent_position.py fit/valid/test.

Frozen transfer to the new up/down distinction: validation (42 pairs, seven speakers), “heat STATE”/“turn STATE the heat” teaching: previous learner 22/12, varied plain 22/8, contrast 14/11, random 11/8, raw acoustic contrast 10/14, tail 13/9. Test (43 pairs, eight speakers): previous 12/11, varied plain 12/15, contrast 17/11, random 18/5, raw acoustic contrast 18/15, tail 18/1. On the 23 test volume pairs alone, varied plain got 6/9, contrast 9/7, raw contrast 9/9, acoustic tail 11/0. No reliable advantage over both random and acoustic baselines across the two splits/teaching positions. Multiple cases are sensitive to the wording of the lesson even when the query recording never changes. For raw contrast, 4/43 test pairs succeeded with both forms versus 29/43 with at least one; this is hindsight headroom rather than live lesson selection. New target outcomes supplied as two examples with explicit opposite labels; no “up/down” classifications from source training. Local fresh-process transfer via fluent_updown.py valid/test and fluent_position_audit.py. Checkpoints and per-query comparisons in dual-role-word. No deployed reader change.

### Requiring two grounds for the same heard direction

Freeze the two archived acoustic contrast banks per speaker (one heat-up/down pair, one turn-up/down-the-heat pair). Use the registered spectral envelopes and the same teacher-selected 35% fragments. Compare (i) each lesson alone, (ii) pooling by the closest single template, (iii) averaging each lesson's minimum word cost with independent locations, (iv) requiring both templates for a proposed class to match the same probe interval center before averaging costs. Search centers on every frame; use widths .65/1/1.5 times each template duration, including the original span widths; keep valid, wholly contained intervals only. Four-recording arms have identical training audio, costs and query spans. Tests: regressions on previous six carriers and separately five new phrasings (“turn it STATE”, “turn sound STATE”, “turn STATE the temperature in the bedroom”, “turn the heat STATE in the washroom”, “turn STATE the heat in the washroom”). Use the same enrolled speakers, both members per available pair, first recording per outcome, no test labels until scoring. Require agreement arm ≥70% complete pairs and ≥5 points above best single and independent fusion on both new cohorts. Intervene by exchanging both sound-to-action labels, and record when requiring agreement changes choices. No new model training. The localization assumption concerns the same heard word; the two teaching snippets can have different durations.

Separate offset study after initial results: estimate signed displacement of the two learned snippets for each class by spotting the short-form snippet in its same-label long teaching utterance and vice versa. In each clip compare the cross-match center with the known center of its own teaching snippet, convert difference to mean snippet-width units and average the two signed estimates. At test multiply by mean enrolled width and require the two probe matches to differ by that signed number of frames. Compare exact common center, reversed sign with equal magnitude, independent matching and single closest template. No query labels in offset estimation. Use four additional waveform-disjoint paired carriers: “kitchen heat STATE”, “turn the heat STATE in the kitchen”, “turn STATE the bathroom temperature”, “turn the temperature in the bedroom STATE”. Require a gain of ≥5 points in complete-pair accuracy over common AND wrong-sign on both splits, and report comparisons to unrestricted pooling. Examining a successful offset would identify a timing relationship only, not word boundaries.

Original panels re-scored on the dense grid, short/long/closest pool/independent/common-center complete pairs: validation 11/12/14/16/13 of 42, test 18/15/19/16/12 of 43. New five carrier panels: validation 7/13/16/11/9 of 34, test 18/15/21/15/12 of 40. Grid differences from older results include evaluating every integer center, not only alternate starts. On new test, common-center changed five previously correct independent outputs to wrong and three wrong to correct; validation six lost, four gained. Perfect choice between the two individual lessons after seeing each answer could recover 23/34 and 30/40 complete pairs on the new validation/test wording; no live selector has that information. Simple pooling on new test reached 60/80 individual commands but only 21/40 pairs. The strong agreement hypothesis failed on both splits.

Offset follow-up validation (28 pairs): zero/signed/reverse centers 9/12/11, unrestricted independent 14, closest pool 15. Test (32 pairs): 16/16/14, unrestricted independent 18, closest pool 19; the short lesson alone also gave 19/32. Signed offsets did not meet the across-split threshold. All three wording panels within each split were WAV-disjoint; the same seven validation and eight test speakers participated. Confirmed label-exchange equivariance of class costs, distinct teaching and query audio identities and saved per-command costs. Raw spans, costs and signed offsets archived externally. Local reproduction fluent_agree.py valid/test and fluent_offset.py valid/test through mac.sh. No production reader change.

### Learning which acoustic lesson to trust on a new wording

Freeze a generic two-reader gate. The four teaching examples and local match curves are unchanged. For each lesson expose the log distance margin between the two candidate meanings, closest log distance, its depth below the median cost at other possible times, relative time of its best match, and normalized temporal separation between the opposing matches. The gate receives differences between lessons on the four unsigned measures and absolute margin; a sigmoid mixes their two signed log odds. Fit weights by regularized binary log loss on the first 16 eligible sorted train-split speakers with both teaching pairs and four paired probe carriers (“volume STATE”, “turn STATE the volume”, “turn the bedroom heat STATE”, “turn STATE the bedroom heat”). One first recording per cell. Compare closest-template, independent average, each single, and a constant mixture fitted to the same source. New test/validation panels use all eligible speakers and distinct target carriers (“bathroom heat STATE”, “turn the heat STATE in the bathroom”, “turn STATE the heat in the kitchen”, “turn STATE the bedroom temperature” where both recordings exist). Include the opened five-carrier panel separately. Require +5 percentage points complete pairs above the strongest single, closest-template, independent or constant arm on each fresh cohort and positive paired speaker bootstrap lower bound. Check label-exchange symmetry. Hindsight is diagnostic only.

Separate open-vocabulary boundary: ask whether an utterance contains the spoken form up/down, or neither. “Increase the volume” lacks both word forms even though its requested direction is increase. Keep closest-template direction predictions frozen. Using the 16 source speakers, fit a separate presence/rejection logistic reader on four positive source carrier pairs and four negative wordings (“change language”, “lights on”, “increase the heating”, “decrease the heating”). Balance positive and negative total loss, L2 .05 on standardized coefficients. Inputs: log closest pooled cost, absolute log odds, and absolute difference between lessons' signed odds. Compare with a one-feature distance-only logistic boundary, always spotting and always abstaining. Test fresh directional pairs from the prior stage with four new lexical negatives (“switch on the lights”, “switch off the lights”, “increase the volume”, “decrease the volume”) from the same participants. Report explicit direction correctly accepted and named, abstention on power/volume separately, and matched speakers only with explicit pairs and all four negative clips. Require ≥70% in each group on BOTH splits before claiming lexical presence detection. Exclude all transcripts and semantic outcome labels at prediction; this is a word-form detection test only.

Reliability source: 128 labelled probe outcomes from 16 training speakers after their two teaching pairs. On fresh validation wording, short/long/closest pool/independent average/constant/gate completed 9/6/11/10/11/11 of 21 pairs (seven speakers); test 12/9/16/11/12/14 of 23 (eight speakers). Gate minus pool speaker-bootstrap 95% intervals [−14.3,+14.3] and [−20.8,0.0] percentage points (30,000 resamples). The offered bedroom-temperature wording had no opposite pair. Opened validation/test gate vs closest pool 12 vs 16 of 34 and 20 vs 21 of 40. Strong control prevailed; mechanism superiority gate failed. Five unsigned cue differences plus regularized intercept, source-only parameters; exact input-class interchange checked.

Lexical presence results: fitted on 128 explicit + 64 negative recordings from 16 source speakers, carrying over four personal exemplars each. Validation and test each had seven eligible speakers. Validation multifeature reader correctly accepted AND named 28/42 explicit directions, rejected 6/14 power commands and 11/14 volume paraphrases. Test corresponding counts 26/40, 5/14, 5/14. Distance-only gave 28/42, 6/14, 11/14 on validation and 26/40, 4/14, 5/14 on test. Always return closest direction on these exact panels: 31/42 and 33/40 explicit correct, zero negative rejections. Always abstain: zero explicit correct, all negatives rejected. With the learned gate, 39/42 and 32/40 true explicit utterances were accepted regardless of polarity; presence-vs-absence score AUC .800/.663. No three-group criterion passed. Rejecting an increase/decrease paraphrase here is correct solely for the literal up/down detection task; it gives no score for understanding the intended action. Verified unique evaluation files and reconstructed frozen predictions from saved coefficients. Local reproduction via fluent_trust.py fit/valid/test and fluent_absence.py fit/valid/test with mac.sh in dual-role-word. No production reader changed.

### Separating the expressed word form from the requested change

Freeze a balanced natural-audio four-way panel: an explicit down/up command and a decrease/increase paraphrase about the same object. Supply two supervised outputs: whether “up” or “down” occurred (absent/down/up), and the direction of the requested change. Source: first 24 sorted eligible train speakers, one recording per eight phrases from “heat STATE”, “ACTION the heating”, “turn STATE the heat in the washroom”, and “ACTION the heating in the washroom”. Feed the model registered acoustic frames only. No pretrained recognizer. Train local two-layer 32-channel convolutions with concatenated mean/max pooling and independent form/intent heads; also a four-way joint-class control with the same trunk, steps, seed and data. Adam .002 for 40 epochs, batches of 16, seed 85212. Test new wordings on separate official validation/test speakers for volume, bedroom temperature and kitchen temperature with both directions in both forms. Use only complete panels. Compare frozen zero-target and exactly four target examples (the first two short source frames × two directions from that speaker), ten SGD steps at .02, the full net reset per speaker. Reuse target support equally for controls. No test transcript or frame boundaries in the listener. Record both correct on each item and all four per panel, including ability to preserve direction across the paraphrase and vary literal output. Require ≥75% joint items and ≥50% complete quartets on EACH split for mastery; require +10 points in complete quartets above joint-class control to credit separate heads. These are explicit phrase-pair labels for two constructions; no synonym meaning discovery without teaching claim.

Source 192 whole human recordings from 24 speakers. Validation seven different speakers, 21 complete quartets (84 utterances); test another seven speakers, 20 quartets (80 utterances). Validation correct literal/intent/both/quartets: joint classifier before teaching 57/44/40/0, after 58/47/41/3; separate heads before 52/45/39/0, after 62/48/44/2. Test joint before 62/49/47/2 and after 66/55/52/3; separate heads before 58/43/39/0 and after 60/41/38/0. Both absolute mastery and separate-head superiority failed. Joint classifier after personal teaching correctly varied its literal answer while preserving the correct direction on both recordings of 13/42 same-intent pairs on validation and 19/40 on test. Separate heads 15/42 and 13/40. On test each identified the broader literal-vs-paraphrase family in 74/80 cases. Among 40 explicit test commands the joint classifier named the direction correctly 30 times; among 40 paraphrases 25 times. Form family recognition alone therefore does not guarantee correct action direction. Outputs on the simulated device were scored from corpus intent labels, no real device connected.

Diagnostic from the same four personal examples and no source model: whole-utterance DTW nearest example got both outputs right on 38/84 validation and 35/80 test. Routing by that nearest example then spotting the within-family acoustic contrast gave 39/84 and 37/80. Supplying true family to this template baseline gave 54/84 and 53/80 but just 0/21 and 3/20 complete quartets. True-family figures are evaluator aids. Confirmed unique test file identities, complete balanced panels and disjoint supports; separate per-family and matched-pair counts from persisted predictions. Local checkpoint, raw labels, dual outputs and reference decisions in external fluent_form_intent.py fit/valid/test and fluent_form_audit.py valid/test through mac.sh. No production reader changed.

### Describing excessive loudness versus requesting a change

Freeze before fitting. Train on human “too loud”, “too quiet”, “louder”, “quieter”, one recording per cell from the first 32 eligible official training speakers. Evaluate on complete fresh-speaker panels with “make it louder/quieter” and either “far too loud/quiet” or “that's too loud/quiet”. Train the same acoustic convolution and step budget as the preceding experiment. Compare direct action supervision with supervision of both the adjective family and complaint/request distinction, followed by a supplied binary rule reversing action for complaints. No transcript, boundaries or target teaching at inference. Score ≥80% actions and ≥60% entire four-recording panels on each split; compare under identical audio, report component errors and the supplied rule explicitly. Evaluate repeated request recordings as shared within speaker. Even perfect performance would establish transfer to new carriers for these expressions only.

Trained locally on 128 distinct recordings from 32 people. Direct action learner: validation 39/56 panel positions, 3/14 complete; test 45/64, 2/16. Factorized, supplied reversal: 29/56, 0/14; 48/64, 5/16. Each panel shares its two request clips with the other panel of that speaker. Deduplicated recordings: validation 42 from seven people, test 48 from eight. The factorized listener identified the adjective on 13/14 direct requests and 25/28 complaints on validation; its construction decision was correct just 6/14 and 22/28 respectively. On test it interpreted the action correctly on 9/16 distinct direct requests and 30/32 complaints; direct action supervision gave 10/16 and 25/32. Neither reader met both thresholds on either cohort. The supplied reversal can work when it hears both components. The weak validation result on “make it” identifies a specific failure in recognizing an unfamiliar request frame. Corpus action labels and auxiliary family/construction labels were supplied during training; action was scored on the corpus, without physical playback-device actuation. External fluent_complaints.py train/valid/test via mac.sh saves checkpoint and individual decisions. No target fitting or production reader change.

### Learning a request construction from one outcome

Freeze the previous component reader. Demonstrate one of “make it louder/quieter” with the corpus volume-change label; infer its construction using its own adjective guess and the supplied reversal rule. Transfer this construction to a new recording if the normalized acoustic distance between the initial halves is sufficiently small, otherwise keep the old prediction. Compare whole-utterance and final-half matching, no update, and unconditional transfer. Fit one distance threshold per matcher on the first 12 eligible original training speakers, maximizing mean action accuracy weighted equally between opposite-adjective requests and complaints. Both teaching directions; the lesson file is excluded from its own probes. Evaluate both former panels as known regressions and separately on up to 20 eligible original training speakers absent from the base model and calibration. Test a flipped teaching outcome as a simulated receipt intervention. Require ≥15 percentage points gain on opposite requests and ≤3 points complaint loss on each cohort for a transferable improvement. This tests a supplied prefix-comparison mechanism and labelled changes; no claim of discovering phrase boundaries.

Calibrated on 12 base-model speakers (120 lesson/probe decisions); external script fluent_prefix_lesson.py and per-decision JSON via mac.sh. In 20 additional speakers excluded from both fits, no-update → prefix lesson: opposite requests 21/40 → 18/40, complaints 116/160 → 116/160. Comparison request/complaint counts: whole 19/40 and 116/160, suffix 24/40 and 98/160, unconditional 26/40 and 42/160. On the known validation speakers prefix changed 5/14 → 8/14 and 38/56 → 34/56; test 9/16 → 8/16 and 60/64 → 60/64. Complaint counts reuse the same recordings across the two separate lesson forks. With the teaching outcome flipped, prefix request correct 20/40, 2/14 and 8/16 on additional, validation and test speakers respectively. The real outcome alone produced the right interpretation of the lesson in 33/40, 13/14 and 11/16 forks; the prefix matcher allowed transfer to only 10/40, 6/14 and 4/16 opposite-adjective questions. Both missed matches and attribution of an outcome to the wrong construction matter. No cohort passed the combined transfer and retention criteria. Source thresholds used only the 12 calibration speakers; the additional speakers share corpus scripts and are fresh only for these fitted readers. Production reader unchanged.

### Equal-budget phrasing diversity for indirect change requests

Before fitting: in each of four balanced meaning cells select the first 20 training speakers having two ordinary recordings and one alternative wording. Both arms hear the first ordinary recording; arm R hears the second, arm V hears “make the music louder/softer” or “far too loud/quiet”. Ordinary recordings are “louder/quieter” or “too loud/quiet”. Match speaker identity within every cell, labels, steps and number of distinct recordings. V changes both carriers and one adjective, so its effect concerns this package of examples. Test the previously opened complete “make it louder/quieter” and “that's too loud/quiet” panel on separate official validation/test speakers. No target lessons. Primary comparison: the same factorized reader and supplied reversal, ≥15-point benefit on complete panels in each cohort with ≥80% correct actions; compare also a direct action reader. Measure construction and adjective separately. For the small paired comparison report counts of individual speakers gaining/losing a complete panel. Fixed seed and optimization from the prior study. These are known benchmark regressions.

Each arm heard 160 distinct human recordings. Across seven validation speakers the component reader with repetitions → varied wordings improved from 15/28 to 22/28 actions and 0/7 to 3/7 complete panels (three gained, zero lost). Across eight test speakers: 23/32 → 24/32 and 1/8 → 3/8 (three gained, one lost). Construction recognition rose 19/28 → 27/28 and 21/32 → 30/32. Adjective recognition changed 22/28 → 23/28 and 28/32 → 26/32. The direct reader gained one action per cohort with equal complete-panel totals (1/7, 0/8). The component reader cleared the panel-improvement margin in both but missed the 80% action gate in both. Small paired cohorts and known target forms limit generalization. Nine-nearest training utterance durations alone gave 16/28 and 20/32 actions under varied training. Follow-up diagnostic replacing the first half of every registered feature sequence with the source mean left 22/28 and 26/32 construction judgments correct; replacing the last half left 18/28 and 24/32. Neither pattern identifies a localized carrier representation; English comparative endings also distinguish the constructions in this inventory. Scripts fluent_diversity.py train/valid/test, fluent_diversity_duration.py and fluent_diversity_mask.py in the local archive; checkpoint contains exact source filenames, raw JSON contains target identities and individual outputs. No production change.

### Does request recognition need the order of acoustic frames?

Known-panel diagnostic, frozen before collecting diagnostic outputs. Reverse the four contiguous quarters as blocks, preserving all frames and their order within each block; separately permute all frames with a filename-seeded permutation. Compare unchanged judgments, construction correctness and action correctness against the intact recording. Train an explicitly frame-order-invariant two-layer pointwise network with mean/max pooling on the same varied corpus and component labels, data order and optimizer budget; give the pointwise model 80 hidden units to avoid a small-capacity control. Verify its logits on every permutation. Compare with the frozen temporal convolution. If the order-invariant control is within two construction judgments on each cohort, the existing panel alone gives little leverage for claiming a learned sequence; if only the frozen reader degrades on scrambled input this is perturbation sensitivity.
