Frame extraction itself retains within-window waveform information. No target selection or new natural language stimuli.

Order audit results: reversing quarter blocks preserved 27/28 and 31/32 construction choices. Shuffling individual frames preserved 21/28 and 27/32; construction accuracy fell to 20/28 and 25/32. The invariant reader got 24/28 and 27/32 constructions (three fewer on each, missing the two-judgment margin), and 21/28 and 25/32 actions. Its logits passed both permutation checks. Frame order contributes to the trained model, but intact large-scale order was rarely necessary on these panels. Local fluent_order_audit.py train/valid/test.

### Crossed spoken operators over an identical recorded request

Source audit: the corpus supplies whole human “louder/quieter” but lacks the proposed “I want it / already too” minimal pair. Use a quoted request in a constructed two-part message: synthetic “follow this instruction” or “reverse this instruction”, with identical human adjective audio across forks. This tests two operators, not ordinary complaints. Source: first 24 eligible train speakers, first recording per word, two synthetic voices (Samantha, Daniel), 175/205 wpm. Validation/test all eligible official speakers (8/9), separate prefix donors Karen/Tessa, 181/199 wpm, all cells in both orders. No target fitting. Equal silence and joins; counterbalance each source token across operators, orders and both motor polarities. Decoder sees only whole-message registered acoustic frames. Train joint semantic-part labels and direct direction classifier separately; diagnostic separate cue/word boundary access. Supplied algebra combines word, operator and unknown binary device wiring. On separate forks give one labeled motor demonstration on the opposite adjective (both operators counterbalanced, opposite rate and presentation order); infer wiring from its predicted requested change and receipt. Changing only the receipt corresponds to a differently wired device. Compare retained, suppressed (canonical default), changed, cue-only, word-only and exact controls. Public adjust/report mode and source-fitted response table; report intended direction independent of motor wiring. Measure wrong movements in report mode and correct reports separately. Starting position always midpoint; independent one-step simulator. Joint endpoint: both operator choices must yield correct opposite movements for identical target adjective after the SAME demo in each wiring. Unit of uncertainty is human speaker; minimum six per cohort; report speaker-bootstrap 95% intervals, no inference to new prefix-voice population. Criteria ≥80% joint, ≥20 points action accuracy advantage over balanced uninformative teaching receipts, ≤5% report-mode movements on each cohort. Track direct action accuracy as well as the joint test. Register and freeze before fitting. Prior human regressions unchanged. Do not infer natural-utterance or cross-device linguistic transfer.

Local crossed results: 24 source human donors (48 distinct word clips); eight validation and nine test donors (16/18 clips), with four synthetic operator recordings per evaluation cohort. Whole-message component reader heard the human property in 118/128 and 124/144 messages; operator 64/128 and 80/144. Both opposite movements correct after the same demonstration in 4/256 and 8/288 matched forks (speaker-bootstrap 95% intervals 0–4.7%, 0–6.9%). Correct individual movements 256/512 and 256/576 (50%, 44.4%; intervals 50–50%, 33.3–50%). With a fair mixture of true and opposite receipts the score is exactly 50% by complementary wiring, giving teaching effects 0 and −5.6 points; suppressed also 50%. Report-only false moves 0/512 and 0/576 with explicitly supplied binary mode and source-taught response type; reported direction correct 248/512 and 304/576. Direct action classifier complete pairs 0/256 and 36/288. Exact reader 100% on both movements and reporting; cue-only and word-only zero complete pairs. Separate boundary-aware acoustic readers achieved 224/256 and 0/288 complete pairs: isolated cue correct 128/128 and 72/144. Each target cohort has only one synthetic operator voice; donor/prosody generalization remains especially limited.

Evaluator-only hybrid replacing just predicted operator identity with its correct value in both lesson and query raised complete pairs to 212/256 and 200/288. Giving only the true property instead gave 0/256 and 16/288; supplying just the correct interpretation of the demonstration gave 0/256 and 32/288. This localizes much of the failure to hearing the operator again at use. Independent volume-state reconstruction reproduced all movement/report counts. The response-type table is supervised, algebra and possible wirings are supplied; no credit for acquiring a pragmatic norm or discovering the combination rule. No intact human full-message corpus for this contrast. Training order was fixed by seed and both block orders tested. Reproduce locally with mac.sh fluent_crossed.py train, valid, test then mac.sh fluent_crossed_verify.py. Raw decisions, checkpoint, synthetic segments, paired.html and paired.wav under dual-role-word/crossed-audio or its parent. Replay shows first test donor, first fork; on “quieter” both predicted final levels are 4 instead of 4 and 6. No production change.

### Finding an enrolled operator at an unknown position

Freeze a subsequence template comparison before evaluation. Two labelled isolated spoken operators at 167 wpm per installation, versus the same two recordings from another voice (Samantha); source-trained spectral scale, slide each reference across complete test features at .8, 1 and 1.2 times reference duration. Acoustic mean squared error, no semantic or boundary input during matching. Keep the frozen whole-message adjective reader and one motor demonstration. Audit old Karen/Tessa panels, then four new synthetic voices (Moira, Rishi, Fred, Kathy) on the same nine test human adjective donors at 181 and 199 wpm, both orders. Complete every case. Require ≥90% operator recognition in each fresh synthetic voice, and ≥80% complete movement pairs per voice to claim functional mastery. To credit personal enrollment, require ≥10 points operator gain over other-voice templates in at least three fresh voices. Report literal operator decisions and correctly reported meaning separately from calibrated movement; test swapped enrollment labels, which can cancel in motor calibration. No new natural speakers or real device.

Pre-score coverage correction: the waveform identity assertion stopped the first invocation before any results. Karen rendered both 167-wpm operator exemplars byte-identically to the 181-wpm probes. Replace 167 with 135 wpm for every enrollment and donor control, keep all target conditions and matching windows frozen, and retain the identity assertion.

Enrolled operator identification on four new synthetic voices (Moira, Rishi, Fred, Kathy): 144, 144, 140, 144 correct of 144 messages each; other-voice templates 71, 70, 73, 71. Both order positions and rates included. Frozen prior reader 91, 72, 87, 72. All four met the acoustic and relative-enrollment criteria. The 144 messages per voice cross only four distinct operator probe waveforms with 18 prior human adjective recordings and placement. Exact digital repetition across enrollment, control and probe source files excluded by waveform hashes (38 distinct). Shuffling the frames of each teaching template before matching reduced correct operator decisions to 100, 90, 72, 72 respectively. Known Karen/Tessa regression: 128/128 and 126/144 with enrollment.

Complete movement pairs under enrollment 77, 113, 91, 45 of 144 in the fresh voices, all below 80%; another-voice enrollment gave 14, 7, 12, 20. Supplying the true property to both lesson and test raised pairs to 144, 144, 132, 144. Swapping enrollment labels leaves calibrated motor scores identical, while correct direction reports fall from 117, 131, 123, 105 to 27, 13, 21, 39 of 144. Paired motor calibration can cancel a systematic semantic reversal; the independent report matters. Counts collapse the two symmetric wirings. This tests synthetic phrase token recognition from two labelled examples at one other rate, not unsupervised phrase discovery. External fluent_operator_enroll.py and fluent_operator_shuffled.py take voice names through mac.sh and persist individual distances and judgments. No production reader change.

### Using the location of one heard phrase to read the remainder

Before scoring: keep the previous operator templates and the previous independently trained isolated-word reader frozen. Locate the minimum-distance operator match with the existing three durations. Feed the longer of the two contiguous residual sides to the isolated-word reader, with no true join/order; when empty, use a single mean frame. This supplies a two-part message assumption. Compare same reader on whole message, shorter residual side, and true isolated adjective; compare the earlier whole-message component reader. Score four previously tested operator voices on nine original test human donors, plus an independent human donor panel (first 16 eligible training speakers absent from crossed model training, same four synthetic voices). Evaluate movement pairs under the previous one-demonstration wiring protocol and literal adjective accuracy. Require ≥80% complete pairs and ≥90% correct adjectives for each voice in both panels for mastery, plus ≥10-point improvement over the original component reader in joint movements on each fresh-panel voice. No target tuning. Count unique human recordings explicitly; acknowledge synthetic repetition and shared source dataset.

The matcher selected the side containing the adjective on every message in both panels (576/576, 1024/1024; same two-part splice structure throughout). In voice order Moira/Rishi/Fred/Kathy, fresh-human residual adjective accuracy 216/212/219/218 of 256, versus the same word reader on whole audio 164/167/171/176. Both movements correct 174/166/156/180 of 256, versus prior whole-message component reader 122/194/123/65. Human-speaker bootstrap paired changes +.203 [−.023,.422], −.109 [−.336,.105], +.129 [−.066,.320], +.449 [.234,.652]. Known test: residual adjective 116/144 per voice, movements 88/88/76/88 versus prior 77/113/91/45. True isolated clips with the same word reader gave 14/18 and 28/32 distinct adjective recordings correct on known and additional panels respectively; repetition across placements does not increase this independent count. All-voice behavioral, acoustic and relative-gain gates failed. Cropping recovered a useful signal for a reader trained on isolated words; Rishi retained a stronger whole-message route. Location of the remaining side is observable here, and true isolation still leaves speaker-specific word errors. Fresh human donors are disjoint from the 24 crossed-model training speakers and nine original test donors; four operator voices and word forms reused. Local external fluent_residual.py VOICE test/train and fluent_residual_verify.py via mac.sh. Independent reconstruction checked same adjective/different lesson, donor separation and reproduction of every frozen known-panel operator decision. No production change.

### Personal acoustic examples for the remaining property

Freeze before fitting. Use one separately recorded “quieter” and “louder” from the listener's current human speaker (second takes) with explicit labels. Score first takes for every eligible official validation/test speaker. Require distinct waveform hashes across teaching and probes. Freeze word network, use its pre-output embedding to compare cosine distances to the two examples. Compare ordinary reader, nearest personal example, nearest other speaker (next sorted donor cyclically), and logistic combination of ordinary logit difference and signed exemplar similarity trained on eligible original crossed-training speakers only. Fit from isolated first takes, L2 logistic regression, standardized predictors, C=1. Primary distinct-recording test: both words correct per human, count gains/losses, plus ≥90% words and no more than one formerly correct word lost in each cohort. Secondary real use: embed these same probes with four synthetic operator voices at both rates/positions; residual extraction unchanged, require 80% successful paired movements per voice. Do not use repeated placements as independent human speakers. No adaptation of the acoustic encoder.

Eleven eligible source speakers (22 first-take labels) fitted the combined rule. Five validation and seven test speakers had two distinct recordings of both words. Validation frozen/nearest/combined each 10/10 unique probes, both words for 5/5. Test frozen 10/14 and 3/7 complete, nearest and combined 11/14 and 4/7, one correction and zero losses each. Other-speaker nearest test 10/14 with one gain and one loss. The 90% test requirement failed. Validation combined complete movement pairs 80/80, 80/80, 77/80, 80/80; test 64/112, 57/112, 51/112, 57/112 across Moira/Rishi/Fred/Kathy. Test nearest-only 77/112, 74/112, 74/112, 80/112. No voice passed the test movement threshold. Importantly nearest personal matching improved paired movements from 218/448 to 305/448 but lowered correct uncalibrated direction reports from 334/448 to 317/448. In 61 of its 305 successes both the lesson and both probes were read backwards; the errors canceled in device calibration. Frozen and combined showed no such successes on these placements. Combined gave 341/448 reports and 229/448 complete movements. Counts from seven humans with repeated voice/position trials. Support disjointness, identities and independent calibration/error counts checked from raw JSON. Local reproduction mac.sh fluent_personal_words.py train/valid/test. No production change.

### Selecting an audible consequence before seeing a device control

Freeze: teach two single-word human recordings with before/after sounds from a simulated source. Use a supplied RMS-ratio comparison for louder/quieter; the learner is supplied the demonstration and candidate time boundaries. Test by pointing to one of two audible changes, before revealing any button. Both options share the same final RMS; one began above, the other below. Shuffle display order. Cross the same speech with conventional English and an explicitly artificial code in which the two utterances request reversed changes. Both receipts change together; this identifies a two-way convention under a supplied binary hypothesis space. No claim that English itself reverses. Hold out human speaker and waveform from model fitting. Test new bare takes and intact “make it louder/quieter”, which are absent from personal teaching. Compare nearest demonstrated acoustic embedding, frozen canonical reader and a reader that calibrates the canonical direction from the two outcomes weighted by its own source logit margins. Include the previous fixed combination with this inferred polarity. All arms get identical information and no test feedback. Primary: ≥80% of speakers with all four recordings correct separately on each split in each convention, and ≥20-point point-accuracy gain over ignored demonstrations on balanced conventions. Score both conventions separately; a valid binary reversal alone cannot establish acoustic comprehension. Count human speakers as units; additional donor panel from unused crossed-training-split speakers; diagnose failures by bare vs longer requests. Conventional recordings are human; outcomes and counterconvention simulated.

In each convention, combined reader: five validation speakers, 10/10 new bare takes, 6/10 intact longer requests, 1/5 all four; seven test speakers, 11/14 bare, 8/14 longer, 1/7 all four. Twenty additional original-training-split donors excluded from encoder training: 35/40 bare, 24/40 longer, 3/20 all four. Balanced point gain over ignoring the two outcomes was +.300, +.179 and +.238 respectively, percentile speaker-bootstrap intervals [.250,.400], [.071,.321], [.162,.300]. Literal accuracy threshold failed on every cohort and the test gain missed +.20. Nearest demonstration alone on additional donors did better (37/40, 26/40, 5/20); test matched combined. Calibrated canonical reader on test got 10/14, 8/14 and 0/7. Flipping just the two changes flipped all grounded answers and no ignored-demo answers. Both conventions yield identical accuracy by construction for these symmetric updating rules, not two independent replications. This is pre-button outcome matching, not production of a verbal explanation. Listener uses supplied RMS comparison and supplied two-convention algebra; nothing is learned about loudness perception itself. Matched fork integrity, disjoint people, waveforms and target-teaching separation checked. External reproduction fluent_outcome.py train/valid/test, fluent_outcome_audit.py, via mac.sh. The illustrative WAV reuses the test “make it louder” request and recreates the two candidate directions with new tones; correct is second and learned choice first. No production change.
